Shifted twisted Yangians and finite W W -algebras of classical type
Kang Lu, Yung-Ning Peng, Lukas Tappeiner, Lewis Topley, Weiqiang Wang

Abstract
. We introduce parabolic presentations of twisted Yangians of types AI and AII, interpolating between the R-matrix presentation and the Drinfeld presentation. Then we formulate and provide parabolic presentations for the shifted twisted Yangians. We define quotient algebras known as truncated shifted twisted Yangians and equip them with baby comultiplications, generalizing the work of Brundan and Kleshchev. PBW bases for all (truncated) shifted twisted Yangians of type AI and AII are established along the way. Applying the theory of universal equivariant quantizations of conic symplectic singularities we show that the truncated twisted shifted Yangian is isomorphic to the finite W W -algebra which quantizes a suitable Slodowy slice. This provides a presentation of the finite W W -algebra associated with every even nilpotent element in type B and C , as well as every nilpotent element with two Jordan blocks in type D . Finally we make a conjecture which would supply presentations in the remaining even cases in type D .



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 17B37.

1.
Introduction

1.1.
History

Drinfeld-Jimbo quantum groups of affine type and their degenerate cousin, Yangians, are one of the most ubiquitous themes in contemporary representation theory and mathematical physics. The importance of these algebras is partly due to the existence of several (Serre type, Drinfeld type, or R-matrix type) presentations. For example, for
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
or for
\mathfrak{g}
a complex simple Lie algebra, the Yangian
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{g})
admits an R-matrix presentation as well as a Drinfeld presentation deforming the current algebra
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}[z])

[
Dr87
]
. Yangian symmetries appear in various contexts in mathematical physics, most notably the study of the 2-dimensional scattering amplitudes, and quantum integrable spin chain models.

Twisted Yangians, associated with symmetric pairs of Satake type AI and AII, were introduced by Olshanski
[
Ol92
,
MNO96
]
as coideal subalgebras of the Yangian
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
, and they are intimately related to classical Lie algebras; Molev’s book
[
Mol07
]
is a standard reference in the subject. A general twisted Yangian associated to (classical) symmetric pairs
(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\theta})
has been introduced in
[
Ma02
]
and then in R-matrix form in
[
GR16
]
, deforming the twisted current algebras
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}[z]^{\theta})
, where the involution
\theta
is extended to
\mathfrak{g}[z]
by
xz^{r}\mapsto(-1)^{r}\theta(x)z^{r}
. In recent years, the
\imath
quantum groups arising from quantum symmetric pairs introduced by G. Letzter and generalized by S. Kolb have emerged as a vast generalization of Drinfeld-Jimbo quantum groups. Many fundamental results on quantum groups have been generalized to the
\imath
quantum group setting; especially, the (split) affine
\imath
quantum groups admit Drinfeld presentations
[
LW21
,
Z22
]
. Through degeneration, this has led to a Drinfeld presentation of split twisted Yangians
[
LWZ25
]
, and for twisted Yangians of type AI, the same Drinfeld presentation was also obtained via Gauss decomposition in
[
LWZ23
]
. Thus, it is natural to view twisted Yangians as a close cousin to affine
\imath
quantum groups and label them in terms of Satake diagrams as well.

Finite
W
-algebras, on the other hand, come from a rather different lineage. They are constructed by quantum Hamiltonian reduction from nilpotent elements in the Lie algebras of reductive groups
[
Ko78
,
Ly79
,
BT93
]
. More precisely, if
e\in\mathfrak{g}
is such an element then the Slodowy slice
\mathcal{S}
to the adjoint orbit of
e
is equipped with a Poisson structure via Hamiltonian reduction, and the finite
W
-algebra
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)
is a filtered algebra with associated graded Poisson algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}]
. For the regular nilpotent elements, Kostant showed that the finite
W
-algebra is isomorphic to the center of the enveloping algebra, whilst for
e=0
we have
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)=\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g})
.

The finite
W
-algebras associated to even nilpotent elements were first studied by Lynch, who noticed that the
W
-algebra could be naturally embedded inside the enveloping algebra of the parabolic subalgebra associated to the nilpotent element
[
Ly79
]
. Finally, Premet introduced finite
W
-algebras in full generality in
[
Pr02
]
into mathematics (cf.
[
BT93
]
), whilst studying the modular representations of Lie algebras of reductive groups. In the same year, Gan–Ginzburg reinterpreted his work in the language of Hamiltonian reductions
[
GG02
]
. The subsequent work of Premet, Losev, Ginzburg and others over the following decade highlights their numerous applications to the classification of primitive ideals in enveloping algebras, as well as the classification of quantizations of nilpotent orbits (see
[
Gi09
,
Lo10
,
Lo22
,
Pr11
,
To23
]
and the references therein). More recent applications include the classification of small modular representations of Lie algebras, as well as the positive energy representations of affine
W
-algebras
[
GT19
,
AM21
,
AvE23
]
.

Despite the numerous uses of finite
W
-algebras, explicit presentations have not been forthcoming. The first substantial progress was the work of Ragoucy and Sorba
[
RS99
]
, who showed that the finite
W
-algebra associated to a rectangular nilpotent element with
N
Jordan blocks of size
\ell
in the general linear Lie algebra, is isomorphic to the level
\ell
truncation of the Yangian
\mathrm{Y}_{N}:=\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
. This work was generalized by Brundan and Kleshchev
[
BK06
]
, who introduced the shifted Yangians
\mathrm{Y}_{N}(\sigma)
and showed that every finite
W
-algebra for
\mathfrak{gl}_{M}
associated to an element with
N
Jordan blocks, arises as a truncation of some
\mathrm{Y}_{N}(\sigma)
. The main technical tools introduced in their proof were parabolic presentations
[
BK05
]
and baby comultiplication. We refer to
[
Pe16
,
Pe21
]
for generalizations of these constructions to finite
W
-superalgebras of type A.

Ragoucy discovered that the level
\ell
truncation of the semiclassical twisted Yangian of type AI and AII is isomorphic to a semiclassical finite
W
-algebra for a rectangular nilpotent element in a classical Lie algebra
[
Ra01
]
. The quantum version of this result was proven by Brown
[
Br09
]
using different methods. In
[
TT24
]
two of the authors extended Ragoucy’s semiclassical result to many other even nilpotent elements in type
BCD
, combining their results on Dirac reduction of the semiclassical limit of
\mathrm{Y}_{N}(\sigma)
with the new Drinfeld presentation for twisted Yangians of type AI introduced in
[
LWZ23
]
.

There has been a long-standing hope that more general finite
W
-algebras in types
BCD
will appear as quotients of the twisted Yangians, which was rekindled by the recent construction of Drinfeld presentations of twisted Yangians of type AI
[
LWZ23
]
. Interesting partial results have been obtained by De Sole–Kac–Valeri
[
DSKV18
]
and by Brown
[
Br09
,
Br16
]
.

In this paper, we make comprehensive progress on this problem by proving isomorphisms between truncated shifted twisted Yangians in general and even finite
W
-algebras in types
B
and
C
, as well as those associated to two-row partitions in type
D
. Modulo a conjecture about the center of certain truncated shifted twisted Yangians, the same result holds for all even nilpotent elements in type
D
.

1.2.
Parabolic presentations

Recall
\mathrm{Y}_{N}=\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
. The twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}_{N}
of type AI is associated with the symmetric pair
(\mathfrak{gl}_{N},\mathfrak{so}_{N})
whilst the twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}_{N}
of type AII is associated with the symmetric pair
(\mathfrak{gl}_{N},\mathfrak{sp}_{N})
. More specifically,
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is generated by the coefficients of the
S
-matrix
S(u)=(s_{ij}(u))
in
\operatorname{Mat}_{N}({\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}[[u^{-1}]])
, where
s_{ij}(u)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}s_{ij}^{(r)}u^{-r}
, subject to the
quaternary
and
symmetry
relations (
2.12
)–(
2.13
). We refer to Molev’s book
[
Mol07
]
and §
2.2
for more detail.

For each (strict) composition
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},...,\mu_{n})
of
N
, applying a
\mu\times\mu
-block version of Gauss decomposition to
S(u)
in the spirit of
[
LWZ23
]
(also see
[
BK05
]
) leads to a parabolic presentation of the twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
below; a parabolic presentation for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
requires all
\mu_{i}
to be even.

Theorem A

(Parabolic presentations, Theorem
4.1
, Theorem
4.4
)
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},...,\mu_{n})
be a strict composition of
N
, with additional assumption that all
\mu_{i}
are even in type AII. Then
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is generated by the elements

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant a\leqslant n,\ 1\leqslant i,j,\leqslant\mu_{a},\ r\geqslant 0\}\ \cup\\
&&\hskip 40.0pt\{B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant a<n,\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a+1},\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\ r>0\},\end{array}

subject to either the relations (
4.1
)–(
4.9
) or the relations (
4.1
)–(
4.7
) and (
4.11
).

The formulation and the proof of this theorem are significantly more involved than for
\mathrm{Y}_{N}
in
[
BK05
]
. Already the current Serre relations which involve lower terms for the Drinfeld presentation of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N}
(which is the special case for
\mu=(1^{N})
here) are highly nontrivial
[
LWZ23
,
LWZ25
]
, and the Serre relations for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
in Theorem
A
even in this case are formulated quite differently. A complete proof of this theorem requires a careful study of the cases
n=2,3
, which we carry out in Sections
5
and
6
.

Let us comment on the evenness on the composition
\mu
for type AII. The symmetric pair
(\mathfrak{gl}_{N},\mathfrak{gl}_{N}^{\theta})
(with
N=2r
even) of type AII is not quasi-split, and the corresponding Satake diagram contains a subdiagram
I_{\bullet}
of black nodes. The subalgebra corresponding to
I_{\bullet}
is a direct sum of
r
copies of
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
. Associated to the composition
\mu=(2^{r})
, the minimal parabolic presentation for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
in Theorem
A
contains
r
copies of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{2}^{-}
in R-matrix form. It remains unclear how to construct a Drinfeld presentation for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
corresponding to
\mu=(1^{N})
already when
N=2
(and one can question its existence). Theorem
A
in the generality with
\mu
even for type AII turns out to be all we need for our application to finite
W
-algebras.

When we consider the algebra
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
with its parabolic presentation associated with
\mu
we often use the notation
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}
, although we note that this algebra does not depend on
\mu
.

1.3.
Shifted twisted Yangians

Now fix
0<n\leqslant N
. Following
[
BK06
]
, a
shift matrix of size
N
is a matrix
\sigma=(\mathfrak{s}_{i,j})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}
of nonnegative integers (shifts) such that

(1.4)

\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}+\mathfrak{s}_{j,k}=\mathfrak{s}_{i,k}

whenever
|i-k|=|i-j|+|j-k|
. In this paper, all shift matrices are symmetric
\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}=\mathfrak{s}_{j,i}
for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
. We note that
\mathfrak{s}_{i,i}=0
for
i=1,...,N
, and that a symmetric shift matrix is determined by the integers
\mathfrak{s}_{1,2},\mathfrak{s}_{2,3},...,\mathfrak{s}_{N-1,N}
, thanks to (
1.4
).

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},...,\mu_{n})
be a strict composition of length
n
. We say that
\mu
is an
admissible shape for the shift matrix
\sigma
if

\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}=0
for all
\sum_{l=1}^{a-1}\mu_{l}<i,j\leqslant\sum_{l=1}^{a}\mu_{l}
for all
a=1,...,n
(by convention
\sum_{l=1}^{0}\mu_{l}=0
).

Let
\sigma
be an
N\times N
symmetric shift matrix and let
\mu
be an admissible shape for
\sigma
. When we consider a twisted Yangian of type AII, we always assume that all parts of
\mu
are even. We define the
shifted twisted Yangian

{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)
to be the algebra generated by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant a\leqslant n,\ 1\leqslant i,j,\leqslant\mu_{a},\ r\geqslant 0\}\ \cup\\
&&\hskip 40.0pt\{B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant a<n,\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a+1},\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\ r>\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}\}\end{array}

subject to relations (
4.1
)–(
4.9
) in every type, along with: additional relation (
8.3
) for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
and additional relation (
8.20
) for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}
, respectively. These relations should be imposed for all admissible indexes. The definition of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)
is a little subtle (in contrast to
[
BK06
]
) as it includes the relations (
8.3
) and (
8.20
) (which are derived relations in the twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
by Lemma
7.3
).

Recall that the twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is associated with a symmetric pair
(\mathfrak{gl}_{N},\mathfrak{g}^{\theta})
where
\mathfrak{g}^{\theta}=\mathfrak{so}_{N}
(for
+
) or
\mathfrak{sp}_{N}
(for
-
). The involution we choose is described in (
2.18
), and we extend the involution
\theta
to
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]
by the twist
z\mapsto-z
. The
twisted current algebra

\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}
is spanned by symmetrized matrix units
f_{i,j}z^{r}:=\frac{1}{2}(e_{i,j}z^{r}+(-1)^{r}\theta(e_{i,j})z^{r})
. This allows us to define a
shifted twisted current algebra
, the subalgebra
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma)
spanned by

\{f_{i,j}z^{r}\mid r\geqslant\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}\}.

We recall that
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is equipped with the
loop filtration
such that
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\cong\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta})
on the level of associated graded algebras.

By definition, there is a natural homomorphism

\displaystyle{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{\mu}.

Our next main theorem is a Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt (PBW) for the shifted twisted Yangians.

Theorem B

(PBW basis for shifted twisted Yangians, Theorem
8.2
, Theorem
8.11
)
.

The natural homomorphism
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\rightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{\mu}
is injective, deforming the inclusion map
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma))\subseteq\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta})
under the identification
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}=\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta})
.

The PBW bases for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)
in Theorem
8.2
and Theorem
8.11
are expressed in terms of parabolic (Drinfeld-type) generators. For
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}
(i.e.,
\sigma=0
), a PWB basis in terms of
s_{ij}^{(r)}
was known; cf.
[
Mol07
]
.

For each admissible shape
\mu
for
\sigma
we view the shifted twisted Yangian as a subalgebra,
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{\mu}(\sigma)\subseteq{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
. In Proposition
8.3
and Corollary
8.12
we show that this subalgebra does not depend on the choice of admissible shape. For the rest of the introduction, we will use the notation
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)
for the shifted twisted Yangian.

1.4.
Truncation, baby comultiplication and the center

Now we fix an integer
\ell>2\mathfrak{s}_{1,N}
, which we call the
level
. For each level, we define a truncation
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma):={\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)/I_{\ell}
where
I_{\ell}
is the two-sided ideal generated by elements in (
11.6
) in type AI, and (
11.31
) in type AII; note that the generators in the ideal can be more sophisticated than those used in defining truncated shifted Yangians in
[
BK06
]
. A priori, this truncation depends on the admissible shape
\mu
, and its parabolic presentation. However, we show that these truncations are canonically isomorphic (see Corollaries
11.6
and
11.14
), which justifies the notation.

With our chosen conventions,
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\subseteq\mathrm{Y}_{N}
is a right coideal subalgebra, and so we have comultiplication

\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\otimes\mathrm{Y}_{N}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})

where the second map is the evaluation homomorphism. This structure does not descend to the shifted subalgebras or the truncations, however some residual structure is left behind after shifting and truncating. This residual structure is called the
baby comultiplication
, and it generalizes that for
\mathrm{Y}_{N}
, first appearing in the seminal work of Brundan and Kleshchev
[
BK06
]
.

Assume
\sigma\neq 0
and choose
\mu=(\mu_{1},...,\mu_{n})
to be minimal admissible to
\sigma
; here “minimal” means that
\mu
has minimal length. Define
\dot{\sigma}:=(\dot{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}
according to the following rule

(1.8)

\dot{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j}:=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}-1,&\text{ if }i\leqslant N-\mu_{n}<j\text{ or }j\leqslant N-\mu_{n}<i,\\
\mathfrak{s}_{i,j},&\text{ otherwise. }\\
\end{cases}

Theorem C

(Baby comultiplication, Theorem
9.2
)
.

For each
N\times N
shift matrix
\sigma
there exists an injective homomorphism
\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{\mu_{n}})
where
\mu=(\mu_{1},...,\mu_{n})
is the minimal admissible shape. Moreover, for each
\ell>2\mathfrak{s}_{1,N}
, this descends to an injective algebra homomorphism

\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell-2}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{\mu_{n}}).

One finds explicit formulas for
\Delta_{R}
acting on generators of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
in Theorem
9.2
. Showing the baby comultiplication equipped with these formulas is an algebra homomorphism in our twisted Yangian setting requires significantly more effort than the counterpart in
[
BK06
]
.
The baby comultiplication allows us to study the truncation
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
inductively, by reducing the level. This allows us to obtain a PBW theorem, and describe the center of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
. These results are pivotal when we relate twisted Yangians to finite
W
-algebras. In order to state these results, we now introduce the combinatorics involved in relating twisted Yangians and finite
W
-algebras.

Let
(\phi,\sigma,\ell)
be a triple consisting of a sign
\phi\in\{\pm\}
, an
N\times N
shift matrix
\sigma
for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\phi}
and a level
\ell>2\mathfrak{s}_{1,N}
. From this data we construct a pair
(\mathfrak{g},e)
consisting of a classical Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and a nilpotent element
e\in\mathfrak{g}
, via the following recipe. We let
M:=N\ell-\sum_{i=1}^{N}2\mathfrak{s}_{i,N}
, and then set

(1.9)

\mathfrak{g}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{so}_{M},&\textnormal{ for }\phi=+,\ \ell\textnormal{ odd},\textnormal{ or }\phi=-,\ \ell\textnormal{ even}.\\
\mathfrak{sp}_{M},&\textnormal{ for }\phi=+,\ \ell\textnormal{ even},\textnormal{ or }\phi=-,\ \ell\textnormal{ odd}.\end{cases}

Consider the partition
p=(p_{1},...,p_{N})
of
M
given by

(1.10)

p_{i}:=\ell-2\mathfrak{s}_{i,N}.

By construction, there exist nilpotent elements in
\mathfrak{g}
such that the sizes of the Jordan blocks are given by
p
. We let
e\in\mathfrak{g}
be any such nilpotent element.

The Sklyanin determinant is the formal power series
{\mathrm{sdet}}S(u)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}c_{r}u^{-r}
with coefficients in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
, such that
\{c_{r}\mid r\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\}
is a collection of algebraically independent generators for the center
Z{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
, see
[
MNO96
]
.

Theorem D

(Truncated PBW theorem, Corollaries
11.10
,
11.18
and Theorem
12.6
)
.

(i)

There is a natural surjection
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma)\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{e}
, and the identification
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)=\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma))
induces an identification
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)=\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{e})
.

(ii)

If
\mathfrak{g}
in (
1.9
) has type
B
or
C
then the map
Z{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)\twoheadrightarrow Z{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
is surjective and
Z{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
is a polynomial ring in rank
\mathfrak{g}
variables.

We also show that Theorem
D
(ii) holds also if
\mathfrak{g}
has type
\sf{D}
and
e
is a nilpotent element with 2 Jordan blocks in Theorem
12.9
.

1.5.
Dirac reduction of shifted Yangians and Slodowy slices

Let
(\phi,\sigma,\ell)
and
(\mathfrak{g},e)
be related as per (
1.9
) and (
1.10
). The Slodowy slice associated to
e
will be denoted
\mathcal{S}
. It admits a natural Poisson structure which is quantized by the finite
W
-algebra
[
GG02
]
. In recent work
[
To23
,
TT24
]
two of the authors used Dirac reduction to describe the Poisson structure on the Slodowy slice. If
(\mathfrak{g},e)
arises from a triple with
\phi=+
, they showed that this Poisson structure actually arises as a quotient of the semiclassical twisted shifted Yangian of type AI. Our next results extend that work to type AII.

Twisted Yangians are equipped with two filtrations. The loop filtration has been used above to deform twisted current algebras, whilst the canonical filtration has an associated graded algebra which is commutative. We write
y_{N}^{\pm}:=\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
for the associated canonically graded algebra. As a commutative algebra, it is isomorphic to
S(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta})
; however it comes equipped with an interesting Poisson structure, deforming the Lie–Poisson bracket. We introduce similar notation
y_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
in the shifted and truncated setting.

Write
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}]
for the algebra of regular functions on the Slodowy slice associated to
(\mathfrak{g},e)
, together with its graded Poisson structure.

Theorem E

(Corollaries
11.11
and
11.19
)
.

We have a graded Poisson algebra isomorphism

y_{N,\ell}^{\phi}(\sigma)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}]

where the doubled canonical grading coincides with the Kazhdan grading.

1.6.
Equivariant quantizations and finite
W
-algebras

The isomorphism between truncated shifted Yangians and finite W-algebras of type A in
[
BK06
]
was constructive via miraculously explicit formulas, then leveraging the powerful baby comultiplications to prove that these formulas satisfy all of the desired properties. We are unable to generalize such a constructive approach in our twisted setting in spite of serious effort, partly due to the use of different bilinear forms for
\mathfrak{g}
in
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)
and twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
. This is where our methods differ the most from
[
BK06
]
.

Conic symplectic singularities are a class of singular Poisson varieties, introduced by Beauville
[
Be00
]
.
In recent years there has been a huge amount of research activity surrounding the deep relationship between their geometry and the representation theory of their quantizations.
The key example of a conic symplectic singularity in this paper is the nilpotent Slodowy variety, i.e., the intersection of a nilpotent cone with a Slodowy slice.

Let
X
be a conic symplectic singularity. Namikawa has shown in
[
Na10
,
Na11
]
that the functor
\operatorname{PD}_{X}
of Poisson deformations is representable, and Losev has shown in
[
Lo22
]
that the universal Poisson deformation can be quantized. What is more, Losev’s quantization admits a very nice universal property. In
[
ACET23
]
the functor
\operatorname{Q}_{X}
of quantizations of flat Poisson deformations of
X
was introduced, and it was explained that the universal property is equivalent to the representability of the functor of quantizations. The results of Namikawa and Losev were upgraded
loc. cit.
to the equivariant setting, with respect to a group of graded Poisson automorphisms of the underlying singularity.

Let
(\phi,\sigma,\ell)
and
(\mathfrak{g},e)
be as above. In most cases, the Slodowy slice
\mathcal{S}
to
\mathfrak{g}
at
e
can be identified with the universal Poisson deformation of the nilpotent part
\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{g})

[
LNS12
]
. Consequently, the finite
W
-algebra
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)
is isomorphic to a universal quantization of this symplectic singularity. In the cases where the Slodowy slice is not universal, it turns out that the Slodowy slice is the universal equivariant deformation with respect to a certain group of Poisson automorphisms, unless
(\mathfrak{g},e)
falls into two special cases: regular orbits or nilpotent elements with two odd blocks in type
C
(see
[
AT25
]
and §
for more detail).

If the Slodowy slice is the universal equivariant deformation, we prove that
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
and
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)
are universal equivariant quantizations of the nilpotent Slodowy variety, and thus we prove our final theorem. Note that one of the key conditions for a filtered algebra to be a filtered quantization of the flat Poisson deformation is that the Poisson center of the deformations lifts to the center of the filtered algebra, and so our proof depends crucially on Theorem
D
(ii). In the special cases where the Slodowy slice is not known to be the universal equivariant deformation we prove the following theorem directly in one case and use a result of
[
Br09
]
for the other case. We can now formulate the
\sf B\sf C\sf D
analogue of
[
BK06
]
and a vast generalization of
[
Br09
]
.

Theorem F

(Theorem
)
.

If
\mathfrak{g}
has type
B
or
C
, or if
\mathfrak{g}
has type
D
and
e
has only two Jordan blocks, then there exists an isomorphism of filtered algebras

{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)

where the doubled canonical filtration lines up with the Kazhdan filtration.

We expect that Theorem
F
holds without restrictions in type
D
. To prove this it would suffice to show that the Poisson center of the Slodowy slice lifts to the center of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
through Theorem
E
. This would be implied by an affirmative answer to our Conjecture
12.8
or Conjecture
12.12
.

The results of this article may shed light on several interesting old and new questions. One hopes that our work helps to provide a two-way interaction between the representation theory of twisted Yangians to the representation theory of finite
W
-algebras of type
BCD
(compare, e.g.,
[
BK08
,
BG13
,
Mol07
,
GRW17
]
). It remains an open question to give a presentation for the remaining finite
W
-algebras of type
BCD
. It will be interesting to understand the precise relationship to a wide class of truncated shifted twisted Yangians which are constructed very differently in
[
LWW25
]
.

1.7.
Organization

This paper is organized into two parts.

Part 1, which consists of Sections
2
–
7
, provides the parabolic presentations of twisted Yangians of type AI and AII; this can be viewed as a highly nontrivial twisted counterpart of
[
BK05
]
. Part 2, which consists of Sections
8
–
13
, formulates the shifted twisted Yangians and establishes the connection of the truncated shifted twisted Yangians to finite
W
-algebras. This part achieves a similar goal as in
[
BK06
]
for finite
W
-algebras of types
\sf B\sf C\sf D
; however, we require new techniques coming from universal equivariant quantization of nilpotent Slodowy varieties.

In Section
2
, we recall the definitions and basic properties of Yangians of type A and twisted Yangians of type AI and AII, including coproduct structures, PBW theorem, associated graded, various homomorphisms, and quantum/Sklyanin determinant and minors.

In Section
3
, we obtain new parabolic generators for twisted Yangians by performing the block-wise Gauss decomposition, and establish some simple relations and basic properties for these parabolic generators. Moreover, we describe the
Sklyanin determinant in terms of parabolic generators.

In Section
4
,
we state our first main result (Theorem
A
), the parabolic presentations of type AI and AII in a uniform way.
Assuming some results in low rank cases in subsequent Sections
5
–
6
, we provide a detailed proof for type AI. The defining relations in the special case when the composition
\mu
has only two and three parts are derived in Section
5
and Section
6
, respectively; in particular the Serre relations are deduced.

In Section
7
,
we complete the proof of parabolic presentations (Theorem
A
) for type AII, providing the necessary modifications in this type.

In Section
8
,
we introduce the shifted twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{\mu}(\sigma)
associated to a symmetric shift matrix
\sigma
and an admissible shape
\mu
. We establish their PBW bases (Theorem
B
) and show that the definition does not depend on
\mu
. We also show that in type AI our definition coincides with a previous definition of the shifted Yangians given in
[
TT24
]
in terms of the Drinfeld presentation from
[
LWZ23
]
.

In Section
9
,
we define the baby comultiplications from Theorem
C
and show that they give well defined algebra homomorphisms.

In Section
10
,
we relate the semiclassical shifted twisted Yangian of type AII to a Dirac reduction of its untwisted counterpart. This is a type AII analogue of
[
TT24
, §3]
where this was done for type AI. This is one of our main technical tools used in the proof of Theorem
E
.

In Section
11
,
we recall the notion of pyramids and relate a pyramid to a unique pair
(\sigma,\ell)
, where
\sigma
is a symmetric shift matrix and
\ell
is an integer called level. With
(\sigma,\ell)
, together with a composition
\mu
which is admissible to
\sigma
, we then define the truncated shifted twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
as the quotient of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)
over a certain ideal
I_{\ell}
. We show that the definition is independent of the choice of admissible shape
\mu
. The PBW bases of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
are also obtained.

In Section
12
,
we describe the center of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
explicitly in terms of the Sklyanin determinant whenever the corresponding finite
W
-algebra is of type
B
or
C
. We introduce the notion of a Pfaffian generator in
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
and conjecture the existence of such an element whenever the corresponding finite
W
-algebra is of type
D
. We verify this conjecture when
N=2
.

In Section
13
, we describe the Poisson structures on the Slodowy slices and describe the finite
W
-algebras. We then review the theory of equivariant flat graded Poisson deformations for a conic symplectic singularity
X
, as well as the classification of equivariant quantizations. The key fact we explain is the uniqueness (up to isomorphism) of the universal equivariant quantization (Corollary
). Automorphisms of the truncated shifted twisted Yangians related to subregular finite
W
-algebras in type
B
are also studied. Finally we prove Theorem
F
.

Acknowledgements.
KL and WW are partially supported by WW’s NSF grant DMS–2401351. YP is partially supported by the NSTC grant 111-2628-M-006-006-MY3 and the National Center of Theoretical Sciences, Taipei, Taiwan. LT is funded by the UKRI Future Leaders Fellowship grant number MR/Z000394/1.

Part I
Parabolic presentations of twisted Yangians

2.
Preliminaries

In this section, we review the basics about Yangians and twisted Yangians of type AI and AII; cf.
[
Mol07
]
.

2.1.
Yangians
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})

The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
is generated by elements
e_{ij}
,
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
, with the commutator relations

[e_{ij},e_{kl}]=\delta_{jk}e_{il}-\delta_{il}e_{kj}.

Introduce the generating series in an indeterminate
u
, for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
,

t_{ij}(u)=\delta_{ij}+t_{ij}^{(1)}u^{-1}+t_{ij}^{(2)}u^{-2}+\cdots.

The
Yangian

\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
is a unital associative algebra with generators
t_{ij}^{(r)}
, where
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
and
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, subject to relations

(2.1)

(u-v)[t_{ij}(u),t_{kl}(v)]=t_{kj}(u)t_{il}(v)-t_{kj}(v)t_{il}(u).

By convention, we also set
t_{ij}^{(0)}=\delta_{ij}
.

Let
E_{ij}
,
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
, be the standard matrix units in
\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{C}^{N})
, and

R(u)=1-u^{-1}P,\qquad P=\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}E_{ij}\otimes E_{ji},\qquad T(u)=\big(t_{ij}(u)\big)_{i,j=1}^{N}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}E_{ij}\otimes t_{ij}(u).

Then the relations (
2.1
) can be compactly described by the RTT relations

R(u-v)T_{1}(u)T_{2}(v)=T_{2}(v)T_{1}(u)R(u-v),

considered as an equality in
\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{C}^{N})\otimes\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{C}^{N})\otimes\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})[[u^{-1},v^{-1}]]
.

There exists an evaluation homomorphism

(2.2)

\pi_{N}:\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})\longrightarrow\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}),\qquad t_{ij}^{(r)}\mapsto\delta_{1r}e_{ij}.

The Yangian
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
is a Hopf algebra with the coproduct given by

(2.3)

\Delta(t_{ij}(u))=\sum_{k=1}^{N}t_{ik}(u)\otimes t_{kj}(u).

Proposition 2.1

(
[
BK05
, Cor. 3.2]
,
[
MNO96
, Cor. 1.23]
)
.

The monomials in the generators
\{t_{ij}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N,r\geqslant 1\}
taken in any fixed linear order form a basis of
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
.

Define the
loop
filtration on
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
by setting

(2.4)

\displaystyle\deg t_{ij}^{(r)}=r-1

for every
r\geqslant 1
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{s}\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
be the subspace of
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
spanned by elements of degree
\leqslant s
,

\mathcal{F}_{0}\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})\subset\mathcal{F}_{1}\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})\subset\mathcal{F}_{2}\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})\subset\cdots,\qquad\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})=\bigcup_{s\geqslant 1}\mathcal{F}_{s}\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}).

Denote by
\mathrm{gr}\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
the associated graded algebra. We write
\bar{t}_{ij}^{(r)}
for the image of
t_{ij}^{(r)}
in
\mathrm{gr}\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
.

Let
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]
denote the
current algebra

\mathfrak{gl}_{N}\otimes\mathbb{C}[z]
with the standard basis

\{e_{ij}z^{r}\penalty\ |\penalty\ 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N,\ r\geqslant 0\}.

Let
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z])
be the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]
. Then the map

\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z])\to\mathrm{gr}\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}),\qquad e_{ij}\otimes z^{r}\mapsto\bar{t}_{ij}^{(r+1)},

induces a Hopf algebra isomorphism.

Let
\mathfrak{S}_{N}
be the symmetric group on the letters
\{1,2,\dots,N\}
. Define the
quantum determinant
of
T(u)
by

(2.5)

{\mathrm{qdet}}T(u)=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{N}}(-1)^{\sigma}t_{\sigma(1),1}(u)t_{\sigma(2),2}(u-1)\cdots t_{\sigma(N),N}(u-N+1).

The coefficients of
{\mathrm{qdet}}T(u)
are algebraically independent generators of the center of
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
; see
[
Mol07
, Thm. 1.7.5]
. The quantum determinant is group-like under the coproduct
[
Mol07
, Cor. 1.6.10]
:

(2.6)

\Delta({\mathrm{qdet}}T(u))={\mathrm{qdet}}T(u)\otimes{\mathrm{qdet}}T(u).

2.2.
Twisted Yangians

We shall consider twisted Yangians of type AI and AII in some
N\times N
matrix forms. We denote a classical Lie algebra by

(2.7)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{N}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{o}_{N},&\text{ in type AI},\\
\mathfrak{sp}_{N},&\text{ in type AII},\end{cases}

where in the latter case
N
is assumed to be even. Throughout the article, whenever the signs
\pm
or
\mp
show up, the top one corresponds to type AI while the bottom one corresponds to AII. To describe the two cases in a uniform way, we introduce the following convention.

In type AI, we set

(2.8)

i^{\prime}=i\quad\text{ and }\quad\theta_{i}=1,\quad\forall 1\leqslant i\leqslant N.

In type AII, we set

(2.9)

i^{\prime}=\begin{cases}i-1,&\text{ if }i=2k,\\
i+1,&\text{ if }i=2k-1,\end{cases}\qquad\qquad\theta_{i}=(-1)^{i}.

In both cases, we take

(2.10)

G=(\delta_{ij^{\prime}}\theta_{i})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}.

Note that
G=\mathbf{I}_{N}
is the identity matrix in type AI.

Define the generating series, for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
,

(2.11)

s_{ij}(u)=\delta_{ij}+s_{ij}^{(1)}u^{-1}+s_{ij}^{(2)}u^{-2}+\cdots.

Definition 2.2
.

The
twisted Yangian

{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
of type AI/AII is a unital associative algebra with generators
s_{ij}^{(r)}
, where
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
and
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, subject to the
quaternary
relations

(2.12)

\begin{split}(u^{2}-v^{2})[s_{ij}(u),s_{kl}(v)]=&\,(u+v)(s_{kj}(u)s_{il}(v)-s_{kj}(v)s_{il}(u))\\
-&\,(u-v)(\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}s_{ik^{\prime}}(u)s_{j^{\prime}l}(v)-\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}s_{ki^{\prime}}(v)s_{l^{\prime}j}(u))\\
&\,\hskip 46.66249pt+\theta_{i}\theta_{j^{\prime}}(s_{ki^{\prime}}(u)s_{j^{\prime}l}(v)-s_{ki^{\prime}}(v)s_{j^{\prime}l}(u)),\end{split}

and the
symmetry
relations

(2.13)

\theta_{i}\theta_{j}s_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(-u)=s_{ij}(u)\pm\frac{s_{ij}(u)-s_{ij}(-u)}{2u}.

Note that our definition corresponds to the one in
[
Mol07
, §2.15]
with the matrix
G
in (
2.10
). In the orthogonal case, it is the same as the one used in
[
LWZ23
]
while in the symplectic case, it is the one used in
[
Mol07
, §4.1]
after rearranging the indices.

Define the modified transpose for the R-matrix
R(u)
and the matrix
S(u)
, respectively, by

R^{\sf T}(u)=1-u^{-1}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}E_{ij}\otimes E_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}},\quad S(u)=\big(s_{ij}(u)\big)_{i,j=1}^{N}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}E_{ij}\otimes s_{ij}(u).

Note that the modified transpose coincides with the ordinary transpose for orthogonal case. The quaternary relations (
2.12
) can be compactly described by the reflection equations

R(u-v)S_{1}(u)R^{\sf T}(-u-v)S_{2}(v)=S_{2}(v)R^{\sf T}(-u-v)S_{1}(u)R(u-v).

It follows from (
2.12
) that

(2.14)

[s_{ij}(u),s_{kl}^{(1)}]=\delta_{jk}s_{il}(u)-\delta_{il}s_{kj}(u)+\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}\delta_{i^{\prime}k}s_{l^{\prime}j}(u)-\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\delta_{j^{\prime}l}s_{ik^{\prime}}(u).

From (
2.13
), it is immediate that

s_{i^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(-u)=s_{ii}(u)\pm\frac{s_{ii}(u)-s_{ii}(-u)}{2u}

for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
. Indeed, given the quaternary relations, the symmetry relations can be reduced to a single relation as follows.

Lemma 2.3

(cf.
[
LWZ23
, Lem. 2.2]
)
.

Suppose
s_{ij}(u)
,
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
, satisfy the quaternary relations (
2.12
). If for any single
1\leqslant k\leqslant N
we have

(2.15)

s_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime}}(-u)=s_{kk}(u)\pm\frac{s_{kk}(u)-s_{kk}(-u)}{2u},

then
s_{ij}(u)
also satisfy the symmetry relations (
2.13
) for arbitrary
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
.

Proof.

For type AI, it is clear that (
2.15
) is equivalent to
s_{kk}(u)=s_{kk}(-u)
and hence the statement follows from
[
LWZ23
, Lem. 2.2]
.

For type AII, it is similar to the proof of
[
LWZ23
, Lem. 2.2]
. Setting
i=k^{\prime}
and
u=-v
in (
2.12
), we have

\displaystyle 2v\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}s_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime}}(-v)s_{j^{\prime}l}(v)-

\displaystyle 2v\theta_{k^{\prime}}\theta_{l^{\prime}}s_{kk}(v)s_{l^{\prime}j}(-v)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\theta_{k^{\prime}}\theta_{j}(s_{kk}(-v)s_{j^{\prime}l}(v)-s_{kk}(v)s_{j^{\prime}l}(-v))=0.

Substituting
s_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime}}(-v)
using (
2.15
) and simplifying it, we find a common factor
s_{kk}(v)
multiplying from the left. Canceling
s_{kk}(v)
, we obtain the symmetry relation (
2.13
).
∎

Remark 2.4
.

The following slightly stronger statement holds. If
s_{ij}(u)
,
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
, satisfy the quaternary relations (
2.12
) and the equation
s_{kk}^{(2r-1)}+s_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime}}^{(2r-1)}=0
holds for any single
1\leqslant k\leqslant N
, then
s_{ij}(u)
satisfy the symmetry relations (
2.13
) for any
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
.

Proposition 2.5

(
[
MNO96
,
Mol07
]
)
.

(1)

The monomials in the elements

\big\{s_{ij}^{(r)}\big\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},1\leqslant j<i\leqslant N}\cup\big\{s_{kk}^{(2r)}\big\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},1\leqslant k\leqslant N}

taken in any fixed linear order form a PBW basis of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
.

(2)

The monomials in the elements

\displaystyle\big\{s_{2i-1,2i-1}^{(r)}\big\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,1\leqslant i\leqslant N/2}

\displaystyle\cup\big\{s_{2i-1,2i}^{(2r-1)},\,s_{2i,2i-1}^{(2r-1)}\big\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,1\leqslant i\leqslant N/2}

\displaystyle\cup\big\{s_{ij}^{(r)}\big\}_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant N,\,\lfloor\tfrac{i+1}{2}\rfloor\neq\lfloor\tfrac{j+1}{2}\rfloor}

taken in any fixed linear order form a PBW basis of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
.

It is well known that
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
can be identified as a subalgebra of
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
. Specifically, the map

(2.16)

S(u)\mapsto T^{t}(-u)GT(u)G^{-1}

defines an embedding
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
. Here
t
stands for the regular transpose. Then there is a filtration, which we call the
loop filtration
again, on
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
inherited from the one (
2.4
) on
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
such that
\deg s_{ij}^{(r)}=r-1
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
be the subspace of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
spanned by elements of degree
\leqslant s
such that

(2.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{0}{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}\subset\mathcal{F}_{1}{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}\subset\mathcal{F}_{2}{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}\subset\ldots,\qquad\qquad{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}=\bigcup_{s\geqslant 0}\mathcal{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}.

Denote by
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
the associated graded algebra. Let
\bar{s}_{ij}^{(r)}
be the image of
s_{ij}^{(r)}
in the
(r-1)
-st component of
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
.

Let
\theta
be the involution of
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
defined by

(2.18)

\displaystyle\theta:\mathfrak{gl}_{N}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{gl}_{N},\quad e_{ij}\mapsto-\theta_{i}\theta_{j}e_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}.

Let
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}^{\theta}
denote the fixed point subalgebra of
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
under
\theta
. For
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
, set

f_{ij}:=e_{ij}-\theta_{i}\theta_{j}e_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}.

Then
f_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}=-\theta_{i}\theta_{j}f_{ij}
and
f_{ij}
are elements in
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}^{\theta}
. Moreover,
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}^{\theta}
is spanned by
f_{ij}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{N}=\mathfrak{gl}_{N}^{\theta}
. It is well known that the twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
have the evaluation homomorphism

(2.19)

\xi^{\pm}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\to\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{N}),\qquad s_{ij}(u)\mapsto\delta_{ij}+(u+\tfrac{1}{2})^{-1}f_{ij}.

In addition, the map
f_{ij}\mapsto s_{ij}^{(1)}
defines an embedding of the algebra
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{N})
into
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
.

Extend
\theta
to an involution on
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]
(again denoted
\theta
) by sending
g\otimes z^{r}
to
\theta(g)\otimes(-z)^{r}
for
g\in\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
and
r\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}
be the fixed point subalgebra of
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]
under the involution
\theta
. Then it is well known that the map

\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta})\longrightarrow\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm},\qquad e_{ij}\otimes z^{r}-(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}e_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\otimes z^{r}\mapsto\bar{s}_{ij}^{(r+1)}

induces an algebra isomorphism.

The following result follows from (
2.3
) and (
2.16
).

Lemma 2.6
.

The twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is a right coideal subalgebra of
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
. In particular, we have

(2.20)

\Delta\big(s_{ij}(u)\big)=\sum_{a,b=1}^{N}s_{ab}(u)\otimes\theta_{a}\theta_{i}t_{a^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(-u)t_{bj}(u)\in{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\otimes\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}).

Remark 2.7
.

There exists another embedding
\iota:{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
defined by

S(u)\mapsto T(u)GT^{t}(-u)G^{-1},

see
[
Mol07
, (2.108)]
. With respect to
\iota
,
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
becomes a
left
coideal subalgebra of
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
instead,
and the restriction of the comultiplication to
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is given by

\Delta\big(s_{ij}(u)\big)=\sum_{a,b=1}^{N}\theta_{j}\theta_{b}t_{ia}(u)t_{j^{\prime}b^{\prime}}(-u)\otimes s_{ab}(u).

It is convenient to work with the extended twisted Yangians defined below instead of twisted Yangians. By abuse of notations, we shall keep using the same notations for the twisted Yangian and extended twisted Yangians
of various elements such as
s_{ij}(u)
and
S(u)
, etc.

Definition 2.8
.

The
extended twisted Yangian

{\mathscr{X}}^{\pm}_{N}
of type AI/AII is the unital associative algebra with generators
s_{ij}^{(r)}
, where
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
and
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, satisfying the quaternary relations (
2.12
), where
s_{ij}(u)
is again given by (
2.11
).

2.3.
Homomorphisms of (extended) twisted Yangians

In this subsection, we discuss various (anti)homomorphisms between (extended) twisted Yangians.

Let
\widetilde{S}(u)
be the inverse of the generating matrix
S(u)
:

(2.21)

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}(u)=\big(\tilde{s}_{ij}(u)\big):=S(u)^{-1}.

For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
, set

\bar{i}=N+1-i.

Then we have the following (anti)homomorphisms, see e.g.
[
Mol07
, §2]
.

(1)

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have the natural homomorphism
\jmath:{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{m+N}^{\pm}
defined by

\jmath_{m}(s_{ij}(u))=s_{ij}(u).

Here
m
is even if
\mathfrak{g}_{N}=\mathfrak{sp}_{N}
.

(2)

The map
\tau:{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}
defined by

\tau(s_{ij}(u))=\theta_{i}\theta_{j}s_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(u)

is an anti-automorphism.

(3)

The map
\omega_{N}:{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}
defined by

\omega_{N}(S(u))=\widetilde{S}(-u-\tfrac{N}{2})

is an involution.

(4)

The map
\rho_{N}:{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}
defined by

\rho_{N}(s_{ij}(u))=s_{\bar{i}\,\bar{j}}(u)

is an isomorphism.

(5)

The map
\zeta_{N}:{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}
defined by

\zeta_{N}=\rho_{N}\circ\omega_{N},\qquad s_{ij}(u)\mapsto\tilde{s}_{\bar{i}\,\bar{j}}(-u-\tfrac{N}{2})

is an isomorphism.

(6)

For
m>0
, there is a homomorphism
\varphi_{m}:{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{m+N}^{\pm}
given by shifting indices:

\varphi_{m}(s_{ij}(u))=s_{m+i,m+j}(u).

Then the map
\psi_{m}:{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{m+N}^{\pm}
defined by

\psi_{m}=\omega_{m+N}\circ\varphi_{m}\circ\omega_{N}

is a homomorphism. Here
m
is even if
\mathfrak{g}_{N}=\mathfrak{sp}_{N}
.

2.4.
Sklyanin determinants and minors

We recall the definition of Sklyanin determinant and minors from
[
Mol07
, Chap. 2.5–2.6]
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
m\leqslant N
. We adopt the following abbreviation:

u_{i}=u-i+1,\qquad S_{i}=S_{i}(u_{i}),\qquad i=1,2,\ldots,m.

Let

R_{ij}^{\sf T}=R_{ji}^{\sf T}=R_{ij}^{\sf T}(-u_{i}-u_{j}),\qquad 1\leqslant i<j\leqslant m.

Here we use the standard convention of subscripts to indicate the copies of
\mathbb{C}^{N}
in
(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes m}
it acts on, cf.
[
Mol07
, Chap. 1.2]
. Denote by
A_{m}
the image of the anti-symmetrizer
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{m}}\text{sgn }\sigma\cdot\sigma
under the natural action of
\mathfrak{S}_{m}
on
(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes m}
.

For an arbitrary permutation
(p_{1},p_{2},\ldots,p_{m})
of the letters
1,2,\ldots,m
, we denote by

\langle S_{p_{1}},\ldots,S_{p_{m}}\rangle=S_{p_{1}}(R_{p_{1}p_{2}}^{\sf T}\ldots R_{p_{1}p_{m}}^{\sf T})S_{p_{2}}(R_{p_{2}p_{3}}^{\sf T}\ldots R_{p_{2}p_{m}}^{\sf T})\ldots S_{p_{m}}.

Let
m=N
. It is known that

(2.22)

A_{N}\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{N}\rangle=\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{N}\rangle A_{N}.

Since the image of the antisymmetrizer on
(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes N}
is one-dimensional, the element (
2.22
) equals
A_{N}
times a scalar series with coefficients in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
.

Definition 2.9
.

The
Sklyanin determinant
of the matrix
S(u)
with coefficients in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is the formal series

{\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)=1+c_{1}u^{-1}+c_{2}u^{-2}+\cdots

such that the element (
2.22
) equals
A_{N}{\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)
.

If
m\leqslant N
, then we have

A_{m}\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}\rangle=\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{m}\rangle A_{m}.

This element of the tensor product
\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes m}\otimes{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}[[u^{-1}]]
can be written as

\sum e_{a_{1}b_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{a_{m}b_{m}}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{a_{1}\cdots a_{m}}_{b_{1}\cdots b_{m}}(u),

summed over the indices
a_{i},b_{i}\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, where
\mathcal{S}^{a_{1}\cdots a_{m}}_{b_{1}\cdots b_{m}}(u)\in{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}[[u^{-1}]]
are the
Sklyanin minors
of the matrix
S(u)
. Note that the Sklyanin minors are skew-symmetric with respect to permutations of the upper indices and of the lower indices:

(2.23)

\mathcal{S}^{a_{\sigma(1)}\cdots a_{\sigma(m)}}_{b_{1}\cdots b_{m}}(u)=\mathcal{S}_{b_{\sigma(1)}\cdots b_{\sigma(m)}}^{a_{1}\cdots a_{m}}(u)=\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)\mathcal{S}^{a_{1}\cdots a_{m}}_{b_{1}\cdots b_{m}}(u)

for any
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{m}
. Moreover,

\mathcal{S}_{b}^{a}(u)=s_{ab}(u),\quad\mathcal{S}_{1\cdots N}^{1\cdots N}(u)={\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u).

We shall also use the shorthand notation

\mathcal{C}_{k}(u)=\mathcal{S}_{1\cdots k}^{1\cdots k}(u).

The following is well known.

Theorem 2.10

(
[
MNO96
]
)
.

The coefficients of the Sklyanin determinant
{\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)
are central elements in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
(resp.
{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{\pm}
). Moreover,
\{c_{2k}\}_{k\geqslant 1}
is a set of algebraically independent generators of the center of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
.

Define
\gamma_{c}^{\pm}(u)
, for
c\in\mathbb{Z}
, by

(2.24)

\gamma_{c}^{\pm}(u)=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}_{N}=\mathfrak{o}_{N},\\
\frac{2u+1}{2u-c+1},&\text{ if }\mathfrak{g}_{N}=\mathfrak{sp}_{N}.\end{cases}

Identifying
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
as a subalgebra of
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
by (
2.16
), one has by
[
Mol07
, Thm 2.5.3]
that

(2.25)

{\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)=\gamma^{\pm}_{N}(u)\,{\mathrm{qdet}}\,T(u)\,{\mathrm{qdet}}\,T(-u+N-1).

It is immediate from (
2.6
) and (
2.25
) that

(2.26)

\Delta\big({\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)\big)={\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)\otimes{\mathrm{qdet}}\,T(u)\,{\mathrm{qdet}}\,T(-u+N-1).

3.
Gauss decomposition and parabolic generators for type AI

In this section we use Gauss decomposition to construct new parabolic generators and establish a PBW basis result for the twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
of type AI associated to any composition of
N
.
Parallel results for twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
of type AII will be addressed in Section
7
.

3.1.
Gauss decomposition and quasideterminants

We say that
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
is a
(strict) composition
of
N
of length
n
if
\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
\mu_{1}+\ldots+\mu_{n}=N
. Given a composition
\mu
of
N
with length
n
, define

(3.1)

\mu_{(i)}:=\mu_{1}+\ldots+\mu_{i}

for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
. Also denote
\mu_{(0)}=0
.

Recall the
N\times N
matrix
S(u)=\big(s_{ij}(u)\big)_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}
with entries in
{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{+}[[u^{-1}]]
. Since the leading minors of the matrix
S(u)
are invertible, for any given composition
\mu
of
N
, it possesses a Gauss decomposition with respect to
\mu
; that is,

(3.2)

S(u)=F(u)D(u)E(u)

for unique
(\mu\times\mu)
-block matrices
D(u)
,
E(u)
and
F(u)
of the form

D(u)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}D_{1}(u)&0&\cdots&0\\
0&D_{2}(u)&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&D_{n}(u)\end{array}\right),

E(u)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}I_{\mu_{1}}&E_{1,2}(u)&\cdots&E_{1,n}(u)\\
0&I_{\mu_{2}}&\cdots&E_{2,n}(u)\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&I_{\mu_{n}}\end{array}\right),\>

F(u)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}I_{\mu_{1}}&0&\cdots&0\\
F_{2,1}(u)&I_{\mu_{2}}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
F_{n,1}(u)&F_{n,2}(u)&\cdots&I_{\mu_{n}}\end{array}\right),

where

(3.3)

\displaystyle D_{a}(u)

\displaystyle=\big(D_{a;i,j}(u)\big)_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a}},

(3.4)

\displaystyle E_{a,b}(u)

\displaystyle=\big(E_{a,b;i,j}(u)\big)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}},

(3.5)

\displaystyle F_{b,a}(u)

\displaystyle=\big(F_{b,a;j,i}(u)\big)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}},

are
\mu_{a}\times\mu_{a}
,

\mu_{a}\times\mu_{b}

and
\mu_{b}\times\mu_{a}
matrices, respectively, for all
1\leqslant a\leqslant n
in (
3.3
)
and all
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n
in (
3.4
)–(
3.5
). For each
a
, the submatrix
D_{a}(u)
is invertible, and we define the
\mu_{a}\times\mu_{a}
matrix

\widetilde{D}_{a}(u)=\big(\widetilde{D}_{a;i,j}(u)\big)_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a}}
by

\widetilde{D}_{a}(u):=\big(D_{a}(u)\big)^{-1}.

The entries of these matrices are power series in
u^{-1}
,

\displaystyle D_{a;i,j}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}D_{a;i,j}^{(r)}u^{-r},\ \ \qquad\widetilde{D}_{a;i,j}(u)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\widetilde{D}^{(r)}_{a;i,j}u^{-r},

\displaystyle E_{a,b;i,j}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}E_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}u^{-r},\qquad F_{b,a;j,i}(u)=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}F_{b,a;j,i}^{(r)}u^{-r}.

Note that
D_{a;i,j}^{(0)}=\widetilde{D}_{a;i,j}^{(0)}=\delta_{i,j}
. In addition, for
1\leqslant a<n
, we set

\displaystyle E_{a;i,j}(u):=

\displaystyle\,E_{a,a+1;i,j}(u)=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}E_{a;i,j}^{(r)}u^{-r},

\displaystyle F_{a;j,i}(u):=

\displaystyle\,F_{a+1,a;j,i}(u)=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}F_{a;j,i}^{(r)}u^{-r}.

We describe all these series in terms of the generators
s_{ij}(u)
explicitly by
quasideterminants

[
GGRW:2005
]
. Here we follow the notation in
[
BK05
, (4.3)]
. Suppose that
A,B,C
and
D
are
a\times a
,
a\times b
,
b\times a
and
b\times b
matrices respectively with entries in some ring.
Assuming that the matrix
A
is invertible, we define

\left|\begin{array}[]{cc}A&B\\
C&\framebox{\hbox{{$D$}}}\end{array}\right|:=D-CA^{-1}B.

Write the matrix
S(u)
in block form according to
\mu
as

(3.6)

S(u)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,n}(u)\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
{{}^{\mu}}S_{n,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{n,n}(u)\\
\end{array}\right),

where
{{}^{\mu}}S_{a,b}(u)
is a
\mu_{a}\times\mu_{b}
matrix.

Proposition 3.1
.

[
GGRW:2005
]
We have

\displaystyle D_{a}(u)=\left|\begin{array}[]{cccc}{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,a-1}(u)&{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,a}(u)\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,a-1}(u)&{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,a}(u)\\
{{}^{\mu}}S_{a,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{a,a-1}(u)&\hbox{\begin{tabular}[]{|c|}\hline\cr${{}^{\mu}}S_{a,a}(u)$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{array}\right|,

\displaystyle E_{a,b}(u)=\widetilde{D}_{a}(u)\left|\begin{array}[]{cccc}{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,a-1}(u)&{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,b}(u)\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,a-1}(u)&{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,b}(u)\\
{{}^{\mu}}S_{a,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{a,a-1}(u)&\hbox{\begin{tabular}[]{|c|}\hline\cr${{}^{\mu}}S_{a,b}(u)$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{array}\right|,

\displaystyle F_{b,a}(u)=\left|\begin{array}[]{cccc}{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,a-1}(u)&{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,a}(u)\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,a-1}(u)&{{}^{\mu}}S_{a-1,a}(u)\\
{{}^{\mu}}S_{b,1}(u)&\cdots&{{}^{\mu}}S_{b,a-1}(u)&\hbox{\begin{tabular}[]{|c|}\hline\cr${{}^{\mu}}S_{b,a}(u)$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}}\end{array}\right|\widetilde{D}_{a}(u),

for all
1\leqslant a\leqslant n
in (
3.1
) and
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n
in (
3.1
)–(
3.1
).

Denote
S_{a,b;i,j}(u)
the
(i,j)
-th entry of the
\mu_{a}\times\mu_{b}
matrix
{}^{\mu}S_{a,b}(u)
. Let
S_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}
be the coefficient of
u^{-r}
in
S_{a,b;i,j}(u)
. It is clear from Proposition
3.1
that

(3.19)

E_{b-1;i,j}^{(1)}=S_{b-1,b;i,j}^{(1)},\qquad F_{b-1;j,i}^{(1)}=S_{b,b-1;j,i}^{(1)},

for
1<b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b-1}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}
. Moreover, we have

(3.20)

D_{1;i,j}^{(r)}=S_{1,1;i,j}^{(r)}=s_{ij}^{(r)},\quad 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},\ r\geqslant 1.

3.2.
Parabolic generators

Recall
\widetilde{S}(u)=\big(\tilde{s}_{ij}(u)\big)=S(u)^{-1}
from (
2.21
).

Lemma 3.2

(
[
Mol07
, Prop. 2.12.2]
)
.

For
1\leqslant i,j,k,l\leqslant N
, we have

(3.21)

\begin{split}(u^{2}-v^{2})[s_{ij}(u),\tilde{s}_{kl}(v)]=&\,(u+v)\Big(\delta_{jk}\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{ia}(u)\tilde{s}_{al}(v)-\delta_{il}\sum_{a=1}^{N}\tilde{s}_{ka}(v)s_{aj}(u)\Big)\\
+&\,(u-v)\Big(\delta_{jl}\sum_{a=1}^{N}\tilde{s}_{ka}(v)s_{ia}(u)-\delta_{ik}\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{aj}(u)\tilde{s}_{al}(v)\Big)\\
&\hskip 22.76228pt+\Big(\delta_{ik}\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{ja}(u)\tilde{s}_{al}(v)-\delta_{jl}\sum_{a=1}^{N}\tilde{s}_{ka}(v)s_{ai}(u)\Big).\end{split}

Corollary 3.3
.

If
\{i,j\}\cap\{k,l\}=\varnothing
, then
[s_{ij}(u),\tilde{s}_{kl}(v)]=0
.

Lemma 3.4

(cf.
[
LWZ23
, Lem. 3.7]
)
.

For
1<a+1<b<n
and
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}
, we have

(3.22)

E_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}=[E_{a,b-1;i,k}^{(r)},E_{b-1;k,j}^{(1)}],\qquad F_{b,a;j,i}^{(r)}=[F_{b-1;j,k}^{(1)},F_{b-1,a;k,i}^{(r)}],

for any
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{b-1}
.

Proof.

The statement can be proved by induction on
b-a>1
. We only prove the case
a=1
and
b=3
for the first equality. The general case can be proved in a similar way while the second one follows from the first one by applying the anti-automorphism
\tau
; see Lemma
3.14
below.

It follows from (
3.1
) that

\big[E_{1,2;i,k}^{(r)},E_{2,3;l,j}^{(1)}\big]=\Big[\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=0}^{r}\widetilde{D}_{1;i,p}^{(q)}S_{1,2;p,k}^{(r-q)},S_{2,3;l,j}^{(1)}\Big].

Since
\widetilde{D}_{1;i,p}^{(q)}
are expressed in terms of
s_{\alpha,\beta}^{(r)}
for
1\leqslant\alpha,\beta\leqslant\mu_{1}
and

S_{2,3;l,j}^{(1)}=s_{\mu_{1}+l,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}^{(1)}=-\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+l,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}^{(1)},

it follows from (
3.21
) that
\widetilde{D}_{1;i,p}^{(q)}
and
S_{2,3;l,j}^{(1)}
commute. Thus

\displaystyle\big[E_{1,2;i,k}^{(r)},E_{2,3;l,j}^{(1)}\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=0}^{r}\widetilde{D}_{1;i,p}^{(q)}\big[S_{1,2;p,k}^{(r-q)},S_{2,3;l,j}^{(1)}\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=0}^{r}\widetilde{D}_{1;i,p}^{(q)}\big[s_{p,\mu_{1}+k}^{(r-q)},s_{\mu_{1}+l,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}^{(1)}\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=0}^{r}\widetilde{D}_{1;i,p}^{(q)}\delta_{kl}s_{p,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}^{(r-q)}=\delta_{kl}E_{1,3;i,j}^{(r)},

where we applied (
2.14
) and (
3.1
) in the third and last equalities, respectively. The general case is similar, except that the expression of
E_{a,b-1;i,k}^{(r)}
by (
3.1
) is more complicated.
∎

Proposition 3.5
.

The algebra
{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{+}
(or
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
) is generated by the following elements:

\displaystyle D_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{D}_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leqslant a\leqslant n,\ 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a},\ r>0,

\displaystyle E_{a;i,j}^{(r)},F_{a;j,i}^{(r)},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leqslant a<n,\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a+1},\ r>0.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.4
, all elements
E_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}
and
F_{b,a;j,i}^{(r)}
, for
r>0
,
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
, and
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}
, can be generated by these elements. Thus it follows from (
3.2
) that all
s_{ij}^{(r)}
for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
and
r>0
can be generated by them.
∎

By translation on the spectral parameter, we introduce the following formal series.
Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
be given. For
1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant b<n
, define

(3.23)

\displaystyle H_{a;i,j}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}u^{-r}:=D_{a;i,j}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(a-1)}}{2}),

(3.24)

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}u^{-r}:=\widetilde{D}_{a;i,j}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(a-1)}}{2}),

(3.25)

\displaystyle B_{b;k,l}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}B_{b;k,l}^{(r)}u^{-r}:=F_{b;k,l}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(b)}}{2}),

(3.26)

\displaystyle C_{b;l,k}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}C_{b;l,k}^{(r)}u^{-r}:=E_{b;l,k}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(b)}}{2}),

(3.27)

\displaystyle Z_{b;i,j,k,l}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}Z_{b;i,j,k,l}^{(r)}u^{-r}=\widetilde{H}_{b;i,j}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{b}}{2})H_{b+1;k,l}(u).

In particular, we have

(3.28)

Z_{b;i,j,k,l}^{(r)}=\sum_{t=0}^{r}\sum_{m=0}^{r-t}{t+m-1\choose m}\Big(\frac{\mu_{b}}{2}\Big)^{m}\widetilde{H}_{b;i,j}^{(t)}H_{b+1;k,l}^{(r-t-m)}.

For
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n
, set

(3.29)

\displaystyle B_{b,a;k,l}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}B_{b,a;k,l}^{(r)}u^{-r}:=F_{b,a;k,l}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(a)}}{2}),

(3.30)

\displaystyle C_{a,b;l,k}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}C_{a,b;l,k}^{(r)}u^{-r}:=E_{a,b;l,k}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(a)}}{2}).

In case
\mu=(1^{N})
, we further denote

(3.31)

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{a}(u)=1+\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\mathsf{d}_{a,r}u^{-r-1}:=D_{a;1,1}(u),\quad\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ 1\leqslant a\leqslant N,

(3.32)

\displaystyle\mathsf{h}_{0}(u)=1+\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\mathsf{h}_{0,r}u^{-r-1}:=H_{1;1,1}(u),

and

(3.33)

\displaystyle\mathsf{h}_{a}(u)=1+\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\mathsf{h}_{a,r}u^{-r-1}:=Z_{a;1,1,1,1}(u),

(3.34)

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}_{a}(u)=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\mathsf{b}_{a,r}u^{-r-1}:=B_{a;1,1}(u),\qquad\quad\penalty\ \penalty\ \,1\leqslant a<N.

We call the elements listed in Proposition
3.5
and (
3.23
)–(
3.26
) the
parabolic generators
. In case we would like to emphasize the choice of
\mu
, we write
{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{+},{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
as
{\mathscr{X}}_{\mu}^{+},{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
, respectively. The first main goal of this article is to explicitly write down the defining relations of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
in terms of the parabolic generators for any given
\mu
.

3.3.
Properties of parabolic generators

We shall need the following properties of parabolic generators, and in particular the properties under various (anti)homomorphisms
\jmath
,
\psi_{m}
,
\tau
,
\zeta_{N}
,
\varphi_{m}
,
\omega_{N}
introduced in §
2.3
.

Lemma 3.6

(
[
Mol07
, Lemma 2.14.1]
)
.

For any
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
, we have

\psi_{m}\big(s_{ij}(u+\tfrac{m}{2})\big)=\left|\begin{array}[]{cccc}s_{11}(u)&\cdots&s_{1m}(u)&s_{1,m+j}(u)\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
s_{m1}(u)&\cdots&s_{mm}(u)&s_{m,m+j}(u)\\
s_{m+i,1}(u)&\cdots&s_{m+i,m}(u)&\framebox{\hbox{{$s_{m+i,m+j}(u)$}}}\end{array}\right|.

Lemma 3.7
.

We have

\displaystyle D_{a;i,j}(u)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\mu_{(a-1)}}(D_{1;i,j}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{(a-1)}}{2})),

\displaystyle E_{a;i,j}(u)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\mu_{(a-1)}}(E_{1;i,j}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{(a-1)}}{2})),

\displaystyle F_{a;j,i}(u)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\mu_{(a-1)}}(F_{1;j,i}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{(a-1)}}{2})).

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
LWZ23
, Cor. 3.2]
.
∎

Lemma 3.8

(
[
LWZ23
, Lem. 3.3]
)
.

The subalgebras
\jmath({\mathscr{X}}_{m}^{+})
and
\psi_{m}({\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{+})
of
{\mathscr{X}}_{m+N}^{+}
commute with each other.

Corollary 3.9

(cf.
[
LWZ23
, Cor. 3.4]
)
.

We have
[D_{a;i,j}(u),D_{b;k,l}(v)]=0
if
a\neq b
and

\displaystyle[E_{a;i,j}(u),E_{b;k,l}(v)]=[E_{a;i,j}(u),F_{b;k,l}(v)]=[F_{a;i,j}(u),F_{b;k,l}(v)]=0,

\displaystyle\text{if }|a-b|>1,

(3.35)

\displaystyle[D_{a;i,j}(u),E_{b;k,l}(v)]=[D_{a;i,j}(u),F_{b;k,l}(v)]=0,

\displaystyle\text{if }a\neq b,b+1.

Proposition 3.10
.

We have
B_{b,a;l,k}(u)=C_{a,b;k,l}(-u)
for
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{a}
, and
1\leqslant l\leqslant\mu_{b}
.

Proof.

It is proved by induction on
b-a
. By applying (
3.22
) we reduce the general case to the cases for
b-a=1,2
. These two initial cases are based on (
5.28
) and Lemma
6.1
, which requires a careful study in low rank cases (we skip the details here to avoid repetition). Then we apply the shift homomorphism and use Lemma
3.7
.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

The algebra
{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{+}
(or
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
) is generated by the following elements:

\displaystyle H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leqslant a\leqslant n,\ 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a},\ r>0,

\displaystyle B_{a;j,i}^{(r)},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leqslant a<n,\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a+1},\ r>0.

Proof.

This follows from Propositions
3.5
and
3.10
.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

With an additional assumption that each
\mu_{i}
is even, the Gaussian decomposition (
3.2
) exists and Propositions
3.5
holds for type AII as well.
It then follows from Proposition
7.4
below that Corollary
3.11
also holds for
{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{-}
(or
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
).

Theorem 3.13
.

The monomials in the elements

\displaystyle\{H_{a;i,i}^{(2r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 1},\quad\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 1},\quad\{B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 1},

taken in any fixed linear order form a PBW basis of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
.

Proof.

Recall the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}\}_{s\geqslant 0}
from (
2.17
). By Gauss decomposition and the definitions of these elements from (
3.23
)–(
3.25
), we have

\displaystyle H_{a;i,i}^{(2r)}\equiv s_{\mu_{(a-1)}+i,\mu_{(a-1)}+i}^{(2r)}\pmod{\mathcal{F}_{2r-2}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}},

\displaystyle H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\equiv s_{\mu_{(a-1)}+i,\mu_{(a-1)}+j}^{(r)}\pmod{\mathcal{F}_{r-2}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}},

\displaystyle B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\equiv s_{\mu_{(b-1)}+i,\mu_{(a-1)}+j}^{(r)}\pmod{\mathcal{F}_{r-2}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}}.

Hence the statement follows from Proposition
2.5
.
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

We have

\displaystyle\tau(D_{a;i,j}(u))

\displaystyle=D_{a;j,i}(u),\qquad\qquad 1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a},

\displaystyle\tau(E_{a,b;i,j}(u))

\displaystyle=F_{b,a;j,i}(u),\qquad\qquad 1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b},

\displaystyle\tau(F_{b,a;j,i}(u))

\displaystyle=E_{a,b;i,j}(u),\qquad\qquad 1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}.

Finally, it is also convenient to have the following property of the automorphism
\zeta_{N}
of
{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{+}
. For a strict composition
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\ldots,\mu_{n})
of
N
, define

\bar{\mu}=(\mu_{n},\ldots,\mu_{2},\mu_{1}).

Proposition 3.15
.

For all
1\leqslant i,j,k\leqslant\mu_{a}
,
1\leqslant l\leqslant\mu_{a+1}
, we have
\zeta_{N}:{\mathscr{X}}_{\mu}^{+}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{\bar{\mu}}^{+}
with

\displaystyle\zeta_{N}\big(D_{a;i,j}(u)\big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{D}_{n+1-a;\mu_{a}+1-i,\mu_{a}+1-j}(-u-\tfrac{N}{2}),\hskip 27.5992pt1\leqslant a\leqslant n,

\displaystyle\zeta_{N}\big(E_{a;k,l}(u)\big)

\displaystyle=-F_{n-a;\mu_{a}+1-k,\mu_{a+1}+1-l}(-u-\tfrac{N}{2}),\qquad 1\leqslant a<n,

\displaystyle\zeta_{N}\big(F_{a;l,k}(u)\big)

\displaystyle=-E_{n-a;\mu_{a+1}+1-l,\mu_{a}+1-k}(-u-\tfrac{N}{2}),\qquad 1\leqslant a<n.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{N}\big(B_{a;l,k}(u)\big)

\displaystyle=-B_{n-a;\mu_{a}+1-k,\mu_{a+1}+1-l}(u),

\displaystyle\zeta_{N}(H_{a;i,j}(u))

\displaystyle=\widetilde{H}_{n+1-a;\mu_{a}+1-i,\mu_{a}+1-j}(-u),

\displaystyle\zeta_{N}(\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}(u))

\displaystyle=H_{n+1-a;\mu_{a}+1-i,\mu_{a}+1-j}(-u).

Proof.

It is proved similarly to
[
Go07
, Prop. 1]
and
[
LWZ23
, Lem. 3.6]
.
∎

3.4.
Sklyanin determinants in parabolic generators

Recall the notations from §
2.4
. For
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and tuples
\bm{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})
and
\bm{j}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k})
of integers from
\{1,\ldots,N\}
, we denote that

\mathcal{S}_{\bm{i},\bm{j}}(u):=\mathcal{S}^{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}_{j_{1}\cdots j_{k}}(u).

Denote by
m+\bm{i}
the
k
-tuple
(m+i_{1},\ldots,m+i_{k})
; similar for
m+\bm{j}
.

Lemma 3.16
.

The homomorphism
\varphi_{m}:{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{+}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{m+N}^{+}
sends

\mathcal{S}_{\bm{i},\bm{j}}(u)\mapsto\mathcal{S}_{m+\bm{i},m+\bm{j}}(u)
.

Proof.

This follows from the recurrence relations for Sklyanin minors established in
[
Mol07
, proof of Thm. 2.7.2]
, cf.
[
Mol07
, Prop. 2.13.10]
.
∎

Proposition 3.17
.

If
\mathfrak{g}_{N}=\mathfrak{o}_{N}
, then we have
\mathcal{C}_{k}(u)=\mathsf{d}_{1}(u)\mathsf{d}_{2}(u-1)\cdots\mathsf{d}_{k}(u-k+1)
in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
.

Proof.

For
k=N
, the claim follows from
[
Mol07
, Thm. 2.12.1]
. If
k<N
, then it reduces to the previous case by applying the homomorphism
\iota:{\mathscr{X}}_{k}^{+}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{N}^{+}
,
s_{ij}(u)\mapsto s_{ij}(u)
.
∎

Proposition 3.18
.

Let
\bm{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})
and
\bm{j}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k})
be
k
-tuples of distinct integers from
\{1,\ldots,N\}
. Let
\bm{i}^{\prime}=(i_{k+1},\ldots,i_{N})
and
\bm{j}^{\prime}=(j_{k+1},\ldots,j_{N})
be such that

\{i_{1},\dots,i_{N}\}=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{N}\}=\{1,\dots,N\}.

Let
\xi
denote the sign of the permutation
(i_{1},\dots,i_{N})\mapsto(j_{1},\dots,j_{N})
. Then we have

\omega_{N}\big(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{i},\bm{j}}(u)\big)=\xi\big({\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(-u+\tfrac{N}{2}-1)\big)^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{\bm{j}^{\prime},\bm{i}^{\prime}}(-u+\tfrac{N}{2}-1).

Proof.

By
[
Mol07
, Proof of Thm. 2.13.9]
, we have

\displaystyle A_{N}\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle\mathop{\overrightarrow{\prod}}\limits_{i=1,\ldots,k}(R^{\sf T}_{i,k+1}\cdots R_{iN}^{\sf T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{N}\,{\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)\,S_{N}^{\circ}R_{N-1,N}^{\circ}S_{N-1}^{\circ}\cdots S_{k+2}^{\circ}R_{k+1,N}^{\circ}\cdots R_{k+1,k+2}^{\circ}S_{k+1}^{\circ},

where
S^{\circ}(u)=\omega_{N}(S(u))
,
u_{i}=u-i+1
, and

u_{i}^{\circ}=-u_{i}-\tfrac{N}{2},\qquad S_{i}^{\circ}=S_{i}^{\circ}(u_{i}^{\circ}),\qquad R_{ij}^{\circ}=R^{\sf T}_{ij}(-u_{i}^{\circ}-u_{j}^{\circ}).

Equating the coefficients of the element

e_{i_{1}j_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{k},j_{k}}\otimes e_{i_{N}i_{N}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{k+1}i_{k+1}}

in the above identity and using (
2.23
), we deduce that

\mathcal{S}_{\bm{i},\bm{j}}(u)=\xi\ {\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)\ \omega_{N}\big(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{j}^{\prime},\bm{i}^{\prime}}(-u+\tfrac{N}{2}-1)\big).

The claim follows by suitable substitutions.
∎

Now we are able to describe the images of the Sklyanin minors under the homomorphism
\psi_{m}
.

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
\bm{i},\bm{j}
be
k
-tuples of distinct integers from
\{1,\ldots,N\}
. Then

\psi_{m}\big(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{i},\bm{j}}(u)\big)=\mathcal{C}_{m}(u+\tfrac{m}{2})^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{m\#\bm{i},m\#\bm{j}}(u+\tfrac{m}{2}),

where
m\#\bm{i}
denotes the
(m+k)
-tuple
(1,\ldots,m,m+i_{1},\ldots,m+i_{k})
.

Proof.

Note that
\psi_{m}=\omega_{m+N}\circ\varphi_{m}\circ\omega_{N}
. The lemma follows from applying Proposition
3.18
along with Lemma
3.16
.
∎

The next result is a generalization of
[
Mol07
, Thm. 2.12.1]
and
[
Br16
, Thm. 6.1]
; also cf.
[
BK05
, Thm. 8.6]
,
[
CH23
, Prop. 3.2]
.

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
be a composition of
N
with length
n
; recall
\mu_{(i)}
from (
3.1
). Then, we have

{\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)={\mathrm{sdet}}\,D_{1}(u-\mu_{(0)})\,{\mathrm{sdet}}\,D_{2}(u-\mu_{(1)})\cdots\,{\mathrm{sdet}}\,D_{n}(u-\mu_{(n-1)})

and, for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
,

{\mathrm{sdet}}\,{}^{\mu}D_{i}(u-\mu_{(i-1)})=\prod_{k=\mu_{(i-1)}+1}^{\mu_{(i)}}\mathsf{d}_{k}(u-k+1).

Proof.

Fix
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
and let
\bar{\mu}:=(\mu_{i},\mu_{i+1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
. It follows from Lemma
3.7
, we have

{}^{\mu}D_{i}(u-\mu_{(i-1)})=\psi_{\mu_{(i-1)}}({}^{\bar{\mu}}D_{1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(i-1)}}{2})),

where
{}^{\bar{\mu}}D_{1}(u-\mu_{(i-1)})\in{\mathscr{X}}^{+}_{N-\mu_{(i-1)}}
. Note that
{}^{\bar{\mu}}D_{1}(u)={}^{\bar{\mu}}S_{1,1}(u)
. Setting
\bm{i}=\bm{j}=(1,\dots,\mu_{i})
and applying Lemma
3.19
, we have

{\mathrm{sdet}}\,{}^{\mu}D_{i}(u-\mu_{(i-1)})=\psi_{\mu_{(i-1)}}({\mathrm{sdet}}\,{}^{\bar{\mu}}S_{1,1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(i-1)}}{2}))=\mathcal{C}_{\mu_{(i-1)}}(u)^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{\mu_{(i)}}(u).

Now the first statement easily follows from the above as
{\mathrm{sdet}}S(u)=\mathcal{C}_{N}(u)
.

It is known from
[
LWZ23
]
that
[d_{i}(u),d_{j}(v)]=0
for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N
. Thus, it follows from Proposition
3.17
that

{\mathrm{sdet}}\,{}^{\mu}D_{i}(u-\mu_{(i-1)})=\prod_{k=\mu_{(i-1)}+1}^{\mu_{(i)}}\mathsf{d}_{k}(u-k+1).\qed

Remark 3.21
.

Note that the first equality also holds for type AII with the same proof provided each part
\mu_{i}
in the composition
\mu
is even; cf.
[
MNO96
, §7.6]
. An analogue of the second equality for type AII can be deduced from
[
Mol07
, Thm. 4.1.7]
.

4.
Parabolic presentations of twisted Yangians

In this section, we formulate the parabolic presentation of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
associated to an arbitrary (assuming even for AII) composition of
N
. We outline the proof of the main result for type AI, and postpone some lengthy 2-block and 3-block verification to the subsequent Sections
5
and
6
. The proof for type AII is similar, but some modifications are required which are discussed in Section
7
.

4.1.
The formulation

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
be any composition of
N
, for type AII we assume that each
\mu_{i}
is even. Recall from Corollary
3.11
and Remark
3.12
that we have a generating set
\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)},B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}
for the twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
be a composition of
N
, where for type AII we assume that each
\mu_{i}
is even. The twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is generated by elements

\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i,j,\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 0},\qquad\{B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a+1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0}

subject to only the following relations,

(4.1)

\displaystyle H_{a;i,j}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij},\qquad H_{1;1,1}^{(2r-1)}+H_{1;1^{\prime},1^{\prime}}^{(2r-1)}=0,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{a}}\sum_{t=0}^{r}H_{a;i,p}^{(t)}\widetilde{H}_{a;p,j}^{(r-t)}

\displaystyle=\delta_{r0}\delta_{ij},

(4.3)

\displaystyle[H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},H_{b;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{ab}\Big(\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\big(H_{a;k,j}^{(r-1-t)}H_{a;i,l}^{(s+t)}-H_{a;k,j}^{(s+t)}H_{a;i,l}^{(r-1-t)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{t}\big(\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}H_{a;i,k^{\prime}}^{(r-1-t)}H_{a;j^{\prime},l}^{(s+t)}-\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}H_{a;k,i^{\prime}}^{(s+t)}H_{a;l^{\prime},j}^{(r-1-t)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor r/2\rfloor-1}\theta_{i}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\big(H_{a;k,i^{\prime}}^{(r-2-2t)}H_{a;j^{\prime},l}^{(s+2t)}-H_{a;k,i^{\prime}}^{(s+2t)}H_{a;j^{\prime},l}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)\Big),

(4.4)

\displaystyle[H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},B_{b;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{ab}\Big(\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{a}}\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}\sum_{t=0}^{m}(-1)^{m}\delta_{jl^{\prime}}\theta_{l^{\prime}}\theta_{p}{m\choose t}\Big(\frac{\mu_{a}}{2}\Big)^{m-t}H_{a;i,p}^{(r-m-1)}B_{a;k,p^{\prime}}^{(s+t)}

\displaystyle\qquad\penalty\ \penalty\ -\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{a}}\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}\sum_{t=0}^{m}(-1)^{m-t}\delta_{il}{m\choose t}\Big(\frac{\mu_{a}}{2}\Big)^{m-t}B_{a;k,p}^{(s+t)}H_{a;p,j}^{(r-m-1)}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\delta_{a,b+1}\Big(\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{m+1}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\theta_{k}H_{a;i,k^{\prime}}^{(r-1-m)}B_{b;j^{\prime},l}^{(s+m)}+\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}B_{b;i,l}^{(s+m)}H_{a;k,j}^{(r-1-m)}\Big),

(4.5)

\displaystyle[B_{a;i,j}^{(r)},B_{b;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\text{ if }|a-b|>1\text{ or if }b=a+1,\text{ and }i\neq l,

(4.6)

\displaystyle[B_{a;i,j}^{(r)},B_{a;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}B_{a;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{a;i,l}^{(t)}-\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}B_{a;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{a;i,l}^{(t)}-(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}Z_{a;j^{\prime},l,k,i^{\prime}}^{(r+s-1)},

(4.7)

\displaystyle[B_{a;i,j}^{(r+1)},B_{a+1;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=[B_{a;i,j}^{(r)},B_{a+1;k,l}^{(s+1)}]+\frac{\mu_{a+1}}{2}[B_{a;i,j}^{(r)},B_{a+1;k,l}^{(s)}]-\delta_{il}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{a+1}}B_{a;q,j}^{(r)}B_{a+1;k,q}^{(s)},

and the Serre relations

(4.8)

\displaystyle\big[[B_{a;i,j}^{(s)},B_{a+1;k,l}^{(r_{1})}],B_{a+1;f,g}^{(r_{2})}\big]+\big[[B_{a;i,j}^{(s)},B_{a+1;k,l}^{(r_{2})}],B_{a+1;f,g}^{(r_{1})}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\delta_{il}((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})\theta_{k}\theta_{l}[Z_{a+1;l^{\prime},g,f,k^{\prime}}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1)},B_{a;i,j}^{(s)}],\quad\text{ if }\mu_{a+1}>1,\\[5.69054pt]
((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})\sum\limits_{p=1}^{r_{1}+r_{2}-1}\sum\limits_{q=0}^{r_{1}+r_{2}-1-p}(-1)^{p-1}2^{-q}{p+q-1\choose q}B_{a;i,j}^{(p+s-1)}Z_{a+1;l,l,f,k}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1-p-q)},\penalty\ \text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\big[[B_{a+1;i,j}^{(s)},B_{a;k,l}^{(r_{1})}],B_{a;f,g}^{(r_{2})}\big]+\big[[B_{a+1;i,j}^{(s)},B_{a;k,l}^{(r_{2})}],B_{a;f,g}^{(r_{1})}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\delta_{jk}((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})\theta_{k}\theta_{l}[Z_{a;l^{\prime},g,f,k^{\prime}}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1)},B_{a+1;i,j}^{(s)}],\quad\text{ if }\mu_{a+1}>1,\\[5.69054pt]
((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})\sum\limits_{p=1}^{r_{1}+r_{2}-1}\sum\limits_{q=0}^{r_{1}+r_{2}-1-p}(-1)^{q}2^{-q}{p+q-1\choose q}B_{a+1;i,j}^{(p+s-1)}Z_{a;g,l,j,j}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1-p-q)},\penalty\ \text{ otherwise, }\end{cases}

for all possible admissible indices.
For
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
we follow the convention (
2.8
), while for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
we follow (
2.9
).
Here the elements
Z_{a;i,j,k,l}^{(r)}
are defined in (
3.28
). Moreover, in (
4.8
) for
\mu_{a+1}>1
, the left hand side does not depend on
i
and
l
but on
\delta_{il}
. Thus we assume further that
i\neq g
; see the proof of the relation in §
6.3
for further clarification. Similarly, we assume that
j\neq f
in (
4.9
).

Remark 4.2
.

In the case of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
, it follows by setting
i=k
,
j=l
, and
r=s
in (
4.6
) that

(4.10)

Z_{a;i,i,j,j}^{(2r-1)}=0,\qquad 1\leqslant a<n,\penalty\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},\penalty\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a+1},\penalty\ r\geqslant 1.

Remark 4.3
.

When
\mu=(1^{N})
, then the Serre relations (
4.8
) and (
4.9
) correspond to an analogue of
[
LWZ25
, (6.18)]
instead of
[
LWZ23
, (5.5)]
. A detailed proof of equivalence between the Serre relations here and in
[
LWZ23
]
can be found in
[
Lu25
, §2.4]
. Specifically, the component-wise formula (
4.8
) corresponds to the expansion of the RHS of
[
Lu25
, (2.25)]
while
[
LWZ25
, (6.18)]
corresponds to the RHS of
[
Lu25
, (2.26)]
.

The following reduced presentation is helpful, since it can be used to avoid the verification of the sophisticated Serre relations, cf.
[
LWZ25
, Thm. 4.14]
.

Theorem 4.4
.

The twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is generated by elements

\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i,j,\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 0},\qquad\{B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a+1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0}

subject to the relations (
4.1
)–(
4.7
) plus the finite type Serre relations:

(4.11)

\begin{split}\big[[B_{a+1;i,j}^{(1)},B_{a;k,l}^{(1)}],B_{a;f,g}^{(1)}\big]=-\delta_{jk}\delta_{g^{\prime}l}\theta_{f}\theta_{g}B_{a+1;i,f^{\prime}}^{(1)},\\
\big[[B_{a;i,j}^{(1)},B_{a+1;k,l}^{(1)}],B_{a+1;f,g}^{(1)}\big]=-\delta_{il}\delta_{f^{\prime}k}\theta_{f}\theta_{g}B_{a;g^{\prime},j}^{(1)}.\end{split}

Theorem
4.1
and Theorem
4.4
will be proved in the next subsection (modulo some lengthy 2-block and 3-block verification which can be found in Sections
5
–
6
).

4.2.
Proof of main theorems for type AI

In this subsection, we give detailed proofs of Theorem
4.1
and Theorem
4.4
for type AI. Additional details for the proofs of these theorems for type AII will appear in
Section
7
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
for type AI.

The most complicated situation in type AI is when some
\mu_{i}=1
, which does not occur in type AII.

Following the convention (
2.8
). We first show that the twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
has generators
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
B_{b;k,l}^{(r)}
and these generators satisfy the relations stated in the theorem. Define
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
B_{b;k,l}^{(r)}
, and
C_{a;l,k}^{(r)}
