(also the generating series) via Gauss decomposition as in (
3.23
)–(
3.26
) for
1\leqslant a\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant b<n
, and
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. By Corollary
3.11
, the elements
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
B_{b;k,l}^{(r)}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant b<n
,
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, generate the twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
.

We prove that
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
, and
B_{b;k,l}^{(r)}
satisfy the listed relations by induction on
n
. The proofs for the base cases for
n=2,3
are nontrivial and long; they will be presented in Sections
5
–
6
.

The condition
H_{1;1,1}^{(2r-1)}=0
follows from Lemma
2.3
as
H_{1;1,1}(u)=D_{1;1,1}(u)=s_{11}(u)
is even. Suppose now the relations hold for the twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\nu}^{+}
for all
\nu
of length
n
. We would like to prove it for the twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
with
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n+1})
of length
n+1
case. Set

\mu^{+}=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n}),\qquad\mu^{-}=(\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n+1}).

Recall that we have the natural embedding
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu^{+}}^{+}\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
,
s_{ij}(u)\to s_{ij}(u)
which sends
H_{a;i,j}(u)
,
\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}(u)
,
B_{j}(u)
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu^{+}}^{+}
to the series in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
of the same name. Note that we also have the homomorphism
\vartheta_{\mu_{1}}:{\mathscr{X}}_{\mu^{-}}^{+}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{\mu}^{+}
with the properties in Lemma
3.7
. Hence the above relations hold for the case when (
a\leqslant n
,
b<n
) or
a,b\geqslant 2
. It remains to show the relations for the case when (
a=1
and
b=n
) or (
a=n+1
and
b=1
). This follows immediately from Corollary
3.9
when
n\geqslant 3
.

Denote by
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
the algebra generated by the above generators and relations as in the statement of the theorem. The above argument implies that there is a surjective homomorphism

(4.12)

\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+},

which sends the generators
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
B_{b;k,l}^{(r)}
of
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
to the elements of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
denoted by the same symbols. Hence to complete the proof, we need to prove the homomorphism is injective. By Theorem
3.13
, the set of monomials in

\displaystyle H_{a;i,i}^{(2r)},\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},\quad r\geqslant 1,

\displaystyle H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},\quad r\geqslant 1,

\displaystyle B_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leqslant b<a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b},\quad r\geqslant 1,

taken in some fixed linear order is linearly independent in the twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
. Theorem
4.1
will follow if we can prove that the algebra
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
is spanned by the monomials in the elements above with the same symbols, taken in some fixed linear order.

For
1\leqslant b<a\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
, and
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}
, define elements
B_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}
of
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
inductively by

(4.13)

B_{b+1,b;i,j}^{(r)}:=B_{b;i,j}^{(r)},\quad B_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}:=[B_{a-1;i,k}^{(1)},B_{a-1,b;k,j}^{(r)}],

cf. Lemma
3.4
. It follows by induction that the definition is independent of the choice of
k
. Indeed, by (
4.5
) we have
[B_{a-1;i,k}^{(1)},B_{a-1,b;k^{\prime},j}^{(r)}]=0
if
k\neq k^{\prime}
. Bracketing with
H_{a-1;k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}
, it follows from (
4.4
) that

[B_{a-1;i,k}^{(1)},B_{a-1,b;k,j}^{(r)}]=[B_{a-1;i,k^{\prime}}^{(1)},B_{a-1,b;k^{\prime},j}^{(r)}].

Define a filtration on
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
by setting
\deg H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}=\deg B_{b;k,l}^{(r)}=r-1
. Let
\operatorname{gr}\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
be the associated graded algebra. Let
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}
and
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;k,l}^{(r)}
be the images of
H_{a;i,j}^{(r+1)}
and
B_{a,b;,k,l}^{(r+1)}
, respectively, in the
r
-th component of the graded algebra
\operatorname{gr}\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
.

We prepare several useful relations in the associated graded algebra
\operatorname{gr}\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
. The relation (
4.5
) implies that for
|a-c|>1
, we have

(4.14)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;k,l}^{(s)}]=0.

Due to (
4.6
) and (
3.28
), we have

(4.15)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;k,l}^{(s)}]=(-1)^{r}(\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a+1;k,i}^{(r+s)}-\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j,l}^{(r+s)}).

If
|a-c|=1
, then using (
4.7
) we obtain that

(4.16)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(r+1)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;k,l}^{(s)}]=[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;k,l}^{(s+1)}].

If
a-b>1
, then we prove that

(4.17)

{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}=[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;i,k}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,b;k,j}^{(r)}]=[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b+1;i,l}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b+1,b;l,j}^{(0)}],

where the first equality holds by definition (
4.13
). Indeed, for the second equality, we argue by induction on
a-b
with the following computation for the inductive step:

\displaystyle{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,b;i,j}^{(r)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN0}}}{{=}}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,k}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;k,j}^{(r)}]=[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,k}^{(0)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b+1;k,l}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b+1,b;l,j}^{(0)}]]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN4}}}{{=}}[[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,k}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b+1;k,l}^{(r)}],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b+1,b;l,j}^{(0)}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN0}}}{{=}}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,b+1;i,l}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b+1,b;l,j}^{(0)}].

Repeating this argument with the help of (
4.17
), we prove that for
a-b>1
and
r,s\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(4.18)

{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r+s)}=[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;i,k}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,b;k,j}^{(s)}]

with any
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{a-1}
.

We first show that
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j,l}^{(r)}=(-1)^{r+1}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;l,j}^{(r)}
. We prove it by induction on
a
. For the base case
a=1
, setting
i=k=1
and
s=0
in (
4.3
), we find that

(4.19)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,l}^{(0)}]=\delta_{1j}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,l}^{(r)}-\delta_{1l}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,j}^{(r)}-(-1)^{r}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j,l}^{(r)}+(-1)^{r}\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,1}^{(r)}.

Switching
r\leftrightarrow 0
and
j\leftrightarrow l
, we obtain

(4.20)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,l}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,j}^{(r)}]=\delta_{1l}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,j}^{(r)}-\delta_{1j}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,l}^{(r)}-{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;l,j}^{(r)}+\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,1}^{(r)}.

Note that the relation (
4.1
) is equivalent to
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,1}^{(r)}=(-1)^{r+1}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,1}^{(r)}
. Comparing (
4.19
) and (
4.20
), we conclude that
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j,l}^{(r)}=(-1)^{r+1}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;l,j}^{(r)}
. Thus, we have proved it for the case
a=1
. For the general case, note that
Z_{a;i,i,j,j}^{(2r-1)}=0
implies that
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a+1;1,1}^{(2r)}={\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;1,1}^{(2r)}
. Hence for induction step, one only needs to repeat the above calculations.

For
1\leqslant b<a\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{a}
, and
1\leqslant l\leqslant\mu_{b}
, define
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,a;l,k}^{(r)}:=(-1)^{r+1}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;k,l}^{(r)}
. Note that this relation is consistent with the case
a=b
, i.e.
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}=(-1)^{r+1}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j,i}^{(r)}
.

We claim that the following relations hold:

(4.21)

\displaystyle[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c,d;k,l}^{(s)}]=\delta_{bc}\delta_{jk}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,d;i,l}^{(r+s)}-\delta_{ad}\delta_{il}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c,b;k,j}^{(r+s)}-(-1)^{r}\delta_{ac}\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,d;j,l}^{(r+s)}+(-1)^{r}\delta_{bd}\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c,a;k,i}^{(r+s)}.

By the convention
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,a;l,k}^{(r)}:=(-1)^{r+1}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;k,l}^{(r)}
, it suffices to establish (
4.21
) for the case when
a\geqslant b
and
c\geqslant d
; the remainder of this proof is devoted to this task.

We first consider the case
a=b
or
c=d
. Assume that
a=b
. If
c=d
, then (
4.21
) follows immediately from (
4.3
). If
c>d
, then one first proves it for the particular case
c-d=1
using (
4.4
). For the general case, it suffices to use an induction on
c-d
, (
4.13
), and (
4.18
) along with Jacobi identity; see e.g. the proof of
[
LWZ23
, Thm. 5.1]
. Now it remains to prove (
4.21
) for the cases when
a>b
and
c>d
.

If
c=a+1
, then we get from (
4.9
), (
3.28
), and (
4.21
) for the case
a=b
that

(4.22)

\begin{split}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(r)},&[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;k,l}^{(s)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;f,g}^{(p)}]]\\
=-&[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(s)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;k,l}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;f,g}^{(p)}]]-((-1)^{r}+(-1)^{s})\delta_{kg}\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;f,i}^{(r+s+p)}.\end{split}

Similarly, if
c=a-1
, then we get

(4.23)

\begin{split}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(r)},&[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;k,l}^{(s)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;f,g}^{(p)}]]\\
=-&[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(s)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a;k,l}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;f,g}^{(p)}]]-((-1)^{r}+(-1)^{s})\delta_{ik}\delta_{lf}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c+1,c;j,g}^{(r+s+p)}.\end{split}

We shall verify (
4.21
) case by case, considering all relations between pairs of indices
a>b
and
c>d
. There will be seven cases.

(1) If
b>c
, then (
4.21
) is straightforward by (
4.14
) and (
4.17
).

(2) If
b=c
, then using (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) we find that

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b+1,b;i_{1},j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,b-1;k,l_{1}}^{(s)}]=\delta_{jk}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b+1,b-1;i_{1},l_{1}}^{(r+s)}.

Taking the commutator with
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b+2,b+1;i_{2},i_{1}}^{(0)},\ldots,{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;i,i_{a-b-1}}^{(0)}
and using (
4.14
), we obtain

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,b-1;k,l_{1}}^{(s)}]=\delta_{jk}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b-1;i,l_{1}}^{(r+s)}

Similarly, apply the commutator with
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b-1,b-2;l_{1},l_{2}}^{(0)},\ldots,{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{d+1,d;l_{b-d-1},l}^{(0)}
to obtain (
4.21
) for
b=c
.

(3) Now we prove (
4.21
) with
a=c
and
b>d
. Let us first consider the commutator
[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+3,a+1;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+2,a;k,l}^{(s)}]
. It suffices to do the calculation for the case
a=1
as the same argument works for all
a
. Clearly, it follows from (
4.14
), (
4.22
), and (
4.23
) that

(4.24)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;i,k}^{(r)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(s)}]]]=[[[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;i,k}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)}],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)}],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(s)}]=0.

We have

\displaystyle[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{42;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{31;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN8}}}{{=}}[[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;i,k}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)}],[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(s)}]]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN9}}}{{=}}[[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;i,k}^{(r)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(s)}]],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN4}}}{{=}}[[[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;i,k}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)}],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(s)}],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN9}}}{{=}}[[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;i,k}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)}],[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(s)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)}]]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN8}}}{{=}}-[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{42;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{31;k,l}^{(s)}].

Therefore,

(4.25)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+3,a+1;\tilde{i},\tilde{j}}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+2,a;\tilde{k},\tilde{l}}^{(s)}]=[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{42:i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{31;k,l}^{(s)}]=0.

Then we look at a particular case,

\displaystyle[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{31;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN0}}}{{=}}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;i,j}^{(r)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(s)}]]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN7.5}}}{{=}}-[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;i,j}^{(0)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(s)}]]-(1+(-1)^{r})\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(r+s)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN6}}}{{=}}-[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;i,j}^{(0)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;k,j}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(r+s)}]]-(1+(-1)^{r})\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(r+s)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN7.5}}}{{=}}\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(r+s)}-(1+(-1)^{r})\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(r+s)}=(-1)^{r+1}\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(r+s)}.

This implies further that

\displaystyle[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{41;g,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN0}}}{{=}}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;i,j}^{(r)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;g,k}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{31;k,l}^{(s)}]]

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ =\penalty\ \penalty\ [[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;g,k}^{(0)}],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{31;k,l}^{(s)}]-[[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{32;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{31;k,l}^{(s)}],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;g,k}^{(0)}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN0}}}{{=}}-\delta_{ik}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{42;g,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{31;k,l}^{(s)}]-[(-1)^{r+1}\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{21;j,l}^{(r+s)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{43;g,k}^{(0)}]\overset{\eqref{pfN4}}{\underset{\eqref{pfN10}}{=}}0-0=0.

Similarly, we have
[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{d+2,d-1;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{d+1,d;k,l}^{(s)}]=0
. Applying (
4.17
) to it, we get

(4.26)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{d+1,d;k,l}^{(s)}]=0

for any
a>d+1
and
b<d
.

Finally, to show (
4.21
) for the case
a=c
and
b>d
, namely

(4.27)

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,d;k,l}^{(s)}]=(-1)^{r+1}\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,d;j,l}^{(r+s)},

it essentially reduces to verifying for the case
[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-2;k,l}^{(s)}]
by using (
4.17
) and (
4.26
). This case is the same for all
a
, and for
a=3
it has been done above.

(4) The case
a>c
and
b=d
is similar the case (3). Again, the application of (
4.17
) and (
4.26
) reduces the calculation to prove that
[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-2;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,a-2;k,l}^{(s)}]=(-1)^{s+1}\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;i,k}^{(r+s)}
.

(5) For the case
a=c
and
b=d
, we show by induction on
a-b
that

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;k,l}^{(s)}]=(-1)^{r}(\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;k,i}^{(r+s)}-\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,b;j,l}^{(r+s)}).

The base case
a-b=1
is known as (
4.15
). By the induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN0}}}{{=}}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;k,f}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,b;f,l}^{(s)}]]

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ =\penalty\ \penalty\ [[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;k,f}^{(0)}],{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,b;f,l}^{(s)}]+[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;k,f}^{(0)},[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,b;f,l}^{(s)}]]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pfN12}}}{{=}}[\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,b;f,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,b;f,l}^{(s)}]+[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;k,f}^{(0)},(-1)^{(s+1)}\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a-1;i,f}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ =\penalty\ \penalty\ (-1)^{r}\delta_{ik}(\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,a-1;f,f}^{(r+s)}-{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,b;j,l}^{(r+s)})+(-1)^{s+1}\delta_{jl}({\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,k}^{(r+s)}-\delta_{ik}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a-1,a-1;f,f}^{(r+s)})

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ =\penalty\ \penalty\ (-1)^{r}(\delta_{jl}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;k,i}^{(r+s)}-\delta_{i,k}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,b;j,l}^{(r+s)}).

(6) If
a>c>b>d
, then due to (
4.17
) and (
4.26
), it suffices to verify that

[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+3,a+1;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+2,a;k,l}^{(s)}]=0

which is done in (
4.25
).

(7) If
a>c>d>b
, then similarly it is reduced to (
4.26
).

Thus, we have completed the verification of the relations (
4.21
).

Therefore we have shown that the associated graded algebra
\operatorname{gr}\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}
is spanned by the set of monomials in the elements
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}
,
1\leqslant b<a\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}
,
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
1\leqslant a\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a}
, and
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,i}^{(2r+1)}
,
1\leqslant a\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
taken in some fixed linear order. This proves the injectivity of the homomorphism (
4.12
) and completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4
.

The proof for type AI is almost the same as that of Theorem
4.1
. The main difference is that one needs to show that (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) hold for general
r,s,p
assuming that (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) hold for
r=s=p=0
(corresponding to (
4.11
)) along with other relations whose proof does not involve Serre relations. This can be proved by bracketing
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,i}^{(2q-1)}
to (
4.22
) and (
4.23
) for
r=s=p=0
. The strategy is very similar to the proof of Lemma
6.7
; see also
[
LWZ25
, Prop. 2.9]
. We omit the details.

The proof for type AII is similar with suitable modifications by bracketing
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(q)}
to the image of (
4.11
) in the associated graded, see §
7.4
for more detail.
∎

5.
Relations in
2
-block parabolic presentations

In this section, we verify the
2
-block relations among the parabolic generators for twisted Yangians of type AI. This is one of the main steps in the proof of the parabolic presentation of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
formulated in Theorem
4.1
, following the convention (
2.8
).
In this section, we
set
n=2
and
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
.

5.1.
A recap for
n=2

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
be a strict composition of
N
. The parabolic generators are given as follows:

\displaystyle D_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{D}_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\qquad

\displaystyle a=1,2,\ 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a},\ r\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle E_{1;i,j}^{(r)},F_{1;j,i}^{(r)},\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{1},\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{2},\ r>0.

Comparing both sides of the matrix equation (
3.2
) and its inverse, we have

(5.1)

\displaystyle s_{ij}(u)=D_{1;i,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},

(5.2)

\displaystyle s_{i,\mu_{1}+j}(u)=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1;p,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{2},

(5.3)

\displaystyle s_{\mu_{1}+i,j}(u)=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}F_{1;i,p}(u)D_{1;p,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{2},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{1},

(5.4)

\displaystyle s_{\mu_{1}+i,\mu_{1}+j}(u)=D_{2;i,j}(u)+\sum_{p,r=1}^{\mu_{1}}F_{1;i,p}(u)D_{1;p,r}(u)E_{1;r,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{2},

(5.5)

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{ij}(u)=\widetilde{D}_{1;i,j}(u)+\sum_{q,t=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,t}(u)F_{1;t,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},

(5.6)

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{i,\mu_{1}+j}(u)=-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{2},

(5.7)

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+i,j}(u)=-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;i,q}(u)F_{1;q,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{2},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{1},

(5.8)

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+i,\mu_{1}+j}(u)=\widetilde{D}_{2;i,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{2}.

5.2.
Relations among
D,E,F
generators

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
be a strict composition of
N
. Then the following relations hold in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
:

\displaystyle[D_{a;i,j}(u),D_{b;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\delta_{ab}\Big(\frac{1}{u-v}\big(D_{a;k,j}(u)D_{a;i,l}(v)-D_{a;k,j}(v)D_{a;i,l}(u)\big)

(5.9)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{u+v+\mu_{(a-1)}}\big(D_{a;i,k}(u)D_{a;j,l}(v)-D_{a;k,i}(v)D_{a;l,j}(u)\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(u-v)(u+v+\mu_{(a-1)})}\big(D_{a;k,i}(u)D_{a;j,l}(v)-D_{a;k,i}(v)D_{a;j,l}(u)\big)\Big),

(5.10)

\displaystyle[D_{1;i,j}(u),E_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{jk}}{u-v}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-E_{1;p,l}(u)\big)

\displaystyle\,+\frac{\delta_{ik}}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(F_{1;l,p}(u)-E_{1;p,l}(v)\big)D_{1;p,j}(u),

(5.11)

\displaystyle[D_{1;i,j}(u),F_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{il}}{u-v}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(F_{1;k,p}(u)-F_{1;k,p}(v)\big)D_{1;p,j}(u)

\displaystyle\,+\frac{\delta_{jl}}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\big(F_{1;k,p}(v)-E_{1;p,k}(u)\big),

(5.12)

\displaystyle[D_{2;i,j}(u),E_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{u-v}D_{2;i,l}(u)\big(E_{1;k,j}(u)-E_{1;k,j}(v)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\big(E_{1;k,i}(v)-F_{1;i,k}(u)\big)D_{2;l,j}(u),

(5.13)

\displaystyle[E_{1;i,j}(u),F_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{u-v}\big(\widetilde{D}_{1;i,l}(u)D_{2;k,j}(u)-\widetilde{D}_{1;i,l}(v)D_{2;k,j}(v)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\big(E_{1;i,k}(u)-F_{1;k,i}(v)\big)\big(E_{1;l,j}(u)-F_{1;j,l}(v)\big),

(5.14)

\displaystyle[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{u-v}\big(E_{1;i,l}(u)-E_{1;i,l}(v)\big)\big(E_{1;k,j}(u)-E_{1;k,j}(v)\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\big(\widetilde{D}_{1;i,k}(u)D_{2;l,j}(u)-\widetilde{D}_{1;i,k}(-v-\mu_{1})D_{2;l,j}(-v-\mu_{1})\big),

(5.15)

\displaystyle F_{1;j,i}(u)=

\displaystyle\,E_{1;i,j}(-u-\mu_{1}).\hskip 199.16928pt

Proof.

Equations (
5.9
)
. If
a=b=1
, it is directly from (
2.12
). If
a=b=2
, then it follows from the case
a=b=1
by applying the shift homomorphism
\psi_{\mu_{1}}
. If
a=1
and
b=2
, then
D_{1;i,j}(u)=s_{i,j}(u)
and
\widetilde{D}_{2;k,l}(v)=\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+k,\mu_{1}+l}(v)
. By (
3.21
), we have
[D_{1;i,j}(u),\widetilde{D}_{2;k,l}(v)]=0
for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1}
and
1\leqslant k,l\leqslant\mu_{2}
. Thus we also have
[D_{1;i,j}(u),D_{2;k,l}(v)]=0
for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1}
and
1\leqslant k,l\leqslant\mu_{2}
.

Equations (
5.10
) and (
5.11
)
.
By (
3.21
), we have

\displaystyle(u^{2}-v^{2})[s_{ij}(u),

\displaystyle\,\tilde{s}_{k,\mu_{1}+l}(v)]=\delta_{jk}(u+v)\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{ia}(u)\tilde{s}_{a,\mu_{1}+l}(v)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\delta_{ik}(u-v)\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{aj}(u)\tilde{s}_{a,\mu_{1}+l}(v)+\delta_{ik}\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{ja}(u)\tilde{s}_{a,\mu_{1}+l}(v).

Transforming it in terms of parabolic generators, we have

\displaystyle(u^{2}-v^{2})

\displaystyle\big[D_{1;i,j}(u),\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;k,q}(v)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)\big]

\displaystyle=\delta_{kj}(u+v)\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\big(E_{1;p,q}(v)-E_{1;p,q}(u)\big)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)

\displaystyle\ -\delta_{ik}(u-v)\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(D_{1;p,j}(u)E_{1;p,q}(v)-F_{1;q,p}(u)D_{1;p,j}(u)\big)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)

\displaystyle\ +\delta_{ik}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}D_{1;p,j}(u)(E_{1;p,q}(v)-E_{1;p,q}(u)\big)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v).

Therefore, using the fact that
[D_{1;i,j}(u),\tilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)]=0
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\big[D_{1;i,j}(u),E_{1;k,l}(v)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{kj}}{u-v}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-E_{1;p,l}(u)\big)

(5.16)

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta_{ik}}{u+v}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\Big(\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-F_{1;l,p}(u)\big)D_{1;p,j}(u)+[D_{1;p,j}(u),E_{1;p,l}(v)]\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta_{ik}}{u^{2}-v^{2}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;j,p}(u)\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-E_{1;p,l}(u)\big).

Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big[D_{1;p,j}(u),E_{1;p,l}(v)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u-v}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;j,p}(u)\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-E_{1;p,l}(u)\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{\mu_{1}}{u+v}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\Big(\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-F_{1;l,p}(u)\big)D_{1;p,j}(u)+[D_{1;p,j}(u),E_{1;p,l}(v)]\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu_{1}}{u^{2}-v^{2}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;j,p}(u)\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-E_{1;p,l}(u)\big).

Solving for
\sum\limits_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big[D_{1;p,j}(u),E_{1;p,l}(v)\big]
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big[D_{1;p,j}(u),E_{1;p,l}(v)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u-v}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;j,p}(u)\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-E_{1;p,l}(u)\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{\mu_{1}}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(E_{1;p,l}(v)-F_{1;l,p}(u)\big)D_{1;p,j}(u).

Plugging this into (
5.16
) and simplifying, one obtains (
5.10
). The relation (
5.11
) follows from (
5.10
) by applying the anti-automorphism
\tau
.

Equation (
5.12
)
. It follows from (
5.11
) that

(5.17)

\begin{split}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big[D_{1;i,p}(u),F_{1;q,p}(v)\big]&=\frac{1}{u-v}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(F_{1;q,p}(u)-F_{1;q,p}(v)\big)D_{1;p,i}(u)\\
&+\frac{\mu_{1}}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\big(F_{1;q,p}(v)-E_{1;p,q}(u)\big).\end{split}

By (
3.21
), we have

\displaystyle(u^{2}-v^{2})[s_{i,\mu_{1}+j}(u),

\displaystyle\,\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+k,\mu_{1}+l}(v)]=\delta_{jk}(u+v)\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{ia}(u)\tilde{s}_{a,\mu_{1}+l}(v)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\delta_{jl}(u-v)\sum_{a=1}^{N}\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+k,a}(v)s_{ia}(u)-\delta_{jl}\sum_{a=1}^{N}\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+k,a}(v)s_{ai}(u).

Rewriting it using parabolic generators, we get

\displaystyle(u^{2}-v^{2})

\displaystyle\Big[\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1;p,j}(u),\widetilde{D}_{2;k,l}(v)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\delta_{jk}(u+v)\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}D_{1;i,p}(u)(-E_{1;p,q}(v)+E_{1;p,q}(u))\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\delta_{jl}(u-v)\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)(-\boxed{F_{1;q,p}(v)D_{1;i,p}(u)}+D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1;p,q}(u))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\,\delta_{jl}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)(F_{1;q,p}(v)-F_{1;q,p}(u))D_{1;p,i}(u).

Commuting
F_{1;q,p}(v)
and
D_{1;i,p}(u)
in the above box via (
5.17
) and using
[D_{1;i,j}(u),\tilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)]=0
, we find that

(5.18)

\begin{split}\big[E_{1;i,j}(u),\widetilde{D}_{2;k,l}(v)\big]&=\frac{\delta_{jk}}{u-v}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}(E_{1;i,q}(u)-E_{1;i,q}(v))\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)\\
&+\frac{\delta_{jl}}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)(E_{1;i,q}(u)-F_{1;q,i}(v)).\end{split}

Then one easily deduces (
5.12
) from (
5.18
).

Equation (
5.13
)
. We start with the relation from (
2.12
),

\displaystyle(u-v)[s_{i,\mu_{1}+j}(u),\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+k,l}(v)]=\delta_{jk}\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{ia}(u)\tilde{s}_{al}(v)-\delta_{il}\sum_{a=1}^{N}\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+k,a}(v)s_{a,\mu_{1}+j}(u),

which gives

\displaystyle\,(u-v)\Big[\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1;p,j}(u),-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)F_{1;q,l}(v)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\delta_{jk}\Big(\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\widetilde{D}_{1;p,l}(v)+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q,t=1}^{\mu_{2}}D_{1;i,p}(u)(E_{1;p,t}(v)-E_{1;p,t}(u))\widetilde{D}_{2;t,q}(v)F_{1;q,l}(v)\Big)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\,\delta_{il}\Big(\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)D_{2;q,j}(u)+\sum_{p,r=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)(F_{1;q,r}(u)-F_{1;q,r}(v))D_{1;r,p}(u)E_{1;p,j}(u)\Big).

We further rewrite it as

\displaystyle\,\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)\Big(\boxed{(u-v)F_{1;q,l}(v)D_{1;i,p}(u)-\delta_{il}\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(F_{1;q,r}(v)-F_{1;q,r}(u)\big)D_{1;r,p}(u)}\Big)E_{1;p,j}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\Big(\boxed{(u-v)E_{1;p,j}(u)\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)+\delta_{jk}\sum_{t=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(E_{1;p,t}(v)-E_{1;p,t}(u)\big)\widetilde{D}_{2;t,q}(v)}\Big)F_{1;q,l}(v)

\displaystyle\hskip 193.47882pt+\delta_{jk}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\widetilde{D}_{1;p,l}(v)-\delta_{il}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)D_{2;q,j}(u).

Using (
5.11
) and (
5.18
) to rewrite the formulas in boxes, we have

\displaystyle(u-v)\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)D_{1;i,p}(u)\Big(F_{1;q,l}(v)E_{1;p,j}(u)-\frac{1}{u+v+\mu_{1}}(F_{1;q,p}(v)-E_{1;p,q}(u))E_{1;l,j}(u)\Big)

\displaystyle=(u-v)\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)\Big(E_{1;p,j}(u)F_{1;q,l}(v)+\frac{1}{u+v+\mu_{1}}(E_{1;p,q}(u)-F_{1;q,p}(v))F_{1;j,l}(v)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 204.85974pt+\delta_{jk}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\widetilde{D}_{1;p,l}(v)-\delta_{il}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)D_{2;q,j}(u).

Multiplying it from left by
D_{2;\alpha,k}(v)\widetilde{D}_{1;\beta,i}(u)
and summing over
i=1,\ldots,\mu_{1}
and
k=1,\dots,\mu_{2}
, one finds that

\displaystyle(u-v)F_{1;\alpha,l}(v)E_{1;\beta,j}(u)-\frac{u-v}{u+v+\mu_{1}}(F_{1;\alpha,\beta}(v)-E_{1;\beta,\alpha}(u))E_{1;l,j}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(u-v)E_{1;\beta,j}(u)F_{1;\alpha,l}(v)-\frac{u-v}{u+v+\mu_{1}}(F_{1;\alpha,\beta}(v)-E_{1;\beta,\alpha}(u))F_{1;j,l}(v)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\widetilde{D}_{1;\beta,l}(v)D_{2;\alpha,j}(v)-\widetilde{D}_{1;\beta,l}(u)D_{2;\alpha,j}(u),

completing the proof of (
5.13
).

Equations (
5.14
) and (
5.15
)
. Multiplying (
5.10
) by
u+v+\mu_{1}
and setting
v=-u-\mu_{1}
, one finds that

\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(F_{1;l,p}(u)-E_{1;p,l}(-u-\mu_{1})\big)D_{1;p,j}(u)=0,

which further implies (
5.15
). Using (
5.13
) and (
5.15
), one obtains (
5.14
).
∎

The relations (
5.9
)–(
5.12
) and (
5.15
) appeared in
[
Br16
]
for the special case
N=3
and
\mu=(2,1)
.

Remark 5.2
.

Interchanging
u
and
v
in (
5.14
) and relabeling the subscripts, we have

(5.19)

\begin{split}[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{1;k,l}(v)]&=\frac{1}{u-v}\big(E_{1;k,j}(u)-E_{1;k,j}(v)\big)\big(E_{1;i,l}(u)-E_{1;i,l}(v)\big)\\
&+\frac{1}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\big(\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}(-u-\mu_{1})D_{2;j,l}(-u-\mu_{1})-\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}(v)D_{2;j,l}(v)\big).\end{split}

Setting
u=v
in (
5.14
) and (
5.19
), we have

(5.20)

\begin{split}\widetilde{D}_{1;i,k}(u)D_{2;l,j}(u)-&\,\widetilde{D}_{1;i,k}(-u-\mu_{1})D_{2;l,j}(-u-\mu_{1})\\
=&\,\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}(-u-\mu_{1})D_{2;j,l}(-u-\mu_{1})-\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}(u)D_{2;j,l}(u),\end{split}

and

(5.21)

[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{1;k,l}(u)]=\frac{1}{2u+\mu_{1}}\big(\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}(-u-\mu_{1})D_{2;j,l}(-u-\mu_{1})-\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}(u)D_{2;j,l}(u)\big).

On the other hand, it follows from (
5.14
) and (
5.19
), respectively,

(5.22)

\begin{split}[E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},E_{1;k,l}(v)]&=E_{1;i,l}(v)E_{1;k,j}(v)-\widetilde{D}_{1;i,k}(-v-\mu_{1})D_{2;l,j}(-v-\mu_{1})+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl},\\
[E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},E_{1;k,l}(v)]&=E_{1;k,j}(v)E_{1;i,l}(v)-\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}(v)D_{2;j,l}(v)+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}.\end{split}

Thus, we conclude that

(5.23)

[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{1;k,l}(u)]=\widetilde{D}_{1;i,k}(-u-\mu_{1})D_{2;j,l}(-u-\mu_{1})-\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}(u)D_{2;l,j}(u).

5.3.
Relations among
H,B
generators

The following results are direct consequences of Proposition
5.1
together with (
3.23
)–(
3.27
).

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
be a strict composition of
N
. Then the following relations hold in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
:

\displaystyle[H_{a;i,j}(u),H_{b;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\delta_{ab}\Big(\frac{1}{u-v}\big(H_{a;k,j}(u)H_{a;i,l}(v)-H_{a;k,j}(v)H_{a;i,l}(u)\big)

(5.24)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{u+v}\big(H_{a;i,k}(u)H_{a;j,l}(v)-H_{a;k,i}(v)H_{a;l,j}(u)\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{u^{2}-v^{2}}\big(H_{a;k,i}(u)H_{a;j,l}(v)-H_{a;k,i}(v)H_{a;j,l}(u)\big)\Big),

(5.25)

\displaystyle[H_{1;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{il}}{u-v+\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(B_{1;k,p}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})-B_{1;k,p}(v)\big)H_{1;p,j}(u)

\displaystyle\,+\frac{\delta_{jl}}{u+v+\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}H_{1;i,p}(u)\big(B_{1;k,p}(v)-B_{1;k,p}(-u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})\big),

(5.26)

\displaystyle[H_{2;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{u+v}H_{2;i,k}(u)\big(B_{1;j,l}(-u)-B_{1;j,l}(v)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{u-v}\big(B_{1;i,l}(v)-B_{1;i,l}(u)\big)H_{2;k,j}(u),

(5.27)

\displaystyle[B_{1;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{u-v}\big(B_{1;k,j}(v)-B_{1;k,j}(u)\big)\big(B_{1;i,l}(u)-B_{1;i,l}(v)\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{u+v}\big(Z_{1;j,l,k,i}(v)-Z_{1;j,l,k,i}(-u)\big),

(5.28)

\displaystyle B_{1;i,j}(-u)=

\displaystyle\,C_{1;j,i}(u),\hskip 199.16928pt

where
Z_{1;j,l,k,i}(u)=\widetilde{H}_{1;j,l}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})H_{2;k,i}(u)
is defined in (
3.27
).

Remark 5.4
.

Using the same approach toward proving (
5.19
), one can deduce the equivalent form of (
5.27
):

(5.29)

[B_{1;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(v)]=\frac{1}{u-v}\big(B_{1;i,l}(u)-B_{1;i,l}(v)\big)\big(B_{1;k,j}(v)-B_{1;k,j}(u)\big)\\
+\frac{1}{u+v}\big(Z_{1;l,j,i,k}(-v)-Z_{1;l,j,i,k}(u)\big).

Proposition 5.5
.

The following relations hold in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
:

(5.30)

\displaystyle[H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},H_{b;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{ab}\Big(\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\big(H_{a;k,j}^{(r-1-t)}H_{a;i,l}^{(s+t)}-H_{a;k,j}^{(s+t)}H_{a;j,l}^{(r-1-t)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{t}\big(H_{a;i,k}^{(r-1-t)}H_{a;j,l}^{(s+t)}-H_{a;k,i}^{(s+t)}H_{a;l,j}^{(r-1-t)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor r/2\rfloor-1}\big(H_{a;k,i}^{(r-2-2t)}H_{a;j,l}^{(s+2t)}-H_{a;k,i}^{(s+2t)}H_{a;j,l}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)\Big),

(5.31)

\displaystyle[H_{1;i,j}^{(r)},B_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}\sum_{t=0}^{m}(-1)^{m}\delta_{jl}{m\choose t}\Big(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Big)^{m-t}H_{1;i,p}^{(r-m-1)}B_{1;k,p}^{(s+t)},

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}\sum_{t=0}^{m}(-1)^{m-t}\delta_{il}{m\choose t}\Big(\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}\Big)^{m-t}B_{1;k,p}^{(s+t)}H_{1;p,j}^{(r-m-1)},

(5.32)

\displaystyle[H_{2;i,j}^{(r)},B_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{m+1}H_{2;i,k}^{(r-1-m)}B_{1;j,l}^{(s+m)}+\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}B_{1;i,l}^{(s+m)}H_{2;k,j}^{(r-1-m)},

(5.33)

\displaystyle[B_{1;i,j}^{(r)},B_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}-\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}-(-1)^{r}Z_{1;j,l,k,i}^{(r+s-1)}

(5.34)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}-\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}+(-1)^{s}Z_{1;l,j;i,k}^{(r+s-1)}.

Proof.

The relations (
5.30
), (
5.31
) and (
5.32
) follow from (
5.24
), (
5.25
) and (
5.26
), respectively.
To show (
5.33
), we collect and compare the coefficient of
u^{-r}v^{-s}
on both sides of (
5.27
).
Use the following expansion for the right-hand-side,

(5.35)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{u-v}=\sum_{a\geqslant 0}u^{-1-a}v^{a},\qquad\frac{1}{u+v}=\sum_{a\geqslant 0}(-1)^{a}u^{-1-a}v^{a}.

Since the terms

B_{1;k,j}(u)B_{1;i,l}(u)
and
Z_{1;j,l,k,i}(-u)
contribute nothing, we may ignore them. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle[B_{1;i,j}^{(r)},B_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}B_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}B_{1;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}B_{1;k,j}^{(r-1-a)}B_{1;i,l}^{(s+a)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{x,y\geqslant 1}^{x+y=s+r-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(x)}B_{1;i,l}^{(y)}+(-1)^{r-1}Z_{1;j,l;k,i}^{(r+s-1)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}+\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(r+s-1-t)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{t=1}^{r+s-2}B_{1;k,j}^{(t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(r+s-1-t)}-(-1)^{r}Z_{1;j,l;k,i}^{(r+s-1)}

\displaystyle=\boxed{\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}-\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}}-(-1)^{r}Z_{1;j,l;k,i}^{(r+s-1)},

which is (
5.33
). Starting from (
5.29
) with exactly the same argument, one deduces (
5.34
).
∎

Note that the boxed part above can be rewritten as

\sum_{t=\min\{r,s\}}^{r-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}-\sum_{t=\min\{r,s\}}^{s-1}B_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}B_{1;i,l}^{(t)}

and hence does not contain the
B_{1;k,j}^{(t)}
for small
t
. This observation will be used to define the shifted twisted Yangian in §
8.1
.

6.
The
3
-block parabolic presentations

In this section, we establish the
3
-block relations among parabolic generators for twisted Yangians of type AI. This completes the proof of the parabolic presentation of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
formulated in Theorem
4.1
, following the convention (
2.8
).
In this section, we set
n=3
and
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3})
.

6.1.
The case for
n=3

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3})
be a strict composition of
N
.
The parabolic generators associated to
\mu
are given as follows,

\displaystyle D_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{D}_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\qquad\ \ a=1,2,3,\ 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a},\ r\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle E_{a;i,j}^{(r)},F_{a;j,i}^{(r)},\qquad\ \ \,a=1,2,\ \ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a+1},\ r>0.

Comparing both sides of the matrix equation (
3.2
) and its inverse, we have

\displaystyle s_{ij}(u)=D_{1;i,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},

\displaystyle s_{i,\mu_{1}+j}(u)=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1;p,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{2},

\displaystyle s_{i,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}(u)=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1,3;p,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{3},

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{i,\mu_{1}+j}(u)=-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,j}(u)-\sum_{r,t=1}^{\mu_{3}}\widetilde{E}_{1,3;i,r}(u)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,t}(u)F_{2;t,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{2},

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{i,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}(u)=\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\widetilde{E}_{1,3;i,r}(u)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{3},

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+i,\mu_{1}+j}(u)=\widetilde{D}_{2;i,j}(u)+\sum_{r,t=1}^{\mu_{3}}E_{2;i,r}(u)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,t}(u)F_{2;t,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{2},

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+i,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}(u)=-\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}E_{2;i,r}(u)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{2},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{3},

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+i,\mu_{1}+j}(u)=-\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\widetilde{D}_{3;i,r}(u)F_{2;r,j}(u),

\displaystyle 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{3},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{2},

where
\widetilde{E}_{1,3;i,r}(u)=\sum\limits_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)E_{2;q,r}(u)-E_{1,3;i,r}(u)
.

Lemma 6.1
.

We have
E_{a,b;i,j}(u)=F_{b,a;j,i}(-u-\mu_{(a)})
for
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant 3
. In particular, we have
B_{b,a;j,i}(u)=C_{a,b;i,j}(-u)
.

Proof.

The case when
a=1
and
b=2
follows from the previous Section
5
. For
a=2
and
b=3
, this is obtained from the case
a=1
and
b=2
by taking the homomorphism
\psi_{\mu_{1}}
. As for the case
a=1
and
b=3
, this follows from a further application of (
3.22
).
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

We have

\displaystyle(u-v)\big[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{2;k,l}(v)\big]

\displaystyle=\delta_{jk}\Big(\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(E_{1;i,q}(u)-E_{1;i,q}(v)\big)E_{2;q,l}(v)

(6.1)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+E_{1,3;i,l}(v)-E_{1,3;i,l}(u)\Big),

(6.2)

\displaystyle[E_{2;i,j}^{(1)},\widetilde{D}_{3;k,l}(v)]

\displaystyle=-\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\big(\delta_{jk}E_{2;i,r}(v)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,l}(v)+\delta_{jl}\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)F_{2;r,i}(v)\big).

Proof.

By (
3.21
), we have

(u-v)[s_{i,\mu_{1}+j}(u),\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+k,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}(v)]=\delta_{jk}\sum_{a=1}^{N}s_{ia}(u)\tilde{s}_{a,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+l}(v).

Using parabolic generators, we have

(6.3)

\displaystyle(u-v)\Big[\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1;p,j}(u),-\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}E_{2;k,r}(v)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,l}(v)\Big]

\displaystyle=\delta_{jk}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\Big(\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(E_{1;p,q}(v)-E_{1;p,q}(u)\big)E_{2;q,r}(v)-E_{1,3;p,r}(v)+E_{1,3;p,r}(u)\Big)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,l}(v).

Note that by Corollary
3.9
, we have

[E_{1;p,j}(u),\widetilde{D}_{3;r,l}(v)]=[D_{1;i,p}(u),E_{2;k,r}(v)]=0.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle(u-v)\Big[\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1;p,j}(u),

\displaystyle-\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}E_{2;k,r}(v)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,l}(v)\Big]

\displaystyle=(u-v)\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\big[E_{1;p,j}(u),-E_{2;k,r}(v)\big]\widetilde{D}_{3;r,l}(v).

Comparing it with (
6.3
) and canceling
D_{1}(u)
from left and
D_{3}(v)
from right, one immediately obtains (
6.1
).

Expanding (
5.18
) in the region
\{(u,v)\penalty\ |\penalty\ u\gg v\}
and taking the coefficients of
u^{-1}
, we find that

[E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},\widetilde{D}_{2;k,l}(v)]=-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(\delta_{jk}E_{1;i,q}(v)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)+\delta_{jl}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)F_{1;q,i}(v)\big).

Applying the homomorphism
\psi_{\mu_{1}}
to the above formula in the algebra
{\mathscr{Y}}_{(\mu_{2},\mu_{3})}^{+}
, the relation (
6.2
) follows from Lemma
3.7
.
∎

Thus we have the following component-wise commutator relations for
B_{1;i,j}^{(r)}
and
B_{2;k,l}^{(s)}
.

Corollary 6.3
.

We have

[B_{1;i,j}^{(r+1)},B_{2;k,l}^{(s)}]-[B_{1;i,j}^{(r)},B_{2;k,l}^{(s+1)}]-\frac{\mu_{2}}{2}[B_{1;i,j}^{(r)},B_{2;k,l}^{(s)}]=-\delta_{il}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}B_{1;q,j}^{(r)}B_{2;k,q}^{(s)}.

Lemma 6.4
.

We have

(6.4)

\big[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),E_{2;k,l}(v)\big]=\\
E_{2;k,j}(v)[E_{1;i,g}(u),E_{2;g,l}(v)]+\frac{1}{u+v+\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\widetilde{D}_{2;k,q}(v)(F_{1;q,i}(v)-E_{1;i,q}(u))D_{3;j,l}(v).

Proof.

Start with (
3.21
)

(u-v)[s_{i,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+j}(u),\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+k,\mu_{1}+l}(v)]=\\
\delta_{jk}\Big(\sum_{a=1}^{\mu_{1}}s_{i,a}(u)\tilde{s}_{a,\mu_{1}+l}(v)+\sum_{b=\mu_{(1)}+1}^{\mu_{(2)}}s_{i,b}(u)\tilde{s}_{b,\mu_{1}+l}(v)+\sum_{c=\mu_{(2)}+1}^{\mu_{(3)}}s_{i,c}(u)\tilde{s}_{c,\mu_{1}+l}(v)\Big).

Replacing everything by parabolic generators, we obtain

(u-v)\Big[\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1,3;p,j}(u),-\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)F_{2;r,l}(v)\Big]=\delta_{jk}\times\bigg\{\\
\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}D_{1;i,p}(u)\Big(-E_{1;p,q}(v)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)+\sum_{r,t=1}^{\mu_{3}}(E_{1,3;p,r}(v)-E_{1;p,q}(v)E_{2;q,r}(v))\widetilde{D}_{3;r,t}(v)F_{2;t,l}(v)\Big)\\
+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\Big(\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1;p,q}(u)\Big)\Big(\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)+\sum_{r,t=1}^{\mu_{3}}E_{2;q,r}(v)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,t}(v)F_{2;t,l}(v)\Big)\\
+\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\Big(\sum_{a=1}^{\mu_{1}}D_{1;i,p}(u)E_{1,3;p,r}(u)\Big)\Big(-\sum_{t=1}^{\mu_{3}}\widetilde{D}_{3;r,t}(v)F_{2;t,l}(v)\Big)\bigg\}.

Since
D_{1;i,j}(u)=s_{ij}(u)
and
\tilde{s}_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+k,\mu_{1}+l}(v)
commute, we may cancel
D_{1}(u)
from the left and deduce that

(6.5)

(u-v)\Big[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)F_{2;r,l}(v)\Big]=\delta_{jk}\times\bigg\{\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(E_{1;i,q}(v)-E_{1;i,q}(u)\big)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)\\
+\sum_{r,t=1}^{\mu_{3}}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\Big(E_{1,3;i,r}(u)-E_{1,3;i,r}(v)+\big(E_{1;i,q}(v)-E_{1;i,q}(u)\big)E_{2;q,r}(v)\Big)\widetilde{D}_{3;r,t}(v)F_{2;t,l}(v)\bigg\}.

Using (
6.1
), the RHS of (
6.5
) can be written as

(6.6)

\delta_{jk}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\Big(\big(E_{1;i,q}(v)-E_{1;i,q}(u)\big)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)-(u-v)\sum_{r,t=1}^{\mu_{3}}[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{2;k,r}(v)]\widetilde{D}_{3;r,t}(v)F_{2;t,l}(v)\Big).

For the LHS of (
6.5
), we have

(6.7)

(u-v)\Big[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)F_{2;r,l}(v)\Big]\\
=(u-v)\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\Big(\big[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)\big]F_{2;r,l}(v)+\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)\big[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),F_{2;r,l}(v)\big]\Big).

We shall work on the first term in the RHS of (
6.7
),

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}

\displaystyle\big[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)\big]F_{2;r,l}(v)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{efgen}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\big[[E_{1;i,g}(u),E_{2;g,l}^{(1)}],\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)\big]F_{2;r,l}(v)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{defcom}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\big[E_{1;i,g}(u),[E_{2;g,l}^{(1)},\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)]\big]F_{2;r,l}(v)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{e20d3}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{r,t=1}^{\mu_{3}}\big[E_{1;i,g}(u),\delta_{jk}E_{2;g,t}(v)\widetilde{D}_{3;t,r}(v)+\delta_{jr}\widetilde{D}_{3;k,t}(v)F_{2;t,g}(v)\big]F_{2;r,l}(v)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{defcom}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{jk}\sum_{r,t=1}^{\mu_{3}}\big[E_{1;i,g}(u),E_{2;g,t}(v)\big]\widetilde{D}_{3;t,r}(v)F_{2;r,l}(v)

(6.8)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)\big[E_{1;i,g}(u),F_{2;r,g}(v)\big]F_{2;j,l}(v).

Plugging (
6.8
) into (
6.7
) and comparing the resulting (
6.7
) with (
6.6
), we conclude that

\delta_{jk}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(E_{1;i,q}(v)-E_{1;i,q}(u)\big)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)\\
=-(u-v)\sum_{r=1}^{\mu_{3}}\Big(\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)\big[E_{1;i,g}(u),F_{2;r,g}(v)\big]F_{2;j,l}(v)-\widetilde{D}_{3;k,r}(v)\big[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),F_{2;r,l}(v)\big]\Big).

Multiplying
D_{3}(v)
from left, we have

(6.9)

\begin{split}[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),F_{2;k,l}(v)]&=[E_{1;i,g}(u),F_{2;k,g}(v)]F_{2;j,l}(v)\\
&+\frac{1}{u-v}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(E_{1;i,q}(v)-E_{1;i,q}(u)\big)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(v)D_{3;k,j}(v).\end{split}

Finally, applying the anti-automorphism
\tau
to (
6.9
) with the substitution
u\to-u-\mu_{1}
, the relation (
6.4
) follows from Lemmas
3.14
and
6.1
.
∎

6.2.
Preparation for Serre relations

We prepare several identities that will be used to establish the Serre relations. By (
6.1
), we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{2;k,l}^{(1)}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{jk}E_{13;i,l}(u),

(6.11)

\displaystyle[E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},E_{2;k,l}(u)]

\displaystyle=\delta_{jk}\Big(E_{13;i,l}(u)-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)E_{2;q,l}(u)\Big).

It follows from (
6.4
) that

(6.12)

[E_{1,3;i,j}(u),E_{2;k,l}^{(1)}]=-\delta_{jl}E_{1;i,k}(u).

Using Lemma
6.1
and (
6.9
), we obtain

(6.13)

\begin{split}&[E_{1,3;i,j}^{(1)},E_{2;lk}(u)]\\
&=[E_{1;i,g}^{(1)},E_{2;g,k}(u)]E_{2;l,j}(u)+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(-u-\mu_{(2)})\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(-u-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;k,j}(-u-\mu_{(2)}).\end{split}

Applying the homomorphism
\psi_{\mu_{1}}
to (
5.22
) and using Lemma
3.7
, we get

(6.14)

[E_{2;i,j}(u),E_{2;k,l}^{(1)}]=\widetilde{D}_{2;i,k}(u)D_{3;l,j}(u)-\delta_{ik}\delta_{lj}-E_{2;i,l}(u)E_{2;k,j}(u).

Lemma 6.5
.

We have

(6.15)

\begin{split}&\big[[E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},E_{2;j,k}(u)],E_{2;l,g}^{(1)}\big]+\big[[E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},E_{2;j,k}^{(1)}],E_{2;l,g}(u)\big]\\
=&\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\Big(E_{1;i,q}(-u-\mu_{(2)})\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(-u-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;g,k}(-u-\mu_{(2)})-E_{1;i,q}(u)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(u)D_{3;g,k}(u)\Big).\end{split}

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\big[[E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},E_{2;j,k}(u)],E_{2;l,g}^{(1)}\big]+\big[[E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},E_{2;j,k}^{(1)}],E_{2;l,g}(u)\big]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ser1}}{\underset{\eqref{ser2}}{=}}

\displaystyle\Big[E_{1,3;i,k}(u)-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)E_{2;q,k}(u),E_{2;l,g}^{(1)}\Big]+[E_{1,3;i,k}^{(1)},E_{2;l,g}(u)]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ser3}}{\underset{\eqref{ser4}}{=}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{kg}E_{1;i,l}(u)-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}[E_{1;i,q}(u),E_{2;l,g}^{(1)}]E_{2;q,k}(u)-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)[E_{2;q,k}(u),E_{2;l,g}^{(1)}]

\displaystyle+[E_{1;i,p}^{(1)},E_{2;p,g}(u)]E_{2;l,k}(u)+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(-u-\mu_{(2)})\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(-u-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;g,k}(-u-\mu_{(2)})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ser1}}{\underset{\eqref{ser5}}{=}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{kg}E_{1;i,l}(u)-E_{13;i,g}(u)E_{2;l,k}(u)

\displaystyle-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)\Big(\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(u)D_{3;g,k}(u)-\delta_{kg}\delta_{ql}-E_{2;q,g}(u)E_{2;l,k}(u)\Big)

\displaystyle+E_{13;i,g}(u)E_{2;l,k}(u)-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)E_{2;q,g}(u)E_{2;l,k}(u)

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(-u-\mu_{(2)})\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(-u-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;g,k}(-u-\mu_{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(u)\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(u)D_{3;g,k}(u)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}E_{1;i,q}(-u-\mu_{(2)})\widetilde{D}_{2;q,l}(-u-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;g,k}(-u-\mu_{(2)}),

completing the proof.
∎

Recall that by Lemma
6.1
, we have

(6.16)

B_{a;j,i}(u)=E_{a;i,j}(-u-\tfrac{\mu_{(a)}}{2}).

Lemma 6.6
.

We have

\displaystyle\Big[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,i}^{(2)},E_{1;i,j}(u)\Big]

\displaystyle=-(2u+\mu_{1})E_{1;i,j}(u)+2E_{1;i,j}^{(1)},

\displaystyle\Big[D_{2;j,j}^{(2)},E_{1;i,j}(u)\Big]

\displaystyle=-(2u+\mu_{1})E_{1;i,j}(u)+2E_{1;i,j}^{(1)}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\Big[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,i}^{(2)},B_{1;j,i}^{(r)}\Big]=2B_{1;j,i}^{(r+1)},\qquad\Big[D_{2;j,j}^{(2)},B_{1;j,i}^{(r)}\Big]=2B_{1;j,i}^{(r+1)}.

Proof.

We only prove the first one as the second one is similar.

It follows from (
5.10
) that

(6.17)

\begin{split}&(u-v)(u+v+\mu_{1})[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,j}(u),E_{1;k,l}(v)]\\
&=(u+v+\mu_{1})(E_{1;i,l}(u)-E_{1;i,l}(v))\widetilde{D}_{1;k,j}(u)+(u-v)\widetilde{D}_{1;i,k}(u)(E_{1;j,l}(v)-F_{1;l,j}(u)).\end{split}

Taking the coefficients of
u
in (
6.17
), we find that

(6.18)

[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,j}^{(1)},E_{1;k,l}(v)]=-\delta_{jk}E_{1;i,l}(v)+\delta_{ik}E_{1;j,l}(v).

In particular, we have

(6.19)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,i}^{(1)},E_{1;k,l}(v)]

\displaystyle=0.

Taking the coefficients of
u^{0}
and applying (
6.19
), we obtain

(6.20)

[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,i}^{(2)},E_{1;k,l}(v)]=-(2v+\mu_{1})\delta_{ik}E_{1;i,l}(v)+2\delta_{ik}E_{1;i,l}^{(1)}+\widetilde{D}_{1;i,k}^{(1)}E_{1;i,l}(v)-E_{1;i,l}(v)\widetilde{D}_{1;k,i}^{(1)}.

Thus the first identity follows from (
6.20
) and (
6.19
).
∎

6.3.
Serre relations

In this subsection, we shall deduce the Serre relations involving

\big[[E_{1;i,j}^{(s)},E_{2;j,k}^{(r_{1})}],E_{2;l,g}^{(r_{2})}\big]+\big[[E_{1;i,j}^{(s)},E_{2;j,k}^{(r_{2})}],E_{2;l,g}^{(r_{1})}\big].

6.3.1.
The case
\mu_{2}>1

Using (
5.14
), we find that

(6.21)

\begin{split}&[E_{2;j,k}(v),E_{2;l,g}(w)]+[E_{2;j,k}(w),E_{2;l,g}(v)]\\
=&\frac{1}{v+w+\mu_{(2)}}\Big(\widetilde{D}_{2;j,l}(v)D_{3;g,k}(v)-\widetilde{D}_{2;j,l}(-w-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;g,k}(-w-\mu_{(2)})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\widetilde{D}_{2;j,l}(w)D_{3;g,k}(w)-\widetilde{D}_{2;j,l}(-v-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;g,k}(-v-\mu_{(2)})\Big).\end{split}

In this case, it follows from (
6.1
) that
[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{2;j,k}(v)]
is independent of
j
and hence we can assume further that
j\neq l
. By the Jacobi identity and (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\big[[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{2;j,k}(v)],E_{2;l,g}(w)\big]+\big[[E_{1;i,j}(u),E_{2;j,k}(w)],E_{2;l,g}(v)\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[E_{1;i,j}(u),[E_{2;j,k}(v),E_{2;l,g}(w)]\big]+\big[E_{1;i,j}(u),[E_{2;j,k}(w),E_{2;l,g}(v)]\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[E_{1;i,j}(u),[E_{2;j,k}(v),E_{2;l,g}(w)]+[E_{2;j,k}(w),E_{2;l,g}(v)]\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v+w+\mu_{(2)}}\Big[E_{1;i,j}(u),\widetilde{D}_{2;j,l}(v)D_{3;g,k}(v)-\widetilde{D}_{2;j,l}(-w-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;g,k}(-w-\mu_{(2)})

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt+\widetilde{D}_{2;j,l}(w)D_{3;g,k}(w)-\widetilde{D}_{2;j,l}(-v-\mu_{(2)})D_{3;g,k}(-v-\mu_{(2)})\Big].

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\big[[B_{1;j,i}(u),

\displaystyle B_{2;k,j}(v)],B_{2;g,l}(w)\big]+\big[[B_{1;j,i}(u),B_{2;k,j}(w)],B_{2;g,l}(v)\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v+w}\big[B_{1;j,i}(u),Z_{2;j,l,g,k}(v)+Z_{2;j,l,g,k}(w)-Z_{2;j,l,g,k}(-v)-Z_{2;j,l,g,k}(-w)\big].

This gives rise to the following component-wise formula,

(6.22)

\big[[B_{1;j,i}^{(q)},B_{2;k,j}^{(r_{1})}],B_{2;g,l}^{(r_{2})}\big]+\big[[B_{1;j,i}^{(q)},B_{2;k,j}^{(r_{2})}],B_{2;g,l}^{(r_{1})}\big]=((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})[Z_{2;j,l,g,k}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1)},B_{1;j,i}^{(q)}].

One can obtain a different version of Serre relations by using (
5.19
) instead of (
5.14
),

(6.23)

\begin{split}\big[[B_{1;j,i}(u),&B_{2;k,j}(v)],B_{2;g,l}(w)\big]+\big[[B_{1;j,i}(u),B_{2;k,j}(w)],B_{2;g,l}(v)\big]\\
=&\frac{1}{v+w}\big[B_{1;j,i}(u),Z_{2;l,j,k,g}(-v)+Z_{2;l,j,k,g}(-w)-Z_{2;l,j,k,g}(v)-Z_{2;l,j,k,g}(w)\big].\end{split}

Applying the automorphism
\zeta_{N}
and using Proposition
3.15
, one obtains the other Serre relation

\big[[B_{2;i,j}^{(q)},B_{1;k,l}^{(r_{1})}],B_{1;f,g}^{(r_{2})}\big]+\big[[B_{2;i,j}^{(q)},B_{1;k,l}^{(r_{2})}],B_{1;f,g}^{(r_{1})}\big]=\delta_{jk}((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})[Z_{1;l,g,f,k}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1)},B_{2;i,j}^{(q)}].

6.3.2.
The case
\mu_{2}=1

In this case, (
6.15
) simplifies as

(6.24)

\begin{split}\big[[B_{1;j,i}^{(1)}&,B_{2;k,j}(u)],B_{2;g,j}^{(1)}\big]+\big[[B_{1;j,i}^{(1)},B_{2;k,j}^{(1)}],B_{2;g,j}(u)\big]\\
&=B_{1;j,i}(-u+\tfrac{1}{2})Z_{2;j,j,g,k}(u)-B_{1;j,i}(u+\tfrac{1}{2})Z_{2;j,j,g,k}(-u)\end{split}

Fix
i,j,g,k
. We shall use the following shorthand notation:

(6.25)

\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1},r_{2}):=\big[[B_{1;j,i}^{(q)},B_{2;k,j}^{(r_{1})}],B_{2;g,j}^{(r_{2})}\big]+\big[[B_{1;j,i}^{(q)},B_{2;k,j}^{(r_{2})}],B_{2;g,j}^{(r_{1})}\big],

(6.26)

\Omega(q;r)=\sum_{p_{1}=1}^{r-1}\sum_{p_{2}=0}^{r-1-p_{1}}(-1)^{p_{1}-1}2^{-p_{2}}{p_{1}+p_{2}-1\choose p_{2}}B_{1;j,i}^{(p_{1}+q-1)}Z_{2;j,j,g,k}^{(r-1-p_{1}-p_{2})}.

Since
\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1},r_{2})
is symmetric in
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
, we shall assume further that
r_{1}\geqslant r_{2}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Under the above assumption, we have

(6.27)

\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1},r_{2})=\big((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}}\big)\Omega(q;r_{1}+r_{2}).

Proof.

Our proof follows the strategy of
[
LWZ23
, §4.3]
.

By (
6.24
) we know that (
6.27
) holds for the case
q=r_{2}=1
. Now we recursively apply the map
X\mapsto[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,i}^{(2)},X]
to the equality
\mathbb{S}(1;r_{1},1)=\big((-1)^{r_{1}}-1\big)\Omega(1;r_{1}+1)
.
Note that by Corollary
3.9
, we have

[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,i}^{(2)},B_{2;l,j}(u)]=[\widetilde{D}_{1;i,i}^{(2)},Z_{2;j,j,g,k}(u)]=0.

Thus it follows from Lemma
6.6
that the equality (
6.27
) also holds for the case
r_{2}=1
(namely
q
can be arbitrary).

Note that
[s_{11}(u),s_{11}(v)]=0
by (
2.12
), and hence
[D_{2;j,j}^{(2)},D_{2,j,j}(u)]=0
by Lemma
3.7
. Further, we have
[D_{2;j,j}^{(2)},Z_{2;j,j,g,k}(u)]=0
.

Finally, we prove (
6.27
) by induction on
r_{1}+r_{2}
. The base case has been established above. Suppose we have
\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1},r_{2})=\big((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}}\big)\Omega(q;r_{1}+r_{2})
. We apply the commutator by
D_{2;j,j}^{(2)}
to obtain

2\mathbb{S}(q+1;r_{1},r_{2})-2\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1}+1,r_{2})-2\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1},r_{2}+1)=2\big((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}}\big)\Omega(q+1;r_{1}+r_{2}).

By induction hypothesis, we conclude that
\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1},r_{2}+1)=-\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1}+1,r_{2})
. Using this equality multiple times, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1},r_{2}+1)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r_{2}}\mathbb{S}(q;r_{1}+r_{2},1)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r_{2}}((-1)^{r_{1}+r_{2}}-1)\Omega(q;r_{1}+r_{2}+1)

\displaystyle=((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}+1})\Omega(q;r_{1}+r_{2}+1).

This completes the proof.
∎

Applying the automorphism
\zeta_{N}
and using Proposition
3.15
, one obtains the other Serre relation:

\displaystyle\big[[B_{2;i,j}^{(q)},B_{1;j,k}^{(r_{1})}],B_{1;j,g}^{(r_{2})}\big]+\big[[B_{2;i,j}^{(q)},B_{1;j,k}^{(r_{2})}],B_{1;j,g}^{(r_{1})}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})\sum_{p_{1}=1}^{r_{1}+r_{2}-1}\sum_{p_{2}=0}^{r_{1}+r_{2}-1-p_{1}}(-1)^{p_{2}}2^{-p_{2}}{p_{1}+p_{2}-1\choose p_{2}}B_{2;i,j}^{(p_{1}+q-1)}Z_{1;g,k,j,j}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1-p_{1}-p_{2})}.

7.
Parabolic presentations for type AII: the proof

The proof of Theorem
4.1
for AII type is very similar to AI type, but in some places different treatments are required. In this section we provide the proof by explaining the differences in detail.
We assume
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
is an arbitrary even composition of
N
and follow the convention (
2.9
) throughout this section.

7.1.
PBW bases for twisted Yangians of type AII

The assumption that
\mu
is even ensures that the Gauss decomposition (
3.2
) works equally well with respect to
\mu
.
As a consequence, we obtain the associated parabolic generators
D_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
E_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
E_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}
,
F_{a;j,i}^{(r)}
,
F_{b,a;j,i}^{(r)}
,
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
B_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}
in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
by following the same routine.
The next result is the analogue of Theorem
3.13
for type AII, which can be deduced from Proposition
2.5
(2) by exactly the same way.

Proposition 7.1
.

The monomials in the elements

\displaystyle\big\{H_{a;2i-1,2i-1}^{(r)},\ H_{a;2i-1,2i}^{(2r-1)},

\displaystyle\ H_{a;2i,2i-1}^{(2r-1)}\big\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},r\geqslant 1},

\displaystyle\big\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\big\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},\lfloor\tfrac{i+1}{2}\rfloor\neq\lfloor\tfrac{j+1}{2}\rfloor,r\geqslant 1},

\displaystyle\quad\big\{B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\big\}_{1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 1},

taken in any fixed linear order form a PBW basis of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
.

7.2.
Relations in 2-block case

The relations in the cases of 2-block and 3-block are essential for the proof of Theorem
4.1
.
It turns out that the relations in the 3-block case are exactly the same as the AI type, and hence we list only the relations in the 2-block case explicitly.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
be an even strict composition of
N
. Then the following relations hold in
{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{\mu}
:

\displaystyle[H_{a;i,j}(u),H_{b;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\delta_{ab}\Big(\frac{1}{u-v}\big(H_{a;k,j}(u)H_{a;i,l}(v)-H_{a;k,j}(v)H_{a;i,l}(u)\big)

(7.1)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{u+v}\big(\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}H_{a;i,k^{\prime}}(u)H_{a;j^{\prime},l}(v)-\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}H_{a;k,i^{\prime}}(v)H_{a;l^{\prime},j}(u)\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\theta_{i}\theta_{j^{\prime}}}{u^{2}-v^{2}}\big(H_{a;k,i^{\prime}}(u)H_{a;j^{\prime},l}(v)-H_{a;k,i^{\prime}}(v)H_{a;j^{\prime},l}(u)\big)\Big),

(7.2)

\displaystyle[H_{1;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\delta_{il}}{u-v+\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\big(B_{1;k,p}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})-B_{1;k,p}(v)\big)H_{1;p,j}(u)

\displaystyle\,+\frac{\delta_{jl^{\prime}}\theta_{l^{\prime}}}{u+v+\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2}}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\theta_{p}H_{1;i,p}(u)\big(B_{1;k,p^{\prime}}(v)-B_{1;k,p^{\prime}}(-u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})\big),

(7.3)

\displaystyle[H_{2;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\theta_{j^{\prime}}\theta_{k}}{u+v}H_{2;i,k^{\prime}}(u)\big(B_{1;j^{\prime},l}(-u)-B_{1;j^{\prime},l}(v)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{u-v}\big(B_{1;i,l}(v)-B_{1;i,l}(u)\big)H_{2;k,j}(u),

(7.4)

\displaystyle[B_{1;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(v)]=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{u-v}\big(B_{1;k,j}(v)-B_{1;k,j}(u)\big)\big(B_{1;i,l}(u)-B_{1;i,l}(v)\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\theta_{i}\theta_{j}}{u+v}\big(Z_{1;j^{\prime},l,k,i^{\prime}}(v)-Z_{1;j^{\prime},l,k,i^{\prime}}(-u)\big),

(7.5)

\displaystyle B_{1;i,j}(-u)=

\displaystyle\,\theta_{i}\theta_{j}C_{1;j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}(u),\hskip 199.16928pt

where
Z_{1;j,l,k,i}(u)=\widetilde{H}_{1;j,l}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})H_{2;k,i}(u)
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition
5.1
.
∎

7.3.
Some symmetry relations

The following derived relations will be used in Definition
8.10
of shifted twisted Yangians of type AII.

Lemma 7.3
.

The following
Z
-relations hold in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}
, for
1\leqslant a\leqslant n-1
:

(7.6)

\theta_{i}\theta_{j}Z_{a;j^{\prime},l,k,i^{\prime}}^{(2r-1)}+\theta_{k}\theta_{l}Z_{a;l^{\prime},j,i,k^{\prime}}^{(2r-1)}=0.

Proof.

We carry out the proof in the case
a=1
, but the argument works well for any
a
.
To begin with, one can deduce the equivalent form of (
7.4
):

(7.7)

\begin{split}[B_{1;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(v)]=\frac{1}{u-v}\big(B_{1;i,l}(u)-B_{1;i,l}(v)\big)\big(B_{1;k,j}(v)-B_{1;k,j}(u)\big)\\
+\frac{\theta_{k}\theta_{l}}{u+v}\big(Z_{1;l^{\prime},j,i,k^{\prime}}(-v)-Z_{1;l^{\prime},j,i,k^{\prime}}(u)\big).\end{split}

Setting
u=v
in (
7.4
), we have

[B_{1;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(u)]=\frac{\theta_{i}\theta_{j}}{2u}(Z_{1;j^{\prime},l,k,i^{\prime}}(u)-Z_{1;j^{\prime},l,k,i^{\prime}}(-u)).

Similarly, setting
u=v
in (
7.7
), we have

[B_{1;i,j}(u),B_{1;k,l}(u)]=\frac{\theta_{k}\theta_{l}}{2u}(Z_{1;l^{\prime},j,i,k^{\prime}}(-u)-Z_{1;l^{\prime},j,i,k^{\prime}}(u)).

Thus the desired relation (
7.6
) follows by taking the coefficients of
u^{-2r}
on the RHS.
∎

We point out the following analogue of Proposition
3.10
for AII type, whose proof is similar to Lemma
6.1
by using (
7.5
).

Proposition 7.4
.

For
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a}
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b}
, we have

B_{b,a;i,j}(u)=\theta_{i}\theta_{j}C_{a,b;j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}(-u).

7.4.
Proof of Theorem
4.1
for type AII

The proof of this result is similar to type AI, and hence we will only indicate the steps of the argument which need to be treated differently.
Let
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}^{-}
denote the algebra generated by the generators and relations as in the statement of the theorem.
By induction on
n
, one checks that the parabolic generators satisfy the listed relations, where the initial step is reduced to the case when
n=2,3
.
Then we have a surjective homomorphism
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}^{-}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
and it suffices to show this map is injective.

For
1\leqslant b<a\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
, and
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}
, define the elements
B_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}
of
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}^{-}
by exactly the same rule (
4.13
), which do not depend on the choice of
k
as well.
Let
\operatorname{gr}\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}^{-}
be the associated graded algebra with respect to the loop filtration, which is defined similarly by setting
\deg H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}=\deg B_{a,b;k,l}^{(r)}=r-1
.
Let
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}
and
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;k,l}^{(r)}
be the images of
H_{a;i,j}^{(r+1)}
and
B_{a,b;,k,l}^{(r+1)}
, respectively, in the
r
-th component of the graded algebra
\operatorname{gr}\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}^{-}
.
In addition, for
1\leqslant b<a\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
, and
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}
, define
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,a;j,i}^{(r)}:=(-1)^{r+1}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(r)}
.
A similar argument shows that for
b-a>1
and
r,s\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r+s)}=[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,b-1;i,k}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b-1,a;k,j}^{(s)}]

with any
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{a-1}
.

We claim the following identity holds for all
1\leqslant a,b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{b}
,
r\geqslant 0
:

(7.8)

{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}=(-1)^{r+1}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,a;j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)}.

If
a\neq b
, it follows from the definition. Assume from now on
a=b
.
By (
4.3
), we have

(7.9)

\displaystyle[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(0)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;k,l}^{(r)}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{kj}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,l}^{(r)}-\delta_{il}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;k,j}^{(r)}-\big(\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\delta_{ik^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},l}^{(r)}-\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}\delta_{l^{\prime}j}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;k,i^{\prime}}^{(r)}\big),

(7.10)

\displaystyle[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;k,l}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(0)}]

\displaystyle=\delta_{il}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;k,j}^{(r)}-\delta_{kj}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,l}^{(r)}-(-1)^{r}\big(\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}\delta_{k^{\prime}i}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;l^{\prime},j}^{(r)}-\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\delta_{j^{\prime}l}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,k^{\prime}}^{(r)}\big).

Equations (
7.9
) and (
7.10
) differ by
-1
and hence they sum to zero, thus

(7.11)

\delta_{ik^{\prime}}\big(\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},l}^{(r)}+(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;l^{\prime},j}^{(r)}\big)-\delta_{jl^{\prime}}\big(\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;k,i^{\prime}}^{(r)}+(-1)^{r}\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,k^{\prime}}^{(r)}\big)=0.

Setting
k=i^{\prime}
and
l=j
in (
7.11
), we have

\theta_{i^{\prime}}\theta_{j^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},j}^{(r)}+(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{j^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},j}^{(r)}=\theta_{i}\theta_{j}\big(1+(-1)^{r+1}\big){\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},j}^{(r)}=0.

Therefore
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},j}^{(r)}=0
for all odd
r
. Note that (
7.8
) holds trivially for even
r
if
i=j^{\prime}
.
Setting
k=i^{\prime}
and
l=j^{\prime}
in (
7.11
), we have

(7.12)

\big(\theta_{i^{\prime}}\theta_{j^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(r)}+(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j,j}^{(r)}\big)-\big(\theta_{i}\theta_{j}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)}+(-1)^{r}\theta_{i^{\prime}}\theta_{j^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,i}^{(r)}\big)=0

Setting
i=j^{\prime}
in (
7.12
), we have
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,i}^{(r)}={\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)}
for all odd
r
. Next we show by induction on
a
that
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,i}^{(r)}=-{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)}
for all even
r
. The initial case
a=1
follows from (
4.1
), (
4.3
) and Remark
2.4
.
For
a\geqslant 1
, setting
l=j^{\prime}
and
k=i^{\prime}
in (
7.6
), we obtain
Z_{a;j^{\prime},j^{\prime},i,i}^{(2r+1)}=-Z_{a;j,j,i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(2r+1)}
. By (
3.28
), their image in
\operatorname{gr}\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}^{-}
gives the following equality

-{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(2r)}+{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a+1;i,i}^{(2r)}={\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j,j}^{(2r)}-{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a+1,a+1;i^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(2r)}.

Now
{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j,j}^{(2r)}=-{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(2r)}
due to the inductive hypothesis. This completes the induction.

Suppose now
i\neq j
.
Setting
l=i^{\prime}=k
in (
7.11
), we have

\theta_{i^{\prime}}\theta_{j^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)}+(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{i}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}=0,

which completes the proof of (
7.8
).

We claim the following analogue of (
4.21
) holds for AII type:

(7.13)

\begin{split}[{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)},{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c,d;k,l}^{(s)}]=\delta_{bc}\delta_{jk}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{a,d;i,l}^{(r+s)}-\delta_{ad}\delta_{il}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c,b;k,j}^{(r+s)}-(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}\big(\delta_{ac}\delta_{i^{\prime}k}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{b,d;j^{\prime},l}^{(r+s)}-\delta_{bd}\delta_{jl^{\prime}}{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}_{c,a;k,i^{\prime}}^{(r+s)}\big).\end{split}

By (
7.8
) one may assume
a\geqslant b
and
c\geqslant d
in (
7.13
), which then can be established by case-by-case discussions as performed in AI and hence we omit the detail.
The injectivity of
\mathbf{Y}_{\mu}^{-}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
follows from (
7.13
) and Proposition
7.1
.

Part II
Truncated shifted twisted Yangians and finite
W
-algebras

8.
Shifted twisted Yangians and parabolic presentations

In this section we define a shifted twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
of type AI, associated to every symmetric matrix with non-negative integral coefficients, via a parabolic presentation for each composition
\mu
of
N
admissible for
\sigma
. We show that it can be naturally viewed as a subalgebra of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
. We also show that the presentation of the shifted subalgebra is independent of
\mu
. Along the way we provide PBW bases for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
for each shape
\mu
which is admissible for
\sigma
.

Parallel results for type AII are obtained under the additional assumption that every part of
\mu
is even (from henceforth we say that such a
\mu
is even).
We also highlight a different presentation for the shifted twisted Yangian of type AI using the Drinfeld presentation from
[
LWZ23
]
and show that the algebras given by the parabolic presentations and by the Drinfeld presentations are isomorphic.

8.1.
Shifted twisted Yangians of type AI

Fix
1\leqslant n\leqslant N
and a symmetric matrix
\sigma=(\mathfrak{s}_{i,j})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}
of non-negative integers (shifts) such that

(8.1)

\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}+\mathfrak{s}_{j,k}=\mathfrak{s}_{i,k}

provided
|i-j|+|j-k|=|i-k|
. Note that
\mathfrak{s}_{1,1}=\cdots=\mathfrak{s}_{n,n}=0
and
\sigma
is completely determined by
\mathfrak{s}_{a+1,a}
,
1\leqslant a<n
. We call
\sigma
a
shift matrix
.

Fix a shift matrix
\sigma=(\mathfrak{s}_{i,j})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}
. We say that a composition
\mu
of
N
of length
n
is
admissible
for
\sigma
if
\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}=0
for all
\mu_{(a-1)}<i,j\leqslant\mu_{(a)}
for
1\leqslant a\leqslant n
. We also set

(8.2)

\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu):=\mathfrak{s}_{\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(b)}}.

Note that the original shift matrix
\sigma
can be recovered from
(\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu))_{1\leqslant a,b\leqslant n}
if
\mu
is admissible for
\sigma
. Below, we fix an admissible
\mu
for
\sigma
.

Definition 8.1
.

The (
dominantly
)
shifted twisted Yangian
associated to the matrix
\sigma
and the composition
\mu

is the algebra
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
generated by

\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 1},\quad\{B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a+1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>\mathfrak{s}_{a+1,a}(\mu)}

subject to the relations (
4.1
)–(
4.9
)
with all admissible indices together with the following relations (cf. Remark
4.2
)

(8.3)

Z_{a;i,i,j,j}^{(2r-1)}=0,\quad 1\leqslant a<n,\penalty\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},\penalty\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a+1},\penalty\ 1\leqslant r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a+1,a}(\mu).

By Theorem
4.1
, if
\sigma
is the zero matrix, then
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
is exactly
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
. Clearly, we have the canonical homomorphism
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}={\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\mu}
sending the generators
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
and
B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
to elements of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
with same names.

In the rest of this subsection, we shall prove that this homomorphism is injective and its image is independent of the particular choice of the admissible shape
\mu
.

For
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a}
and
r>\mathfrak{s}_{b,a}(\mu)
, define elements
B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}
inductively by

(8.4)

B_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(r)}:=B_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\quad B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}:=[B_{b-1;i,k}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{b,b-1}(\mu)+1)},B_{b-1,a;k,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{b,b-1}(\mu))}],

cf. Lemma
3.4
. Again, the definition is independent of the choice of
k
.
Recall the twisted current algebra
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}
which has a basis

\big\{e_{ij}\otimes z^{r}-(-1)^{r}e_{ji}\otimes z^{r}\big\}_{1\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant N,r\in\mathbb{N}}\setminus\{0\}.

Under the assumption (
8.1
), the vectors

\big\{e_{ij}\otimes z^{r}-(-1)^{r}e_{ji}\otimes z^{r}\big\}_{1\leqslant j<i\leqslant N,r\geqslant\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}}\setminus\{0\}

span a subalgebra of
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}
, and call it the
shifted twisted current algebra
. We denote this subalgebra by
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma)
. The universal enveloping algebra
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma))
has a natural grading induced from the grading on
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]
, which places
xt^{r}
in degree
r
.

Theorem 8.2
.

The monomials in

\{H_{a;i,i}^{(2r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0},\quad\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0},\quad\{B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a<b<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>\mathfrak{s}_{b,a}(\mu)},

taken in some (any) fixed linear order form a PBW basis of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
. In particular, the canonical map
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
is injective.

Proof.

Define a filtration
\mathcal{F}_{0}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)\subset\mathcal{F}_{1}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)\subset\cdots
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
by setting

(8.5)

\deg H_{a;i,i}^{(2r)}=2r-1,\quad\deg H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}=r-1,\quad\deg B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}=r-1.

Note that the elements
H_{a;i,i}^{(2r-1)}
can be expressed in terms
H_{b;j,j}^{(2s)}
by the relations (
4.1
)–(
4.2
) and (
4.10
). Define elements
\{\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r}\}_{1\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant N,r\geqslant\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}}
of the associated graded algebra
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
by the equations

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}H_{a;i,j}^{(r+1)}=\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a-1)}+i,\mu_{(a-1)}+j;r},

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r+1)}=\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(b-1)}+i,\mu_{(a-1)}+j;r}.

We claim that
\mathsf{e}_{i,i;2r}=0
for
r\in\mathbb{N}
. It follows from (
4.6
) that

(8.6)

\big[\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+i,\mu_{(a-1)}+j;r},\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+k,\mu_{(a-1)}+l;s}\big]=(-1)^{r}\big(\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+k,\mu_{(a)}+i;r+s}-\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a-1)}+j,\mu_{(a-1)}+l;r+s}\big).

Interchanging
i\leftrightarrow k
,
j\leftrightarrow l
, and
r\leftrightarrow s
, we obtain

(8.7)

\big[\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+k,\mu_{(a-1)}+l;s},\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+i,\mu_{(a-1)}+j;r}\big]=(-1)^{s}\big(\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+i,\mu_{(a)}+k;r+s}-\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a-1)}+l,\mu_{(a-1)}+j;r+s}\big).

Setting
s=r
,
k=i
, and
l=j
, we find that

(8.8)

\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+i,\mu_{(a)}+i;2r}-\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a-1)}+j,\mu_{(a-1)}+j;2r}=0,

where
r\geqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a+1,a}(\mu)
. Note that (
4.1
) implies that
\mathsf{e}_{1,1;2s}=0
for
s\in\mathbb{N}
. In addition, for
r<\mathfrak{s}_{a+1,a}(\mu)
,
\mathsf{e}_{i,i;2r}=0
follows immediately from (
4.1
) and (
8.3
). Now an induction on
a
with the help of (
8.8
) implies that
\mathsf{e}_{i,i;2r}=0
for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
.

Repeating the same strategy as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
(the paragraph includes (
4.19
) and (
4.20
)), we find that for
r\in\mathbb{N}

\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+i,\mu_{(a)}+j;r}=(-1)^{r+1}\mathsf{e}_{\mu_{(a)}+j,\mu_{(a)}+i;r}.

Extend the set of elements
\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r}
by the rule:
\mathsf{e}_{j,i;r}=(-1)^{r+1}\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r}
, for
1\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant N
and
r\geqslant\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}
. Following the proof of Theorem
4.1
, one verifies that these elements satisfy the relations

(8.9)

[\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r},\mathsf{e}_{k,l;s}]=\delta_{jk}\mathsf{e}_{i,l;r+s}-\delta_{li}\mathsf{e}_{k,j;r+s}-(-1)^{r}\delta_{ki}\mathsf{e}_{j,l;r+s}+(-1)^{r}\delta_{jl}\mathsf{e}_{k,i;r+s}.

Thus there exists a well-defined epimorphism

\varpi:\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma))\to\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
.

(8.10)

\begin{split}&e_{i,j}\otimes z^{r}-(-1)^{r}e_{j,i}\otimes z^{r}\mapsto\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r},\hskip 56.9055pt\text{ for }1\leqslant j\leqslant i\leqslant N,r\geqslant\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}.\end{split}

Recall from the proof of theorem
4.1
that for
\sigma=0
, the map
\varpi
is an isomorphism. Moreover, the ordered monomials in the elements

\big\{\mathsf{e}_{i,i;2r+1}\big\}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N,r\in\mathbb{N}}\bigcup\big\{\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r}\big\}_{1\leqslant j<i\leqslant N,r\in\mathbb{N}}

are linearly independent in
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
.

Consider the canonical map
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
which sends generators to generators of the same name. It is a homomorphism of filtered algebras and hence induces an algebra homomorphism
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)\to\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
which sends
\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r}\in\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
to
\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r}\in\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}
. Therefore the ordered monomials in the elements

\big\{\mathsf{e}_{i,i;2r+1}\big\}_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N,r\in\mathbb{N}}\bigcup\big\{\mathsf{e}_{i,j;r}\big\}_{1\leqslant j<i\leqslant N,r\geqslant\mathfrak{s}_{ij}}

are linearly independent in
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
. Thus
\varpi
is an isomorphism.
∎

Thus, we identify
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
as a subalgebra of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
. Let
\nu
be another admissible shape for
\sigma
, then we have another subalgebra
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\nu}^{+}(\sigma)
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
.

Proposition 8.3
.

The subalgebras
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
and
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\nu}^{+}(\sigma)
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
coincide.

Proof.

The proof is completely parallel to that of
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
; see
[
BK06
, §3]
. We sketch the argument. Suppose
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
is a composition of
N
and
\nu
is a refinement of
\mu
, such that

\nu=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{a-1},\alpha,\beta,\mu_{a+1},\dots,\mu_{n}),

where
\alpha+\beta=\mu_{a}
. It follows from
[
BK06
, Lem. 3.1]
that
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)\subset{\mathscr{Y}}_{\nu}^{+}(\sigma)
as subalgebras of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
. On the other hand, one can show that their associated graded algebras coincide in
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
. Therefore, we must have
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)={\mathscr{Y}}_{\nu}^{+}(\sigma)
. Since the composition
(1^{N})
is a refinement of any composition
\mu
, the statement follows.
∎

Due to independence of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\mu}(\sigma)
from
\mu
from now on we will write
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
again unless we want to put particular emphasis on a choice of
\mu
.

8.2.
The canonical filtration

Recall that the canonical filtration
\mathrm{F}_{0}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\subset\mathrm{F}_{1}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\subset\mathrm{F}_{2}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\subset\cdots
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
is defined by setting

(8.11)

\displaystyle\deg_{2}s_{ij}^{(r)}=r,\quad\forall r\geqslant 1

and letting
\mathrm{F}_{d}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
denote the the span of monomials in
s_{ij}^{(r)}
of total degree
\leqslant d
.
The associated graded algebra is denoted by
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}
, and it is commutative, thanks to (
2.12
).

Suppose we are given a composition
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
of
N
.
Consider the parabolic generators
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
in Theorem
3.13
.
By (
3.1
) – (
3.1
), (
3.23
), (
3.25
) and Lemma
6.1
, we may express the parabolic generators
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
B_{b,a;k,l}^{(r)}
as linear combinations of monomials in
s_{ij}^{(t)}
of total degree
r
.
Conversely, if we define
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
and
B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}
to be of degree
r
, then by (
3.2
) and (
6.1
), each
s_{ij}^{(t)}
