is a linear combination of monomials in
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
and
B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}
of total degree
t
.
As a result, we may also describe
\mathrm{F}_{d}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
as the span of all monomials in the elements
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}
of total degree
\leqslant d
.

For
1\leqslant a\leqslant b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a}
and
r>0
, denote the images of the parabolic generators in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
by

(8.12)

\begin{split}\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}:=\begin{cases}\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},&\text{ if }a=b,\\
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)},&\text{ if }a<b.\\
\end{cases}\end{split}

Proposition 8.4
.

For any shape
\mu
,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
is the free commutative algebra on generators

\displaystyle\big\{\,\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,i}^{(2r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\cup\big\{\,\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt\cup\big\{\,\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0\big\}.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
3.13
and the fact that
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
is commutative.
∎

Now we consider the shifted twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
associated to a shift matrix
\sigma
.
Treating
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
as a subalgebra of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
, we introduce the canonical filtration on
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)

(8.13)

\displaystyle\mathrm{F}_{0}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\subset\mathrm{F}_{1}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\subset\mathrm{F}_{2}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\subset\cdots,\qquad\qquad{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)=\bigcup_{s\geqslant 1}\mathrm{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma),

by defining
\mathrm{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma):={\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\cap\mathrm{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
.
Then the natural embedding
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
is a filtered map and its induced map
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
is injective, allowing us to identify
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
as a subalgebra of the commutative algebra
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
.

Proposition 8.5
.

For any admissible shape
\mu
,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
is the subalgebra of
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
generated by

\displaystyle\big\{\,\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,i}^{(2r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\cup\big\{\,\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},r>0\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt\cup\big\{\,\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)\big\}.

Proof.

Due to (
4.7
) all of these elements in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
are identified with the elements in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
sharing the same notation by the induced map
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
.
Then the statement follows from Theorem
8.2
and Proposition
8.4
.
∎

Remark 8.6
.

A consequence of Proposition
8.5
is that one can intrinsically define the canonical filtration on
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
by setting the elements
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
to be of degree
r
, and letting
\mathrm{F}_{d}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
be the span of monomials in these elements of total degree
\leqslant d
. This definition is independent of the choice of admissible shape
\mu
. A similar statement for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
holds as well; see Proposition
8.13
.

8.3.
An alternative presentation

There is another natural way to define the shifted twisted Yangian of type AI, using the Drinfeld presentation of the twisted Yangian from
[
LWZ23
]
. This definition has already appeared in
[
TT24
, §3]
.
We will recall it here for the convenience of the reader. To avoid confusion we will call it the (
dominantly
)
Drinfeld shifted twisted Yangian
. In this subsection, we will see that these two shifted twisted Yangians are isomorphic, as algebras.
Let
A=(c_{ij})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N-1}
be the Cartan matrix of type
A_{N-1}
, and set
c_{0j}=-\delta_{j,1}
.

Definition 8.7
.

The (
dominantly
)
shifted Drinfeld twisted Yangian
associated to the matrix
\sigma
is the algebra
{}^{\textnormal{Dr}}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
over
\mathbb{C}

generated by
\{\mathsf{h}_{i,r}\}_{r\geqslant 0}
,
\{\mathsf{b}_{j,r}\}_{r\geqslant\mathfrak{s}_{j+1,j}}
, for
0\leqslant i<N
and
1\leqslant j<N
, subject to the following relations, for
r,s\in\mathbb{N}
:

(8.14)

\displaystyle[\mathsf{h}_{i,r},\mathsf{h}_{j,s}]

\displaystyle=0,\qquad{\mathsf{h}_{i,2r}=0,}

(8.15)

\displaystyle[\mathsf{h}_{i,r+1},\mathsf{b}_{j,s}]-[\mathsf{h}_{i,r-1},\mathsf{b}_{j,s+2}]

\displaystyle=c_{ij}\{\mathsf{h}_{i,r-1},\mathsf{b}_{j,s+1}\}+\frac{1}{4}c_{ij}^{2}[\mathsf{h}_{i,r-1},\mathsf{b}_{j,s}],

(8.16)

\displaystyle[\mathsf{b}_{i,r+1},\mathsf{b}_{j,s}]-[\mathsf{b}_{i,r},\mathsf{b}_{j,s+1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{ij}}{2}\{\mathsf{b}_{i,r},\mathsf{b}_{j,s}\}-2\delta_{ij}(-1)^{r}\mathsf{h}_{i,r+s+1},

(8.17)

\displaystyle[\mathsf{b}_{i,r},\mathsf{b}_{j,s}]

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\text{ for }|i-j|>1,

(8.18)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\big[\mathsf{b}_{i,k_{1}},[\mathsf{b}_{i,k_{2}},\mathsf{b}_{j,r}]\big]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k_{1}}\sum_{p\geqslant 0}2^{-2p}\big(

\displaystyle[\mathsf{h}_{i,k_{1}+k_{2}-2p-1},\mathsf{b}_{j,r+1}]-\{\mathsf{h}_{i,k_{1}+k_{2}-2p-1},\mathsf{b}_{j,r}\}\big),\qquad\text{ if }c_{i,j}=-1.

for all admissible indices
i,j,r,s
. Here by convention,
\mathsf{h}_{i,-1}=1
.

If
\sigma
is the zero matrix, then one recovers the Drinfeld presentation of twisted Yangians; see
[
LWZ23
, Thm. 5.1]
. The definition naturally extends to the symmetric Cartan matrices (i.e. simply laced types), which correspond to shifted twisted Yangians of split
ADE
type; cf.
[
LWZ25
]
. In the context of the current paper, the Drinfeld shifted twisted Yangians are associated to
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
. To obtain the
\mathfrak{sl}_{N}
version, one only needs to exclude the generators
\mathsf{h}_{0,r}
.

Remark 8.8
.

More general shifted twisted Yangians, which also include the antidominantly shifted case, are introduced and studied in
[
LWW25
]
in connection with affine Grassmanian slices. Contrary to the dominantly shifted case, the antidominantly shifted twisted Yangians are usually not subalgebras of twisted Yangians.

Proposition 8.9
.

The algebras
{}^{\textnormal{Dr}}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
and
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
are isomorphic.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.3
, it suffices to prove the statement for the case when
\mu=(1^{N})
. Again, we only sketch the proof.

First, we show that the map

(8.19)

\mathsf{b}_{a,r}\mapsto B_{a;a+1,a}^{(r+1)},\quad\mathsf{h}_{a,r}\mapsto Z_{a;a,a,a+1,a+1}^{(r+1)},\quad\mathsf{h}_{0,r}\mapsto H_{1;1,1}^{(r+1)},\quad 1\leqslant a<N,r\in\mathbb{N},

defines an algebra homomorphism
{}^{\textnormal{Dr}}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
. We need to check the relations (
8.14
)–(
8.18
) are satisfied by the images of
\mathsf{h}_{a,r}
and
\mathsf{b}_{a,r}
in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
under the map (
8.19
). The relations (
8.14
)–(
8.17
) can be verified as in
[
LWZ23
, §4–§5]
. To verify (
8.18
), we check that assuming the relations (
8.14
)–(
8.17
) then the relation (
6.23
) (which is used to obtain the Serre relations for the parabolic presentations when the particular block has size one) implies the relation (
8.18
); see also Remark
4.3
. Thus the map (
8.19
) defines an algebra homomorphism. To see this is an isomorphism, one uses the PBW theorems (
[
TT24
, Thm. 3.2(2)]
and Theorem
8.2
) to see that a basis maps to a basis.
∎

By Proposition
8.9
we can drop the prefix
Dr
from
{}^{\textnormal{Dr}}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N}(\sigma)
, and we will do so unless we want to emphasize that we are working with this particular presentation.

8.4.
Shifted twisted Yangians of type AII

The notion of parabolic presentation allows one to define the shifted twisted Yangian in a very similar way as the AI type, except that we need to assume that all parts of the composition
\mu
are even, and we will refer to such compositions as
even
.
Another major difference is that the
Z
-relations (
8.20
) for type AII are different from (
8.3
) for type AI.

In this subsection, we follow the convention (
2.9
).
Let
\sigma
be a symmetric shift matrix of even size
N
.
Recall a composition
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
of
N
is admissible to
\sigma
if
the condition in §
8.1
holds.

Definition 8.10
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
be an even composition of
N
, which is admissible to
\sigma
, which means
\mathfrak{s}_{i,i+1}=0
for
i
odd. The
(dominantly) shifted twisted Yangian of type AII associated to
\sigma
, denoted by
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
, is the associative unital algebra over
\mathbb{C}
generated by

\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\ \widetilde{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 1},\quad\{B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a+1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>\mathfrak{s}_{a+1,a}(\mu)}

subject to the relations given in Theorem
4.1
with all admissible indices together with the following relation (cf. (
7.6
))

(8.20)

Z_{a;i,i,j,j}^{(2r-1)}+Z_{a;i^{\prime},i^{\prime},j^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(2r-1)}=0,\quad 1\leqslant a<n,\penalty\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},\penalty\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a+1},\penalty\ 1\leqslant r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a+1,a}(\mu).

By Theorem
4.1
, if
\sigma
is zero matrix,
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
is exactly
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
. Clearly, we have the canonical homomorphism
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}={\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
sending the generators
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
and
B_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
to the elements of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
with same names.

Exactly the same as AI type, for
1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a}
and
r>\mathfrak{s}_{b,a}(\mu)
, define elements
B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\in{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
inductively by

B_{a+1,a;i,j}^{(r)}:=B_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\quad B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}:=[B_{b-1;i,k}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{b,b-1}(\mu)+1)},B_{b-1,a;k,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{b,b-1}(\mu))}].

By the same argument, one deduces that the definition is independent of the choice of
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{b-1}
.

The following results are parallel to Theorem
8.2
and Proposition
8.3
, and the proofs are almost identical.

Theorem 8.11
.

The monomials in

\displaystyle\big\{H_{a;2i-1,2i-1}^{(r)},H_{a;2i-1,2i}^{(2r-1)},H_{a;2i,2i-1}^{(2r-1)}\big\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},r>0},

\displaystyle\big\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\big\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},\lfloor\tfrac{i+1}{2}\rfloor\neq\lfloor\tfrac{j+1}{2}\rfloor,r>0},\qquad\big\{B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\big\}_{1\leqslant a<b<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>\mathfrak{s}_{b,a}(\mu)},

taken in some fixed linear order form a basis of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
. In particular, the canonical map
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
is injective.

Corollary 8.12
.

Up to isomorphism, the definition of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
is independent of the choice of even admissible
\mu
.

The canonical filtration for shifted twisted Yangian of AII type

(8.21)

\mathrm{F}_{0}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)\subset\mathrm{F}_{1}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)\subset\mathrm{F}_{2}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)\subset\cdots,\qquad\qquad{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)=\bigcup_{s\geqslant 1}\mathrm{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma),

can be given exactly the same way as AI type by defining
\mathrm{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma):={\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)\cap\mathrm{F}_{s}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
.
When
\sigma=0
, it follows from (
2.12
) that the associated graded
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
is commutative and, similarly, that
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
can naturally be viewed as a subalgebra of
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
.

Consider the elements of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
in Theorem
8.11
.
Denote their images in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
by

(8.22)

\begin{split}\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}:=\begin{cases}\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}H_{a;i,i}^{(r)}&\text{ if }a=b,\text{ and }i=j\text{ is odd },\\[2.84526pt]
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}H_{a;i,i-1}^{(r)},&\text{ if }a=b,\,r\text{ is odd and }i=j+1\text{ is even },\\[2.84526pt]
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}H_{a;i,i+1}^{(r)},&\text{ if }a=b,\,r\text{ is odd and }i=j-1\text{ is odd },\\[2.84526pt]
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}H_{a;i,j}^{(r)},&\text{ if }a=b,\,\,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a}\text{ and }\lfloor\tfrac{i+1}{2}\rfloor\neq\lfloor\tfrac{j+1}{2}\rfloor,\\[2.84526pt]
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)},&\text{ if }a<b.\\
\end{cases}\end{split}

The following result is an analogue of Proposition
8.5
.

Proposition 8.13
.

For any even admissible shape
\mu
,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
is the subalgebra of
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}
generated by

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i-1,2i-1}^{(r)},\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i-1,2i}^{(2r-1)},\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i,2i-1}^{(2r-1)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},r\geqslant 1\}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\cup\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},\lfloor\tfrac{i+1}{2}\rfloor\neq\lfloor\tfrac{j+1}{2}\rfloor,r\geqslant 1\,\}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt\cup\{\mathsf{f}_{a,b;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>\mathfrak{s}_{b,a}(\mu)\}.

Remark 8.14
.

It follows from Proposition
3.20
that the center of the twisted Yangian
Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm})
is contained in the center of the shifted twisted Yangian
Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma))
. In fact, for any
\sigma
we have

(8.23)

Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm})=Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)).

Let
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}
denote the twisted current algebra defined in §
2.2
,
\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}=\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma)
denote its subalgebra defined in §
8.1
, and
\mathfrak{c}_{r}
denote the subalgebra of
\mathfrak{c}
spanned by elements
xz^{s}
with
s>r
.
To establish (
8.23
) one must check the inclusion
Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma))\subseteq Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm})
and, for this, it suffices to show
S(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma})^{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}}\subseteq S(\mathfrak{c})^{\mathfrak{c}}
.
By a slight modification of the argument for
[
Mol07
, Lem 2.8.1]
one can prove that
S(\mathfrak{c})^{\mathfrak{c}}=S(\mathfrak{c})^{\mathfrak{c}_{r}}
for any
r\geqslant 0
. Picking
r
large enough so that
\mathfrak{c}_{r}\subseteq\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}
, we have
S(\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma})^{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}}\subseteq S(\mathfrak{c})^{\mathfrak{c}_{\sigma}}\subseteq S(\mathfrak{c})^{\mathfrak{c}_{r}}=S(\mathfrak{c})^{\mathfrak{c}}
.

9.
Baby comultiplication

In this section, we establish a baby comultiplication, which is an algebra homomorphism from
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)
to
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
. This provides a powerful technical tool for studying both structure and representation theory (cf.
[
BK08
]
).

9.1.
The formulation

Assume
\sigma\neq 0
and suppose
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
is the minimal admissible shape of
\sigma
.
Let
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)
be the shifted twisted Yangian associated to
\sigma
and
\mu
, where we assume
\mu
to be even for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
.
Define
\dot{\sigma}:=(\dot{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}
according to the following rule

(9.1)

\dot{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j}:=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}-1,&\text{ if }i\leqslant N-\mu_{n}<j\text{ or }j\leqslant N-\mu_{n}<i,\\
\mathfrak{s}_{i,j},&\text{ otherwise. }\\
\end{cases}

By definition,
\dot{\sigma}
is a symmetric shift matrix and
\mu
is admissible for
\dot{\sigma}
. Therefore the definition of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})
makes sense.
Due to Theorem
8.2
we have the embedding
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})
, sending
H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\mapsto\dot{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
and
B_{a;f,g}^{(s)}\mapsto\dot{B}_{a;f,g}^{(s)}
. Here and after we use
\dot{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
,
\dot{B}_{a;f,g}^{(s)}
to denote elements in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})
to avoid possible confusion.

Proposition 9.1
.

The homomorphism

(9.2)

\Delta_{R}=(1\otimes\pi_{N})\circ\Delta:{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})

sends

(9.3)

s_{ij}^{(r)}\mapsto s_{ij}^{(r)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{N}s_{ip}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{pj}-\sum_{q=1}^{N}s_{qj}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{q^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\theta_{q}\theta_{i}-\sum_{p,q=1}^{N}s_{qp}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{q^{\prime}i^{\prime}}e_{pj}\theta_{q}\theta_{i}.

Proof.

Follows from the definitions.
∎

The following is an analogue of
[
BK05
, Thm. 4.1]
for shifted twisted Yangians and will provide a powerful tool in later sections. Its proof is much more technically challenging than
loc. cit.
and will be presented in the two subsections below.

Theorem 9.2
.

Suppose
\sigma\neq 0
. Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
be the minimal admissible shape associated to
\sigma
and let
t=\mu_{n}
. The map
\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
such that

(9.4)

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}(H_{a;i,j}^{(r)})

\displaystyle=\dot{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1,\,\,\qquad\forall 1\leqslant a\leqslant n-1,

(9.5)

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}(B_{a;f,g}^{(s)})

\displaystyle=\dot{B}_{a;f,g}^{(s)}\otimes 1-\delta_{a,n-1}\sum_{p=1}^{t}\dot{B}_{n-1;p,g}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{p^{\prime}f^{\prime}}\theta_{p}\theta_{f},\quad\forall 1\leqslant a\leqslant n-1,

(9.6)

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}(H_{n;l,k}^{(r)})

\displaystyle=\dot{H}_{n;l,k}^{(r)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{t}\dot{H}_{n;l,p}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{pk}-\sum_{q=1}^{t}\dot{H}_{n;q,k}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\theta_{q}\theta_{l}-\sum_{p,q=1}^{t}\dot{H}_{n;q,p}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{q^{\prime}l^{\prime}}e_{pk}\theta_{q}\theta_{l},

is a well-defined algebra homomorphism; it is understood that the last term with superscript
(r-2)
in (
9.6
) drops if
r=1
.

We shall refer to
\Delta_{R}
as the baby comultiplication.

9.2.
Proof of Theorem
9.2
for AI type

Proof.

We assume the convention (
2.8
) in this subsection.
Let
\sigma\neq 0
. Recall from Definition
8.1
that
the defining relations for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
are precisely the relations (
4.1
)–(
4.9
) with all admissible indices together with (
8.3
). Checking the relations is trivial unless they involve
B_{n-1;f,g}^{(s)}
and
H_{n;l,k}^{(r)}
, and so it suffices to check the relations involving only the very last two or three blocks. Thus we may simply assume
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
or
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3})
.

Assume
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
. We check that the relation (
4.6
), which is (
5.33
), is preserved by
\Delta_{R}
. Recall from (
3.27
) that

Z_{i,k,l,j}(v):=\sum_{r\geqslant 0}Z_{1;i,k,l,j}^{(r)}v^{-r}=\widetilde{H}_{1;i,k}(v-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})H_{2;l,j}(v).

Its image under
\Delta_{R}
is given by

(9.7)

\Delta_{R}(Z_{i,k,l,j}^{(r)})=\dot{Z}_{i,k,l,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{Z}_{i,k,l,p}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{pj}-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{Z}_{i,k,q,j}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{ql}-\sum_{p,q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{Z}_{i,k,q,p}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{ql}e_{pj}.

Using (
5.28
), we can rewrite (
5.33
) in the following equivalent form

(9.8)

[C_{1;i,j}^{(r)},C_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]=\sum_{a=1}^{s-1}C_{1;i,l}^{(a)}C_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}-\sum_{a=1}^{r-1}C_{1;i,l}^{(a)}C_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}-(-1)^{s}Z_{1;i,k,l,j}^{(r+s-1)}.

Similarly, the image of
\Delta_{R}
on
C_{1;f,g}^{(s)}
is explicitly given by

(9.9)

\Delta_{R}(C_{1;f,g}^{(s)})=\dot{C}_{1;f,g}^{(s)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{t}\dot{C}_{1;f,p}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{pg},

thus we may compute the bracket as

\displaystyle[

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}(C_{1;i,j}^{(r)}),\Delta_{R}(C_{1;k,l}^{(s)})]=[\dot{C}_{1;i,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{pj},\,\,\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}\otimes 1+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{ql}]

\displaystyle=[\dot{C}_{1;i,j}^{(r)},\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}[\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(r-1)},\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]\otimes e_{pj}+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}[\dot{C}_{1;i,j}^{(r)},\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}]\otimes e_{ql}

\displaystyle+\sum_{p,q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{pj}e_{ql}-\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{ql}e_{pj}\big)

\displaystyle=[\dot{C}_{1;i,j}^{(r)},\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}[\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(r-1)},\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]\otimes e_{pj}+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}[\dot{C}_{1;i,j}^{(r)},\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}]\otimes e_{ql}

\displaystyle+\sum_{p,q=1}^{\mu_{2}}[\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(r-1)},\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}]\otimes e_{ql}e_{pj}+\sum_{p,q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}\otimes(\delta_{qj}e_{pl}-\delta_{lp}e_{qj})

\displaystyle=\big(\sum_{a=1}^{s-1}\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}-\sum_{a=1}^{r-1}\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}-(-1)^{s}\dot{Z}_{i,k,l,j}^{(r+s-1)}\big)\otimes 1

\displaystyle+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(\sum_{a=1}^{s-1}\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(r+s-2-a)}-\sum_{a=1}^{r-2}\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(r+s-2-a)}-(-1)^{s}\dot{Z}_{i,k,l,p}^{(r+s-2)}\big)\otimes e_{pj}

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(\sum_{a=1}^{s-2}\dot{C}_{1;i,q}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-2-a)}-\sum_{a=1}^{r-1}\dot{C}_{1;i,q}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-2-a)}-(-1)^{s-1}\dot{Z}_{i,k,q,j}^{(r+s-2)}\big)\otimes e_{ql}

\displaystyle+\sum_{p,q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\big(\sum_{a=1}^{s-2}\dot{C}_{1;i,q}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(r+s-3-a)}-\sum_{a=1}^{r-2}\dot{C}_{1;i,q}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(r+s-3-a)}-(-1)^{s-1}\dot{Z}_{i,k,q,p}^{(r+s-3)}\big)\otimes e_{ql}e_{pj}

\displaystyle-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{qj}+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{pl}.

Note that the last two terms can be collected in the summation in
(\cdot)\otimes e_{pj}
and
(\cdot)\otimes e_{ql}
, respectively.

On the other hand, we may compute the bracket first by (
9.8
) and then apply
\Delta_{R}
to the result using (
9.9
), therefore

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}([C_{1;i,j}^{(r)},C_{1;k,l}^{(s)}])=

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{s-1}\big((\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(a-1)}\otimes e_{pl})(\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}\otimes 1+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(r+s-2-a)}\otimes e_{qj})\big)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{r-1}\big((\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(a-1)}\otimes e_{pl})(\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}\otimes 1+\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(r+s-2-a)}\otimes e_{qj})\big)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(-1)^{s}\Delta_{R}(Z_{i,k,l,j}^{(r+s-1)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{s-1}(\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}\otimes 1)+\sum_{a=2}^{s-1}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}(\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(a-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}\otimes e_{pl})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{s-1}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}(\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(r+s-2-a)}\otimes e_{qj})+\sum_{a=2}^{s-1}\sum_{p,q=1}^{\mu_{2}}(\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(a-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(r+s-2-a)}\otimes e_{pl}e_{qj})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{r-1}(\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}\otimes 1)-\sum_{a=2}^{r-1}\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}(\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(a-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(r+s-1-a)}\otimes e_{pl})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{r-1}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}(\dot{C}_{1;i,l}^{(a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(r+s-2-a)}\otimes e_{qj})-\sum_{a=2}^{r-1}\sum_{p,q=1}^{\mu_{2}}(\dot{C}_{1;i,p}^{(a-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(r+s-2-a)}\otimes e_{pl}e_{qj})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(-1)^{s}\big(\dot{Z}_{i,k,l,j}^{(r+s-1)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{Z}_{i,k,l,p}^{(r+s-2)}\otimes e_{pj}-\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{Z}_{i,k,q,j}^{(r+s-2)}\otimes e_{ql}-\sum_{p,q=1}^{\mu_{2}}\dot{Z}_{i,k,q,p}^{(r+s-3)}\otimes e_{ql}e_{pj}\big).

Comparing the coefficients of
(\cdot)\otimes 1
,
(\cdot)\otimes e_{pj}
,
(\cdot)\otimes e_{ql}
and
(\cdot)\otimes e_{ql}e_{pj}
on both sides, we find that (
9.8
) is preserved by
\Delta_{R}
and hence (
5.33
) as well.

We need some preparation to show that (
8.3
) is preserved by
\Delta_{R}
.
Fix any
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{1}
. By (
4.3
), the series
\{Z_{k,k,i,j}(u)\,|\,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{2}\}
satisfies the quaternary relation (
2.12
) where
Z_{k,k,i,j}(u)
replaces the role of
s_{ij}(u)
. Together with (
4.10
) and Lemma
2.3
, the following holds for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{2}
:

(9.10)

Z_{k,k,j,i}(-u)=Z_{k,k,i,j}(u)+\frac{Z_{k,k,i,j}(u)-Z_{k,k,i,j(-u)}}{2u}.

Let
r
be an odd integer. Then

\Delta_{R}(Z_{i,i,j,j}^{(r)})=\sum_{a,b\geqslant 0}^{a+b=r}\Delta_{R}\big(\widetilde{H}_{1;i,i}^{[a]}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})\big)\Delta_{R}\big(H_{2;j,j}^{(b)}(u)\big),

where
X^{[a]}(u-c)
denotes the coefficient of
u^{-a}
in the expansion of the series
X(u-c)
. By definition of
\Delta_{R}
, the above equals

(9.11)

\begin{split}\sum_{a,b\geqslant 0}^{a+b=r}&\big(\widetilde{H}_{1;i,i}^{[a]}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})H_{2;j,j}^{(b)}\otimes 1+\sum_{p=1}^{t}\widetilde{H}_{1;i,i}^{[a]}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})H_{2;j,p}^{(b-1)}\otimes e_{pj}\\
&-\sum_{p=1}^{t}\widetilde{H}_{1;i,i}^{[a]}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})H_{2;p,j}^{(b-1)}\otimes e_{pj}-\sum_{p,q=1}^{t}\widetilde{H}_{1;i,i}^{[a]}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{1}}{2})H_{2;q,p}^{(b-2)}\otimes e_{qj}e_{pj}\big).\end{split}

The very first term is precisely
Z_{i,i,j,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1
, which is zero due to (
4.10
).
The second and third terms can be collected as

(9.12)

\sum_{p=1}^{t}\big(Z_{i,i,j,p}^{(r-1)}-Z_{i,i,p,j}^{(r-1)}\big)\otimes e_{pj}=\sum_{p=1}^{t}Z_{i,i,p,j}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{pj}=\sum_{p<j}Z_{i,i,p,j}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{pj}+\sum_{p>j}Z_{i,i,p,j}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{pj},

where the first equality follows from (
9.10
) and the fact that
r-1
is even.
We separate the last term of (
9.11
) into three parts:
p>q
,
p<q
, and
p=q
respectively:

(9.13)

-\sum_{p>q}Z_{i,i,q,p}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{qj}e_{pj}-\sum_{p<q}Z_{i,i,q,p}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{qj}e_{pj}-\sum_{p=q}Z_{i,i,q,p}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{qj}e_{pj}.

Those terms
p=q
of (
9.13
) are all zero by (
4.10
). We use the commutator relation in
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
to rewrite those terms
p<q
of (
9.13
) as

(9.14)

\begin{split}&-\sum_{p<q}Z_{i,i,q,p}^{(r-2)}\otimes(e_{pj}e_{qj}+\delta_{jp}e_{qj}-\delta_{jq}e_{pj})\\
&=-\sum_{p<q}Z_{i,i,q,p}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{pj}e_{qj}-\sum_{j<q}Z_{i,i,q,j}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{qj}+\sum_{p<j}Z_{i,i,j,p}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{pj}.\end{split}

In (
9.14
), the terms
j<q
cancel the terms
p>j
of (
9.12
), while the terms
p<j
can be collected with those
p<j
in (
9.12
) into

\sum_{p<j}\big(Z_{i,i,p,j}^{(r-2)}+Z_{i,i,j,p}^{(r-2)}\big)\otimes e_{pj},

which is zero due to (
9.10
) since
r-2
is odd.
Finally, interchanging the indices
p
and
q
in the remaining term
p<q
of (
9.14
) and it can be collected with the first term of (
9.13
) as

-\sum_{p>q}\big(Z_{i,i,q,p}^{(r-2)}+Z_{i,i,p,q}^{(r-2)}\big)\otimes e_{qj}e_{pj},

which is zero due to (
9.10
). This shows that (
4.10
), and in particular (
8.3
), is preserved by
\Delta_{R}
.

We now check (
4.4
), which can be reduced to (
5.32
), is preserved by
\Delta_{R}
. In the following proof, the indices
p,q,x
are summed from 1 to
\mu_{2}
.
We first rewrite (
5.32
) into the following equivalent form

(9.15)

[H_{2;i,j}^{(r)},C_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]=-\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}H_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}C_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{a}C_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}H_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)}.

We start with
[\Delta_{R}(H_{2;i,j}^{(r)}),\Delta_{R}(C_{1;k,l}^{(s)})]
, which equals to

[A,X]+[A,Y]+[B,X]+[B,Y]-[C,X]-[C,Y]-[D,X]-[D,Y]

where
A=\dot{H}_{2;i,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1
,
B=\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{pj}
,
C=\dot{H}_{2;q,j}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{qi}
,
D=\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{pi}e_{qj}
,
X=\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}\otimes 1
and
Y=\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\penalty\ \otimes\penalty\ e_{xl}
.
Those terms without
Y
and those with
A
can be explicitly written as

[A,X]=[\dot{H}_{2;i,j}^{(r)},\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]\otimes 1
,
[B,X]=[\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)},\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]\otimes\penalty\ e_{pj}
,
[C,X]=[\dot{H}_{2;q,j}^{(r-1)},\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]\otimes e_{qi}
,
[D,X]=[\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-2)},\dot{C}_{1;k,l}^{(s)}]\otimes e_{pi}e_{qj}
,
[A,Y]=[\dot{H}_{2;i,j}^{(r)},\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}]\otimes e_{xl}
.

We rewrite the remaining 3 terms by the commutator relations on the
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
factor.
For example,

\displaystyle[B,Y]

\displaystyle=\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{pj}e_{xl}-\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{xl}e_{pj}

\displaystyle=\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\otimes(e_{xl}e_{pj}+\delta_{xj}e_{pl}-\delta_{pl}e_{xj})-\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{xl}e_{pj}

\displaystyle=[\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)},\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}]\otimes e_{xl}e_{pj}+\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{pl}-\dot{H}_{2;i,l}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{xj}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle[C,Y]=

\displaystyle[\dot{H}_{2;q,j}^{(r-1)},\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}]\otimes e_{qi}e_{xl}-\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{xi}+\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;q,j}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{ql}

\displaystyle[D,Y]=

\displaystyle[\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-2)},\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}]\otimes e_{pi}e_{xl}e_{qj}+\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-2)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{pi}e_{ql}-\dot{H}_{2;p,l}^{(r-2)}\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{pi}e_{xj}

\displaystyle+\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{pl}e_{qj}-\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;l,q}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{xi}e_{qj}.

All of the brackets appearing above can be computed using (
9.15
), while those non-bracket terms can either be collected, or cancel certain terms in the results according to their corresponding factor in
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
.
For example, the term
\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{pl}
in
[B,Y]
can be collected, and it will cancel the term
a=0
, in the first summation of the result of
[A,Y]
,
while the term
\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{xi}
in
[C,Y]
will be the term
a=-1
in the second summation of the result of
[C,X]
. After suitable changing of indices in the summations, we list the results as follows:

\displaystyle[A,X]+[A,Y]+[B,X]+[B,Y]-[C,X]-[C,Y]-[D,X]-[D,Y]

\displaystyle=\Big(-\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}\dot{H}_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{a}\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)}\Big)\otimes 1

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(-\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}\dot{H}_{2;i,x}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}(-1)^{a+1}\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;x,j}^{(r-2-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{xl}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(-\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}\dot{H}_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s-1+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{a+1}\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,p}^{(r-1-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{pj}+\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;l,p}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{pj}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(-\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}\dot{H}_{2;i,x}^{(r-3-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}(-1)^{a+1}\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;x,p}^{(r-3-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{xl}e_{pj}-\dot{H}_{2;i,x}^{(r-2)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{xl}e_{pj}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}\dot{H}_{2;q,l}^{(r-1-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s-1+a)}-\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{a+1}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{qi}-\dot{H}_{2;q,l}^{(r-1)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s-1)}\otimes e_{qi}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}\dot{H}_{2;q,x}^{(r-3-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}-\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}(-1)^{a+1}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;x,j}^{(r-3-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{qi}e_{xl}-\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1)}\dot{H}_{2;x,j}^{(r-2)}\otimes e_{qi}e_{xl}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}\dot{H}_{2;p,l}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1+a)}-\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}(-1)^{a+1}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,q}^{(r-2-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{pi}e_{qj}

(9.16)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}\dot{H}_{2;p,x}^{(r-3-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1+a)}-\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}(-1)^{a}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;x,q}^{(r-3-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{pi}e_{xl}e_{qj}.

On the other hand, using (
9.15
), we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}

\displaystyle([H_{2;i,j}^{(r)},C_{1;k,l}^{(s)}])=-\sum_{a\geqslant 0}\Delta_{R}(H_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)})\Delta_{R}(C_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)})+\sum_{a\geqslant 0}(-1)^{a}\Delta_{R}(C_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)})\Delta_{R}(H_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{a\geqslant 0}\big(\dot{H}_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}\otimes 1+\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-2-a)}\otimes e_{pl}-\dot{H}_{2;q,l}^{(r-2-a)}\otimes e_{qi}-\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-3-a)}\otimes e_{pi}e_{ql}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt\times\big(\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}\otimes 1+\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\otimes e_{xj}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{a\geqslant 0}(-1)^{a}\big(\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\otimes 1+\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\otimes e_{xi}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\big(\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)}\otimes 1+\dot{H}_{2;l,p}^{(r-2-a)}\otimes e_{pj}-\dot{H}_{2;q,j}^{(r-2-a)}\otimes e_{ql}-\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-3-a)}\otimes e_{pl}e_{qj}\big)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{a\geqslant 0}\Big(\dot{H}_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}\otimes 1+\dot{H}_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\otimes e_{xj}+\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}\otimes e_{pl}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\dot{H}_{2;i,p}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\otimes e_{pl}e_{xj}-\dot{H}_{2;q,l}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}\otimes e_{qi}-\dot{H}_{2;q,l}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\otimes e_{qi}e_{xj}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-3-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}\otimes e_{pi}e_{ql}-\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-3-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\otimes e_{pi}e_{ql}e_{xj}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{a\geqslant 0}(-1)^{a}\Big(\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)}\otimes 1+\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)}\otimes e_{xi}+\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,p}^{(r-2-a)}\otimes e_{pj}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,p}^{(r-2-a)}\otimes e_{xi}e_{pj}-\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;q,j}^{(r-2-a)}\otimes e_{ql}-\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;q,j}^{(r-2-a)}\otimes e_{xi}e_{ql}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-3-a)}\otimes e_{pl}e_{qj}-\dot{C}_{1;k,x}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;p,q}^{(r-3-a)}\otimes e_{xi}e_{pl}e_{qj}\Big).

Collecting the terms according to the factor in
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
and changing the indices when necessary, the above can be written as

\displaystyle\Big(-\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}\dot{H}_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{a}\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)}\Big)\otimes 1

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(-\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}\dot{H}_{2;i,x}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}(-1)^{a+1}\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;x,j}^{(r-2-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{xl}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(-\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}\dot{H}_{2;i,l}^{(r-1-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s-1+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}(-1)^{a}\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,p}^{(r-2-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{pj}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}\dot{H}_{2;q,l}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{a}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,j}^{(r-1-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{qi}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}\dot{H}_{2;p,l}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}(-1)^{a}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;l,q}^{(r-2-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{pi}e_{qj}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(-\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}\dot{H}_{2;i,x}^{(r-2-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s-1+a)}+\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}(-1)^{a+1}\dot{C}_{1;k,i}^{(s+a)}\dot{H}_{2;x,p}^{(r-3-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{xl}e_{pj}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}\dot{H}_{2;q,x}^{(r-3-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,j}^{(s+a)}-\sum_{a=0}^{r-2}(-1)^{a}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;x,j}^{(r-2-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{qi}e_{xl}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}\dot{H}_{2;p,x}^{(r-3-a)}\dot{C}_{1;k,q}^{(s-1+a)}-\sum_{a=0}^{r-3}(-1)^{a}\dot{C}_{1;k,p}^{(s-1+a)}\dot{H}_{2;x,q}^{(r-3-a)}\Big)\otimes e_{pi}e_{xl}e_{qj}.

Note that some summations of certain terms have different upper bounds. After making changes of the indices by
a\mapsto a+1
or
a\mapsto a-1
, the result will be precisely (
9.16
), which shows that (
9.15
) and hence (
5.32
) is preserved by
\Delta_{R}
. This completes the verification of relations when
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
.

Assume now
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3})
. Note that (
4.9
) is obviously preserved by
\Delta_{R}
since there is only one term with non-central image in the factor of
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
and it remains to check (
4.8
).
We first consider the following shift matrix
\eta=(\hat{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}
, where the entries are given by

(9.17)

\hat{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j}:=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ if either }1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{(2)}\text{ or }\mu_{(2)}+1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N,\\
\mathfrak{s}_{2,3}(\mu),&\text{ otherwise. }\\
\end{cases}

By construction, the minimal admissible shape with respect to
\eta
is
\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})=(\mu_{(2)},\mu_{3})
.
It follows from Proposition
8.3
that we have the following chain of subalgebras of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
:

{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)\subseteq{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\eta)={\mathscr{Y}}_{\nu}^{+}(\eta)\subseteq{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}

and all generators of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
belong to
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\eta)
as well.
By our discussion above, we have proved that
{}^{\nu}\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{\nu}^{+}(\eta)\rightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{\nu}^{+}(\dot{\eta})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
is a homomorphism since
\nu
is of length 2.

Take inverse on both sides of the matrix equation (
3.2
) with respect to
\mu
and
\nu
. Since the corresponding entries in the southeastern
\mu_{3}\times\mu_{3}
corner of
S^{-1}(u)
are the same, we have
{}^{\mu}\widetilde{D}_{3;i,j}(u)
=
{}^{\nu}\widetilde{D}_{2;i,j}(u)
, for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{3}=\nu_{2}
, which further implies that

(9.18)

{{}^{\mu}}F_{2;f,g}(u)={{}^{\nu}}F_{1;f,\mu_{1}+g}(u),\qquad\forall 1\leqslant f\leqslant\mu_{3}=\nu_{2},\quad 1\leqslant g\leqslant\mu_{2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle{{}^{\nu}}\Delta_{R}({{}^{\mu}}F_{2;f,g}^{(r)})={{}^{\nu}}\Delta_{R}({{}^{\nu}}F_{1;f,\mu_{1}+g}^{(r)})

\displaystyle={{}^{\nu}}F_{1;f,\mu_{1}+g}^{(r)}\otimes 1-\sum_{p=1}^{\nu_{2}}{{}^{\nu}}F_{1;p,\mu_{1}+g}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{pf}

\displaystyle={{}^{\mu}}F_{2;f,g}^{(r)}\otimes 1-\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{3}}{{}^{\mu}}F_{2;p,g}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{pf}={{}^{\mu}}\Delta_{R}({{}^{\mu}}F_{2;f,g}^{(r)}).

This means that
{}^{\mu}\Delta_{R}
and the restriction of
{}^{\nu}\Delta_{R}
to
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
agree on
{{}^{\mu}}F_{2;f,g}^{(r)}
.

By (
3.1
), (
3.6
) and (
3.1
), we have the following matrix identities:

\displaystyle{{}^{\nu}}D_{1}(u)

\displaystyle={{}^{\nu}}S_{1,1}(u)=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,1}(u)&{{}^{\mu}}S_{1,2}(u)\\
{{}^{\mu}}S_{2,1}(u)&{{}^{\mu}}S_{2,2}(u)\end{array}\right),

(9.21)

\displaystyle{{}^{\mu}}S_{2,1}(u)

\displaystyle={{}^{\mu}}F_{1}(u){{}^{\mu}}D_{1}(u).

The formulas (
9.2
) and (
9.21
) together imply that

{{}^{\mu}}F_{1;i,j}(u)=\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}{{}^{\nu}}D_{1;\mu_{1}+i,p}(u){{}^{\mu}}\widetilde{D}_{1;p,j}(u),\quad\forall 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{2},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{1}.

Note that for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1}
, the elements
{}^{\mu}D_{1;i,j}^{(r)}
are mapped to
{}^{\mu}D_{1;i,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1
by both of
{{}^{\mu}}\Delta_{R}
and
{{}^{\nu}}\Delta_{R}
and similarly for
{{}^{\mu}}\widetilde{D}_{1;i,j}^{(r)}
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle{{}^{\nu}}\Delta_{R}({{}^{\mu}}F_{1;i,j}^{(r)})

\displaystyle={{}^{\nu}}\Delta_{R}(\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{x,y\geqslant 0}^{x+y=r}{{}^{\nu}}D_{1;\mu_{1}+i,p}^{(x)}{{}^{\mu}}\widetilde{D}_{1;p,j}^{(y)})

\displaystyle=(\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{x,y\geqslant 0}^{x+y=r}{{}^{\nu}}D_{1;\mu_{1}+i,p}^{(x)}{{}^{\mu}}\widetilde{D}_{1;p,j}^{(y)})\otimes 1={{}^{\mu}}F_{1;i,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1={{}^{\mu}}\Delta_{R}({{}^{\mu}}F_{1;i,j}^{(r)}).

This means that
{}^{\mu}\Delta_{R}
and the restriction of
{}^{\nu}\Delta_{R}
to
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
agree on
{{}^{\mu}}F_{1;i,j}^{(r)}
.

Recall that
X^{[r]}(u-c)
denotes the coefficient of
u^{-r}
in the expansion of the series
X(u-c)
.
Applying
{}^{\mu}\Delta_{R}
to the LHS of (
4.8
), we have

\displaystyle{{}^{\mu}}\Delta_{R}\Big(\big[[{{}^{\mu}}B_{1;i,j}^{(q)},{{}^{\mu}}B_{2;k,l}^{(r_{1})}],{{}^{\mu}}B_{2;f,g}^{(r_{2})}\big]+\big[[{{}^{\mu}}B_{1;i,j}^{(q)},{{}^{\mu}}B_{2;k,l}^{(r_{2})}],{{}^{\mu}}B_{2;f,g}^{(r_{1})}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle={{}^{\mu}}\Delta_{R}\Big(\big[[{{}^{\mu}}F_{1;i,j}^{[q]}(u-\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}),{{}^{\mu}}F_{2;k,l}^{[r_{1}]}(v-\frac{\mu_{(2)}}{2}),{{}^{\mu}}F_{2;f,g}^{[r_{2}]}(w-\frac{\mu_{(2)}}{2})\big]+

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\big[[{{}^{\mu}}F_{1;i,j}^{[q]}(u-\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}),{{}^{\mu}}F_{2;k,l}^{[r_{2}]}(w-\frac{\mu_{(2)}}{2})],{{}^{\mu}}F_{2;f,g}^{[r_{1}]}(v-\frac{\mu_{(2)}}{2})\big]\Big).

The argument above shows that we may replace
{}^{\mu}\Delta_{R}
by
{}^{\nu}\Delta_{R}
. Since
{}^{\nu}\Delta_{R}
is a homomorphism, it commutes with all of the brackets. Replacing
{}^{\nu}\Delta_{R}
by
{}^{\mu}\Delta_{R}
again, we have proved that (
4.8
) is preserved by
{}^{\mu}\Delta_{R}
.
∎

9.3.
Proof of Theorem
9.2
for AII type

Proof.

We assume the convention (
2.9
) in this subsection.
Let
\sigma\neq 0
.
One needs to check that
\Delta_{R}
preserves the defining relations in Theorem
4.1
when the minimal admissible shape
\mu
is of length 2 and 3. We omit the details here, since they are similar to the proof provided in the previous subsection.

It remains to check that the relations (
8.20
) are preserved by
\Delta_{R}
when the minimal admissible shape
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
is of length 2. This implies that
\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}(\mu)=\mathfrak{s}_{2,1}(\mu)=k
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. Let
\sigma_{k}
denote the corresponding shift matrix and let
\Delta_{R}^{(k)}
denote the baby coproduct with domain
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma_{k})
.

When
k=1
, the relations (
8.20
) are trivially preserved by
\Delta_{R}^{(1)}
. Hence we have a homomorphism

\Delta_{R}^{(1)}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma_{1})\rightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\dot{\sigma}_{1})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{\mu_{2}})={\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{\mu_{2}}).

For a general
k>1
, note that the following elements

\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)},\,H_{2;p,q}^{(r)},\,Z_{1;i,j,p,q}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},\,1\leqslant p,q\leqslant\mu_{2},\,r\geqslant 1\}

of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma_{k})
can be identified as the elements sharing the same notations in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma_{1})
.
By definition,
\Delta_{R}^{(k)}
and
\Delta_{R}^{(1)}
agree on these elements, which implies that (
8.20
) are preserved by
\Delta_{R}^{(k)}
. It then follows that
\Delta_{R}^{(k)}
is a homomorphism.
∎

9.4.
Some consequences

Remark 9.3
.

It can be observed from the proof that the baby coproduct
\Delta_{R}
is defined for any admissible shape
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{m})
such that
\nu_{m}=\mu_{n}
.

Lemma 9.4

(cf.
[
BK05
, Lem. 4.2]
)
.

Retain the same assumption as Theorem
9.2
.
For all
1\leqslant a<b-1\leqslant n-2
and admissible
i,j,r
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}(B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)})=\dot{B}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1,

and

\Delta_{R}(B_{n,a;i,j}^{(r)})=[\dot{B}_{n-1;i,h}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu)+1)},\dot{B}_{n-1,a;h,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))}]\otimes 1-\sum_{p=1}^{t}\dot{B}_{n,a;p,j}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{p^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\theta_{p}\theta_{i},

for any
1\leqslant h\leqslant\mu_{n-1}
.

Proof.

Recall from (
8.4
) that
B_{n,a;i,j}^{(r)}:=[B_{n-1;i,h}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu)+1)},B_{n-1,a;h,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))}]
for any
1\leqslant h\leqslant\mu_{n-1}
. By definition,
\Delta_{R}(B_{n-1,a;h,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))})=\dot{B}_{n-1,a;h,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))}\otimes 1
. Then

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}(B_{n,a;i,j}^{(r)})

\displaystyle=[\dot{B}_{n-1;i,h}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu)+1)}\otimes 1-\sum_{p=1}^{t}\dot{B}_{n-1;p,h}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))}\otimes e_{p^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\theta_{p}\theta_{i}\,,\,\dot{B}_{n-1,a;h,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))}\otimes 1]

\displaystyle=[\dot{B}_{n-1;i,h}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu)+1)},\dot{B}_{n-1,a;h,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))}]\otimes 1-\sum_{p=1}^{t}[\dot{B}_{n-1;p,h}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))},\dot{B}_{n-1,a;h,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))}]\otimes e_{p^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\theta_{p}\theta_{i}

\displaystyle=[\dot{B}_{n-1;i,h}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu)+1)},\dot{B}_{n-1,a;h,j}^{(r-\mathfrak{s}_{n,n-1}(\mu))}]\otimes 1-\sum_{p=1}^{t}\dot{B}_{n,a;p,j}^{(r-1)}\otimes e_{p^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\theta_{p}\theta_{i}.\qed

Proposition 9.5
.

The baby comultiplication
\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
is injective.

Proof.

Define
\varepsilon:\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})\to\mathbb{C}
by
\varepsilon(e_{i,j})=0
for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant t
. Then the composition

(1\bar{\otimes}\varepsilon)\circ\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})

coincides with the natural embedding
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})
, showing that
\Delta_{R}
is injective.
∎

The canonical filtration (
8.11
) on
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)
can be extended to a filtration on
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
by setting the degree of
e_{ij}\in\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
to be 1.
As a consequence of Lemma
9.4
, the baby comultiplication
\Delta_{R}
defined in Theorem
9.2
is a filtered map. By exactly the same argument in Proposition
9.5
one deduces the following result.

Proposition 9.6
.

The associated graded map
\mathrm{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}:\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
is injective.

10.
The associated graded of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
via Dirac reduction

This section is a type AII counterpart of
[
TT24
, §3]
. The latter treated the type AI. We show that the associated graded algebra of a shifted twisted Yangian with respect to the canonical filtration can be identified as Poisson algebras with a Dirac reduction of its untwisted counterpart. Our proofs shall be brief whenever we can refer to
loc. cit.
for parallel arguments.

10.1.
A Poisson presentation of
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)

Recall the definition of the canonical filtration from (
8.21
). As shown after (
8.21
) the associated graded algebra
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
is commutative.

We take a brief digression to discuss presentations of Poisson algebras. The free Poisson algebra generated by a set
X
is the symmetric algebra
S(L_{X})
of the free Lie algebra
L_{X}
generated by the set
X
. A
Poisson presentation
of a Poisson algebra
A
is an exact sequence of the form

I\longrightarrow S(L_{X})\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow 0,

where
I\subseteq S(L_{X})
is a Poisson ideal. The generators of the Poisson ideal will be be referred to as
Poisson relations
. In the sequel it will be important to distinguish between generators of
A
viewed as a commutative algebra and as a Poisson algebra, and so we emphasize this terminology whenever any ambiguity is possible. We refer the reader to
[
To23
, §3.1]
for slightly more detail. As
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
is commutative it carries a natural Poisson structure.

Lemma 10.1
.

The associated graded of the shifted twisted Yangian,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
, is Poisson generated by the elements

\{h_{a;i,j}^{(r)},\widetilde{h}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i,j,\leqslant\mu_{a},r\geqslant 0},\qquad\{b_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\}_{1\leqslant a<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a+1},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},r>\mathfrak{s}_{b,a}(\mu)}

subject to the relations:

(10.1)

\displaystyle h_{a;i,j}^{(0)}=\delta_{ij},\quad

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{a}}\sum_{t=0}^{r}h_{a;i,p}^{(t)}\widetilde{h}_{a;p,j}^{(r-t)}=\delta_{r0}\delta_{ij},\quad h_{1;1,1}^{(2r-1)}+h_{1;1^{\prime},1^{\prime}}^{(2r-1)}=0,

(10.2)

\displaystyle z_{a;i,i,j,j}^{(2r-1)}

\displaystyle+z_{a;i^{\prime},i^{\prime},j^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(2r-1)}=0,\qquad 1\leqslant r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a+1,a}(\mu),

(10.3)

\displaystyle\{h_{a;i,j}^{(r)},h_{b;k,l}^{(s)}\}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ab}\Big(\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\big(h_{a;k,j}^{(r-1-t)}h_{a;i,l}^{(s+t)}-h_{a;k,j}^{(s+t)}h_{a;i,l}^{(r-1-t)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{t}\big(\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}h_{a;i,k^{\prime}}^{(r-1-t)}h_{a;j^{\prime},l}^{(s+t)}-\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}h_{a;k,i^{\prime}}^{(s+t)}h_{a;l^{\prime},j}^{(r-1-t)}\big),

(10.4)

\displaystyle\{h_{a;i,j}^{(r)},b_{b;k,l}^{(s)}\}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ab}\Big(\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{a}}\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{t}\delta_{jl^{\prime}}\theta_{l^{\prime}}\theta_{p}h_{a;i,p}^{(r-t-1)}b_{a;k,p^{\prime}}^{(s+t)}

\displaystyle\qquad\penalty\ \penalty\ -\sum_{p=1}^{\mu_{a}}\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\delta_{il}b_{a;k,p}^{(s+t)}h_{a;p,j}^{(r-t-1)}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\delta_{a,b+1}\Big(\sum_{m=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{m+1}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\theta_{k}H_{a;i,k^{\prime}}^{(r-1-m)}B_{b;j^{\prime},l}^{(s+m)}+\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}b_{b;i,l}^{(s+t)}h_{a;k,j}^{(r-1-t)}\Big),

(10.5)

\displaystyle\{b_{a;i,j}^{(r)},b_{b;k,l}^{(s)}\}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\text{ if }|a-b|>1\text{ or if }b=a+1,\text{ and }i\neq l,

(10.6)

\displaystyle\{b_{a;i,j}^{(r)},b_{a;k,l}^{(s)}\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}b_{a;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}b_{a;i,l}^{(t)}-\sum_{t=1}^{s-1}b_{a;k,j}^{(r+s-1-t)}b_{a;i,l}^{(t)}-(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}z_{a;j^{\prime},l,k,i^{\prime}}^{(r+s-1)},

(10.7)

\displaystyle\{b_{a;i,j}^{(r+1)},b_{a+1;k,l}^{(s)}\}

\displaystyle=\{b_{a;i,j}^{(r)},b_{a+1;k,l}^{(s+1)}\}-\delta_{il}\sum_{q=1}^{\mu_{a+1}}b_{a;q,j}^{(r)}b_{a+1;k,q}^{(s)},

and the Serre relations

(10.8)

\displaystyle\big\{\{b_{a;i,j}^{(q)},b_{a+1;k,l}^{(r_{1})}\},b_{a+1;f,g}^{(r_{2})}\big\}

\displaystyle+\big\{\{b_{a;i,j}^{(q)},b_{a+1;k,l}^{(r_{2})}\},b_{a+1;f,g}^{(r_{1})}\big\}

\displaystyle=\delta_{il}((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})\theta_{k}\theta_{l}\{z_{a+1;l^{\prime},g,f,k^{\prime}}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1)},b_{a;i,j}^{(q)}\},

(10.9)

\displaystyle\big\{\{b_{a+1;i,j}^{(q)},b_{a;k,l}^{(r_{1})}\},b_{a;f,g}^{(r_{2})}\big\}

\displaystyle+\big\{\{b_{a+1;i,j}^{(q)},b_{a;k,l}^{(r_{2})}\},b_{a;f,g}^{(r_{1})}\big\}

\displaystyle=\delta_{jk}((-1)^{r_{1}}+(-1)^{r_{2}})\theta_{k}\theta_{l}\{z_{a;l^{\prime},g,f,k^{\prime}}^{(r_{1}+r_{2}-1)},b_{a+1;i,j}^{(q)}\}.

Here, we define
z_{a;i,j,k,l}^{(r)}:=\sum_{t=0}^{r}\tilde{h}_{b;i,j}^{(t)}h_{b+1;,k,l}^{(r-t)}
.

Proof.

Let
\widehat{\bf y}^{-}_{\mu}(\sigma)
be the algebra with generators and relations as in the statement of the lemma. From Theorem
4.1
and Lemma
7.6
it follows that there is a canonical surjection
\widehat{\bf y}^{-}_{\mu}(\sigma)\to\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
. We inductively define the following elements in
\widehat{\bf y}^{-}_{\mu}(\sigma)
:

(10.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{}^{\sigma}b_{a+1,a,i,j}^{(r)}&:=b_{a,i,j}^{(r)},\ \text{ for }\ 1<i\leqslant n,\ r>s_{i+1,i},\\
{}^{\sigma}b_{b,a,i,j}^{(r)}&:=\{{}^{\sigma}b_{b,b-1,i,k}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{b,b-1}(\mu)+1)},{}^{\sigma}b_{b-1,a,k,j}^{(r-(\mathfrak{s}_{b,b-1}(\mu))}\},\ \text{ for }\ 1\leqslant j<i\leqslant n,\ r>s_{i+1,j},\\
{}^{\sigma}b_{a,a,i,j}^{(r)}&:=h_{a,i,j}^{(r)},\ \text{ for }\ 1\leqslant i\leqslant n,\ r>0,\end{split}

and let
{}^{\sigma}b_{a,b,i,j}^{(r)}:=(-1)^{(r+i+j)}{}^{\sigma}b_{b,a,j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)}
for
a<b
.
From here on the proof is exactly the same as the proof of
[
TT24
, Prop. 3.4]

using the proof of Theorem
4.1
instead of the proof of
[
LWZ23
, Thm. 5.11]
and Theorem
8.11
instead of
[
TT24
, Lem. 3.3]
.
∎

10.2.
Relation to Dirac reduction

In
[
TT24
]
a certain Poisson algebra
R(^{\mu}y_{N}(\sigma),\tau)
, known as the Dirac reduction of the shifted Yangian, was introduced. This algebra depends on a parameter
\beta=\pm 1
and an involution
\tau
of
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
.

In
[
TT24
, Thm. 5.13]
a Poisson presentation for
R(^{\mu}y_{N}(\sigma),\tau)
was given in terms of generators

(10.11)

\begin{split}&\{\eta_{a;i,j}^{(r)}|{1\leqslant a\leqslant m,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\nu_{a},r>0}\},\\
&\{\theta_{a;i,j}^{(r)}|{1\leqslant a<m,1\leqslant i\leqslant\nu_{a},1\leqslant j\leqslant\nu_{a+1},r>s_{a,a+1}(\nu)}\}.\end{split}

We refer the reader to
loc. cit.
for a full list of relations.

We will need two more relations which were not recorded in
[
TT24
]
.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(0)}=\delta_{i,j}
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(r)}=-\sum_{g=1}^{\nu_{a}}\sum_{t=1}^{r}\eta_{a,i,g}^{(t)}\widetilde{\eta}_{a,g,j}^{(r-t)}
.
We then have

(10.12)

\displaystyle\{\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(r)},\theta_{b,h,k}^{(s)}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\delta_{a,b+1}\delta_{i,k}\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\sum_{g=1}^{\nu_{a}}\theta_{b;h,g}^{(r+s-1-t)}\widetilde{\eta}_{a;g,j}^{(t)}-\delta_{a,b}\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\theta_{a;i,k}^{(r+s-1-t)}\widetilde{\eta}_{a;h,j}^{(t)}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\beta^{i+j}}{2}(-1)^{r}\Big(\delta_{a,b+1}\delta_{j^{\prime},k}\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\sum_{g=1}^{\nu_{a}}\theta_{b;h,g}^{(r+s-1-t)}\widetilde{\eta}_{a;g,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}-\delta_{a,b}\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\theta_{a;j^{\prime},k}^{(r+s-1-t)}\widetilde{\eta}_{a;h,i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\Big),

(10.13)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}

\displaystyle=\beta^{i+j}(-1)^{r}\widetilde{\eta}_{a;j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)}.

Proof.

The first relation is proven exactly the same way as
[
TT24
, (5.78)]
using the relations
[
TT24
, (5.61)-(5.62)]
. For the second relation (
10.13
) we induct on
r
. The base case for
r=0
holds since
\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(0)}=\delta_{i,j}
by the definition (cf.
[
TT24
, Theorem 5.13]
). Now assume the relation holds for
l<r
. Using
[
TT24
, 5.76]
and the induction hypothesis this gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(r)}=-\sum_{g=1}^{\nu_{a}}\sum_{t=1}^{r}(-1)^{r}\beta^{i+j}\eta_{a,g^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\eta}_{a,j^{\prime},g^{\prime}}^{(r-t)}=(-1)^{r}\beta^{i+j}\widetilde{\eta}_{a,j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)},

where the last equality can be seen as follows. Define the matrix
\eta_{a}(u)\in R(^{\mu}y_{N}(\sigma),\tau)[[u^{-1}]]
where
(\eta_{a}(u))_{i,j}=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\eta_{a,i,j}^{(r)}u^{-r}
. It follows from a short calculation that
\eta_{a}(u)\widetilde{\eta}_{a}(u)=1
. Similarly letting
\hat{\eta}_{a,i,j}:=-\sum_{g=1}^{\nu_{a}}\sum_{t=1}^{r}\eta_{a,g,j}^{(t)}\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,g}^{(r-t)}
one sees that
\hat{\eta}_{a}(u)\eta_{a}(u)=1
. This gives
\hat{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(r)}=\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(r)}
, as desired.
∎

As mentioned before the algebra
R(^{\mu}y_{N}(\sigma),\tau)
depends on a parameter
\beta=\pm 1
and in
[
TT24
, Thm. 3.8]
it was proven that the Dirac reduction with parameter
\beta=1
is Poisson isomorphic to
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{+}(\sigma)
; it was conjectured that the Dirac reduction with parameter
\beta=-1
is Poisson isomorphic to
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)
, although the precise description of the latter algebra did not exist back then.

Theorem 10.3
.

We have
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)\cong R(^{\mu}y_{N}(\sigma),\tau)
for
\beta=-1
as graded Poisson algebras.

Proof.

We prove that by setting
\beta=-1
in
[
TT24
, (5.76)–(5.83)]
, we obtain the stated isomorphism.

To begin with, we prove the statement for
\mu=(2^{\frac{N}{2}})
. The general statement then follows from
[
TT24
, Prop. 5.9]
and Corollary
8.12
, by composing isomorphisms.

We define a map

\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{-}(\sigma)\to R(^{\mu}y_{N}(\sigma),\tau)
by

(10.14)

\begin{split}h_{a,i,j}^{(r)}&\mapsto 2^{r}\eta_{a,i,j}^{(r)}\ \textnormal{ for }r>0,\\
\widetilde{h}_{a,i,j}^{(r)}&\mapsto 2^{r}\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(r)}\ \textnormal{ for }r>0,\\
b_{a,i,j}^{(r)}&\mapsto(-1)^{\frac{(\mathfrak{s}_{a,a+1}(\mu)+1)}{2}}(-2)^{r}(-1)^{(i+j)}\theta_{a,j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}^{(r)}\textnormal{ for }r>\mathfrak{s}_{a,a+1}(\mu).\end{split}

It follows from
[
TT24
, (5.76)-(5.83)]
that the map is a homomorphism of Poisson algebras. Recall (
2.9
).
Then the first relation in (
10.1
) follows by definition, the second relation in (
10.1
) can be checked using the definition of
\widetilde{\eta}_{a,i,j}^{(r)}
in
[
TT24
, Thm. 5.13]
, whereas the third relation in (
10.1
) can be checked using
[
TT24
, (5.76)]
.

The relation (
10.2
) now follows by definition of
z_{a,i,i^{\prime},j,j^{\prime}}^{(2r-1)}
,
[
TT24
, (5.76)]
and (
10.13
). One can check the relations (
10.3
) and (
10.4
) by using (5.76)–(5.77) and (5.76)–(5.78)
loc. cit.
, respectively. Additionally, one can check the relations (
10.5
), (
10.6
) and (
10.7
) by using (5.81), (5.79), and (5.80)
loc. cit.
, respectively.

Finally we check the Serre relations, (
10.8
) and (
10.9
). When proving the first of these, one can apply the same argument appearing after (
4.13
) to reduce to the case
i\neq g
, and for the latter one can similarly assume
j\neq f
. One can then check the Serre relations, (
10.8
) and (
10.9
), using (
10.12
) together with
[
TT24
, (5.78), (5.82), (5.83)]
.

Using Proposition
8.13
together with
[
TT24
, Cor. 5.16]
we conclude that the map (
10.14
) sends a basis to a basis. Hence it is an isomorphism.
∎

11.
Pyramids and truncated shifted twisted Yangians

In this section, we first recall the notion of pyramids in the special case we need for both type AI and AII. We then formulate the truncated shifted twisted Yangians of both types, and establish their PBW bases.

11.1.
Pyramids

We recall the notion of
pyramid
in a rather special case which we need, which is simply a symmetrized variant of Young diagrams.
This combinatorial object can be used to both label the truncated shifted twisted Yangians, and also to label even nilpotent orbits in classical Lie algebra. This bridges the gap to finite
W
-algebras, which will be discussed later.

Suppose first that a pair
(\sigma,\ell)
is given, where
\sigma=(\mathfrak{s}_{i,j})
is a symmetric shift matrix of size
N
and
\ell>2\mathfrak{s}_{1,N}
is a positive integer. Start with a rectangular Young diagram
\Xi
consisting of
N\times\ell
boxes, where
N
is the height and
\ell
is the width of
\Xi
. We label the rows of
\Xi
from top to bottom by
1,\ldots,N
and for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
we remove
\mathfrak{s}_{i,N}
boxes from both of the left-hand-side and the right-hand-side of the
i^{th}
row of
\Xi
. The resulting diagram is the pyramid corresponding to
(\sigma,\ell)
. Note that any pyramid obtained in this fashion satisfies the following properties:

•

Height
N
and width
\ell
;

•

The number of boxes in each row are either all even or all odd;

•

The diagram is symmetric with respect to its vertical axis.

Conversely, a pyramid satisfying the properties above uniquely determines a pair
(\sigma,\ell)
as follows. Let
N
be the height and let
\ell
be the width of the pyramid, and for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
we let
\mathfrak{s}_{i,N}
denote the number
\frac{\ell-p_{i}}{2}
, where
p_{i}
means the number of boxes in the
i^{th}
row of the pyramid labelled from top to bottom. The two assignments given above are inverses of each other. Hence we have proved the following bijection.

Proposition 11.1
.

The set of pairs
\{(\sigma,\ell)\}
, where
\sigma
is a symmetric shift matrix of size
N
and
\ell
is a positive integer strictly larger than
2\mathfrak{s}_{1,N}
, is in bijection with the set of symmetric pyramids of height
N
and width
\ell
such that all
p_{i}
have the same parity.

For example,

\sigma=\left(\begin{array}[]{llll}0&0&2&3\\
0&0&2&3\\
2&2&0&1\\
3&3&1&0\end{array}\right),\,\ell=8\qquad\longleftrightarrow\qquad\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to10.39995pt{\vss\hbox to10.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.39995pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.39995pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 10.00002pt\vrule width=10.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 1733.73332pt}}$}

Remark 11.2
.

Here we follow the conventions in
[
BK06
]
regarding the definition of the level.
We warn the reader that
[
TT24
]
uses the convention that the level is the smallest part of the partition instead.

Definition 11.3
.

A partition
\lambda
is called orthogonal (resp. symplectic) if each even (resp. odd) part appears with even multiplicity.

Via shapes of Jordan blocks nilpotent orbits in orthogonal (resp. symplectic) Lie algebras are parametrized by orthogonal (resp. symplectic) partitions; cf.
[
Ja04
]
.
In this article, we focus on orthogonal or symplectic partitions
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{N})
satisfying the following additional
parity assumption
:

(11.1)

\lambda_{i}\equiv\lambda_{j}\pmod{2},\quad\forall 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N.

The condition (
11.1
) implies that the Dynkin grading associated to the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple is always even, see §
13.1
.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{N})
be an orthogonal or symplectic partition satisfying (
11.1
). We assign a unique pyramid to
\lambda
as follows. We first draw the corresponding Young diagram associated with
\lambda
in French style; that is, the diagram is left-justified and the longest row is placed at the bottom. Then we move the rows of the Young diagram horizontally to obtain a pyramid
\pi(\lambda)
which is symmetric with respect to its vertical axis in the middle.

With
\pi(\lambda)
, one obtains the corresponding pair
(\sigma,\ell)
through the bijection in Proposition
11.1
.
The pair
(\sigma,\ell)
will determine a
truncated shifted twisted Yangian
which will be defined in the following subsections, where the corresponding type depends not only on the type of partition
\lambda
but also on the (parity of) size of its largest Jordan block, which is exactly
\ell
.
The general principle of the correspondence is that the types are interchanged if and only if
\ell
is even.
To be explicit,

(i)

If
\lambda
is orthogonal and
\ell
is odd, then the corresponding twisted Yangian is of type AI;

(ii)

If
\lambda
is orthogonal and
\ell
is even, then the corresponding twisted Yangian is of type AII;

(iii)

If
\lambda
is symplectic and
\ell
is odd, then the corresponding twisted Yangian is of type AII;

(iv)

If
\lambda
is symplectic and
\ell
is even, then the corresponding twisted Yangian is of type AI.

This correspondence explains the reason why the even assumption on the composition is natural when defining the twisted Yangian of type AII by parabolic presentation.
In particular, in the rectangular case, the above correspondence matches that in
[
Br09
, Thm. 1.1]
.

In the remainder of this section, we shall deal separately with the type AI in §
11.2
–
11.3
and type AII in §
11.4
–
11.5
.

11.2.
Truncated shifted twisted Yangians of type AI

For a symmetric shift matrix
\sigma=(\mathfrak{s}_{i,j})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant N}
,
take a positive integer
\ell>2\mathfrak{s}_{1,N}
, which will be called the
level
.
For each
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
, define

(11.2)

p_{i}:=\ell-2\mathfrak{s}_{i,N}.

If
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
is an admissible shape of
\sigma
, we set the following shorthand notation for each
1\leqslant a\leqslant n
:

(11.3)

p_{a}(\mu):=p_{\mu_{(a)}}.

Note that when
\sigma
is fixed,
\ell
and
p_{1}
determine each other, and all
p_{i}
have the same parity as
\ell
.

Definition 11.4
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
be the minimal admissible shape of
\sigma
. Furthermore if
\mu_{1}=1
introduce the following power series for
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{2}

(11.4)

\mathring{B}_{1;i,1}(u):=\sum_{r>0}B_{1;i,1}^{(r+\mathfrak{s}_{1,2})}u^{-r}

and let
\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1}+1)}
be the coefficient of

u^{-(p_{1}+1)}
in the series

\mathring{B}_{1;i,1}(u+\frac{1}{2})H_{1;1,1}(u)
.
More explicitly,

(11.5)

\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1}+1)}=\sum_{t=0}^{p_{1}}\sum_{k=0}^{p_{1}-t}\binom{p_{1}-t}{k}(-2)^{-p_{1}+t+k}B_{1;i,1}^{(k+1+\mathfrak{s}_{1,2})}H_{1;1,1}^{(t)}.

Define
I_{\ell}
to be the 2-sided ideal of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
generated by

(11.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\begin{cases}\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)}+2^{-1}H_{1;i,j}^{(r-1)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},r>p_{1}\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is odd},\\
\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},r>p_{1}\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is even and }\mu_{1}\geqslant 2,\\
\{H_{1;1,1}^{(r)}\mid r>p_{1}\}\cup\{\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1}+1)}\mid 1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{2}\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is even and }\mu_{1}=1,\end{cases}\end{split}

and define the truncated shifted twisted Yangian to be the quotient

(11.7)

{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma):={\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)/I_{\ell}.

Abusing notation, we will denote elements of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
and their images in the quotient
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
by the same symbols when there is no possible confusion from the context.
Note that when
\sigma=0
, the quotient
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}/I_{\ell}
is the twisted Yangian of level
\ell
considered in
[
Br09
]
.

The following result implies that one may choose any admissible shape to define
I_{\ell}
and
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
,
and they are independent of the choice of the shape, thus our notation is unambiguous.

Proposition 11.5
.

Let
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{m})
be an admissible shape with respect to
\sigma
. Then the ideal
I_{\ell}
equals the 2-sided ideal of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
generated by

(11.8)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)}+2^{-1}H_{1;i,j}^{(r-1)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\nu_{1},r>p_{1}\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is odd},\\
\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\nu_{1},r>p_{1}\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is even and }\nu_{1}\geqslant 2,\\
\{H_{1;1,1}^{(r)}\mid r>p_{1}\}\cup\{\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1}+1)}\mid 1\leqslant i\leqslant\nu_{2}\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is even and }\nu_{1}=1.\end{cases}

Proof.

First deal with the case when
\ell
is even and
\nu_{1}\geqslant 2
.
Let
K
be the 2-sided ideal generated by

\{s_{ij}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant 2,r>p_{1}\}.

Note that
K\subseteq I_{\ell}
.
By (
2.14
), we have

[s_{12}^{(t)},s_{2k}^{(1)}]=s_{1k}^{(t)}\in K
for all
k>2
and
t>p_{1}
.
Using (
2.14
) again, we have

[s_{1k}^{(t)},s_{j1}^{(1)}]=\delta_{jk}s_{11}^{(t)}-s_{jk}^{(t)}\in K
for all
j>1
and
t>p_{1}
, and it follows that
I_{\ell}\subseteq K
.

Next we consider the case when
\ell
is odd.
Let
J
be the ideal generated by
\{\mathsf{h}_{0,r}\,|\,r\geqslant p_{1}\}
, using the Drinfeld presentation.
It suffices to show
I_{\ell}=J
, where the general case follows immediately since the generating set of
J
is contained in the generating set of the 2-sided ideal for any admissible shape.

Clearly
J\subseteq I_{\ell}
since
\mathsf{h}_{0,r}=s_{11}^{(r+1)}=H_{1;1,1}^{(r+1)}
.
We deduce some general facts regardless of the parity of
\ell
.
Since
H_{1;i,j}^{(t)}=s_{ij}^{(t)}
for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1}
, by (
2.14
) we have

(11.9)

[s_{11}^{(t)},s_{kl}^{(1)}]=\delta_{1k}(s_{1l}^{(t)}+s_{l1}^{(t)})-\delta_{1l}(s_{1k}^{(t)}+s_{k1}^{(t)}).

It then follows that

(11.10)

s_{1k}^{(t)}+s_{k1}^{(t)}\in J,\qquad 2\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{1},\,t>p_{1}.

Moreover, the symmetry relation (
2.13
) implies that

(11.11)

s_{ij}^{(2t+1)}=-s_{ji}^{(2t+1)},\qquad s_{ji}^{(2t+1)}=s_{ij}^{(2t+2)}-s_{ji}^{(2t+2)}.

By (
4.3
) and the facts that
s_{ij}^{(0)}=\delta_{ij}
,
s_{11}^{(1)}=0
, we have

[s_{11}^{(2)},s_{k1}^{(t)}+s_{1k}^{(t)}]=2(s_{1k}^{(t+1)}-s_{k1}^{(t+1)})+(s_{1k}^{(t)}-s_{k1}^{(t)})+s_{11}^{(t)}(s_{k1}^{(1)}-s_{1k}^{(1)})+(s_{k1}^{(1)}-s_{1k}^{(1)})s_{11}^{(t)},

and hence
2(s_{1k}^{(t+1)}-s_{k1}^{(t+1)})+(s_{1k}^{(t)}-s_{k1}^{(t)})\in J
for all
t>p_{1}
.
Together with (
11.10
), we have

s_{k1}^{(t+1)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{k1}^{(t)}\in J\quad\text{and}\quad s_{1k}^{(t+1)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{1k}^{(t)}\in J,\quad\forall t>p_{1}.

Suppose now
\ell
is odd.
We need to show that

H_{1;i,j}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{2}H_{1;i,j}^{(t-1)}\in J

for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1}
and
t>p_{1}
.
Since
p_{1}
is also odd, we have

s_{1k}^{(p_{1}+1)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{1k}^{(p_{1})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sijsym}}}{{=}}s_{1k}^{(p_{1}+1)}+\frac{1}{2}(s_{k1}^{(p_{1}+1)}-s_{1k}^{(p_{1}+1)})=\frac{1}{2}(s_{1k}^{(p_{1}+1)}+s_{k1}^{(p_{1}+1)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{s1k}}}{{\in}}J.

We have proved that
s_{1k}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{1k}^{(t-1)}\in J
for all
t>p_{1}
.
By (
4.3
) again, for
j>1
we have

[s_{j1}^{(1)},s_{1k}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{1k}^{(t-1)}]=(s_{jk}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{jk}^{(t-1)})-\delta_{jk}(s_{11}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{11}^{(t-1)})\in J.

If
j=k
, note that
s_{kk}^{(p_{1}+1)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{kk}^{(p_{1})}=2(s_{kk}^{(p_{1}+2)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{kk}^{(p_{1}+1)})\in J
since
s_{kk}^{(p_{1})}=s_{kk}^{(p_{1}+2)}=0
.
As a consequence, we have proved that
s_{jk}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{2}s_{jk}^{(t-1)}\in J
for all
1\leqslant j,k\leqslant\mu_{1}
with
t>p_{1}
, completing the proof when
\ell
is odd.

Now assume
\ell
is even and
\mu_{1}=\nu_{1}=1
, and let

M
be the
2
-sided ideal generated by

(11.12)

\{H_{1;1,1}^{(r)}\,|\,r>p_{1}\}\cup\{\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}+1+p_{1})}\}.

Again we have
M\subseteq I_{\ell}
since its generating set is contained in
I_{\ell}
.
Note that
M
and
I_{\ell}
are exactly the same if
\mu_{2}=\nu_{2}
, thus we only need to show
I_{\ell}\subseteq M
, assuming
\nu_{2}<\mu_{2}
.
Now use the explicit description (
11.5
) together with (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) the elements

(11.13)

[H_{2;i,1}^{(1)},\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}+1+p_{1})}]=\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1}+1)}

are in
M
for all
2\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{2}
, proving that
I_{\ell}\subseteq M
.

Finally we show that when
\ell
is even and
\mu_{1}\geqslant 2
, the ideals defined by (
11.6
) and (
11.8
) coincide.
Let
\mathcal{I}
be the ideal generated by (
11.8
) with respect to some admissible shape
\nu
with
\nu_{1}=1
and let
I_{\ell}
be the ideal defined by (
11.6
).
Note that
\mu_{1}\geqslant 2
implies that
\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}=0
, and the admissible condition forces that
\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}\leqslant\mu_{1}
.
Similar to the previous case, it is enough to take (
11.12
) as the generating set of
\mathcal{I}
.
Moreover, by (
5.3
) we have

\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}+1+p_{1})}=\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(1+p_{1})}=s_{21}^{(p_{1}+1)}={}^{\mu}H_{1;2,1}^{(p_{1}+1)},

which immediately implies
\mathcal{I}\subseteq I_{\ell}
.
On the other hand, with
\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}+1+p_{1})}=s_{21}^{(p_{1}+1)}\in\mathcal{I}
, by taking the same brackets as in previous discussions, we have
s_{21}^{(r)}=H_{1;2,1}^{(r)}\in\mathcal{I}
for all
r>p_{1}
. This suffices to imply
I_{\ell}\subseteq\mathcal{I}
since we have shown that
I_{\ell}
is generated by
\{H_{1;ij}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant 2,r>p_{1}\}
in the previous case.
∎

Corollary 11.6
.

The ideal
I_{\ell}
and the truncation
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
are independent of the choice of the admissible shape.

Even though
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is independent of the choice of shape we will write
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\mu,\ell}(\sigma)
for
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\mu}(\sigma)/I_{\ell}
if we want to emphasize the choice of a particular shape
\mu
.

11.3.
PBW bases for
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)

The natural quotient map
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
carries the canonical filtration (
8.13
) on
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\sigma)
to the truncated shifted twisted Yangian

\mathrm{F}_{0}({\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma))\subset\mathrm{F}_{1}({\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma))\subset\mathrm{F}_{2}({\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma))\subset\cdots,\qquad\qquad{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)=\bigcup_{s\geqslant 1}\mathrm{F}_{s}({\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)).

Similar to the discussion given in §
8.2
, one can declare the elements
H_{a;i,j}^{(t)}
and
B_{b,a;i,j}^{(t)}
in
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
to be of degree
t
and then
\mathrm{F}_{s}({\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma))
is the span of monomials in these elements of total degree
\leqslant s
. Due to Proposition
11.5
, this is independent of the choice of the admissible shape
\mu
.

For
1\leqslant a\leqslant b\leqslant n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a}
and
r>\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)
, by abusing notation, define elements
\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\in\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)

according to the same rule (
8.12
).
Being a quotient of
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N}(\sigma)
, the commutative algebra
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is generated by
\{\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\}
as described in Proposition
8.5
.

Lemma 11.7
.

For any admissible shape
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is generated by the elements

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,i}^{(2r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<2r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}\\
\hskip 20.0pt\cup\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}\\
\hskip 40.0pt\cup\{\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)\}.\end{array}

Proof.

We first prove the statement when
\mu=(1^{N})
.
Define
\mathfrak{g}
by the following combinatorial recipe

(11.18)

\mathfrak{g}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{so}_{M},&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ odd},\\
\mathfrak{sp}_{M},&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ even},\end{cases}

where
M
is the number of boxes in the pyramid
\pi
corresponding to the pair
(\sigma,\ell)
.
It then follows from
[
TT24
, Thms. 3.8 and 6.2]
together with
[
To23
, Prop. 4.7, (4.28)]
that there is a graded surjection

(11.19)

\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma),

where the grading on
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is given by doubling the canonical grading. The
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
here denotes the Slodowy slice
\kappa(e+\mathfrak{g}^{f})
associated to a nilpotent element with partition
\lambda
obtained from
\ell,\sigma
, where
\kappa:\mathfrak{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is induced from the Killing form. Also,
M
is the number of boxes in the pyramid associated to
\lambda
and
\mathfrak{g}
is
\mathfrak{so}_{M}
for
\ell
odd and
\mathfrak{sp}_{M}
for
\ell
even. (
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
here was denoted
S(\mathfrak{g}_{0},e)
in
[
TT24
]
.)
Furthermore, using
[
TT24
, Thm. 3.8 and (6.5)]
it follows that a generating set for
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
gets mapped to the elements in the statement of the lemma. In particular, the elements in the statement generate
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
as a commutative algebra and hence the ordered monomials in the following elements span
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\{H_{a;i,i}^{(2r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<2r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}\\
\hskip 20.0pt\cup\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}\\
\hskip 40.0pt\cup\{B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)\}.\end{array}

Now let
\nu
be any shape which is admissible for
\sigma
. It follows from Corollary
11.6
that there is an isomorphism
\varphi:{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\mu,\ell}(\sigma)\to{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\nu,\ell}(\sigma)
.
Consider the isomorphism

\operatorname{gr}\varphi:\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\mu,\ell}(\sigma)\to\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\nu,\ell}(\sigma)
(now with respect to the loop filtration).
By the proof of Theorem
8.2
and the result for
\mu=(1^{N})
it follows that a spanning set for
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\mu,\ell}(\sigma)
gets sent to the top filtered components of ordered monomials in (
11.3
) for
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\nu,\ell}(\sigma)
. In particular, the ordered monomials in (
11.3
) span
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{\nu,\ell}(\sigma)
.
∎

We claim that the baby coproduct
\Delta_{R}
defined in Theorem
9.2
factors through the quotient and induces a map

(11.24)

\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell-2}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t}).

This is non-trivial only when
\ell
is even and
\mu=(1,N-1)
is the minimal admissible shape.
In this case, compute the bracket
[H_{1;1,1}^{(2)},\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(p_{1}+1+\dot{\mathfrak{s}}_{1,2})}]
using
(
4.4
)
and the fact that
\dot{\mathfrak{s}}_{1,2}={\mathfrak{s}}_{1,2}-1
. From the result one sees the image of
\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(p_{1}+1+\mathfrak{s}_{1,2})}
under
\Delta_{R}
is contained in
\dot{I}_{\ell-2}\otimes 1
, where
\dot{I}_{\ell-2}
is the 2-sided ideal defined with respect to
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}(\dot{\sigma})
and level
\ell-2
, and our claim follows.

By the same approach explained in the paragraph prior to Proposition
9.6
,
\Delta_{R}
respects the canonical filtration and induces the following graded algebra homomorphism

(11.25)

\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}:\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell-2}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t}).

Theorem 11.8
.

For any admissible shape
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is the free commutative algebra on generators

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,i}^{(2r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<2r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\cup\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}
