\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt\cup\{\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)\}.

Moreover, the maps
\Delta_{R}
and
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}
defined in (
11.24
) and (
11.25
) are injective.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
\ell
.
The initial cases
\ell=1
and
2
follow from
[
Br09
, Thm. 2.3]
since
I_{\ell}
defined in (
11.6
) is precisely the kernel of
\kappa_{\ell}
described in
[
Br09
, (1.12), (1.13)]
.
Assume now
\ell\geqslant 3
and the statement has been proved for all positive integers smaller than
\ell
.
By Lemma
11.7
, it suffices to establish the induction step assuming
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
is the minimal admissible shape.

Set
t=\mu_{n}
and the following notation for elements in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell-2}^{+}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})

(11.26)

\begin{split}\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}:=\begin{cases}\mathrm{gr}^{\prime}_{r}\dot{H}_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1&\text{ if }a=b,\\
\mathrm{gr}^{\prime}_{r}\dot{B}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\otimes 1&\text{ if }a<b,\\
\end{cases}\end{split}

and
x_{i,j}:=\mathrm{gr}^{\prime}_{1}1\otimes e_{ij}
for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant t
.
By Theorem
9.2
, Lemma
9.4
and Lemma
11.7
, there exist polynomials
g_{a;i,j}^{(r)}
in all variables
\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}
such that
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}
maps

(11.27)

\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\mapsto\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}

for
1\leqslant a\leqslant b<n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a}
with
\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)
, and

(11.28)

\mathsf{f}_{n,a;i,j}^{(r)}\mapsto\sum_{p=1}^{t}\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{n,a;p,j}^{(r-1)}x_{p,i}+g_{a;i,j}^{(r)}

for
1\leqslant a<n
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant t
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a}
with
\mathfrak{s}_{a,n}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,n}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)
, and

(11.29)

\mathsf{f}_{n,n;i,j}^{(r)}\mapsto\sum_{p=1}^{t}\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{n,n;i,p}^{(r-1)}x_{p,j}-\sum_{q=1}^{t}\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{n,n;q,j}^{(r-1)}x_{q,i}-\sum_{p,q=1}^{t}\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{n,n;q,p}^{(r-2)}x_{q,i}x_{p,j}+g_{n;i,j}^{(r)}

for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant t
,
1\leqslant r\leqslant\ell
, where
\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{n,n;i,j}^{(0)}:=\delta_{i,j}
and
\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{n,n;i,j}^{(-1)}:=0
.

By the induction hypothesis and the PBW theorem for
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
, the following elements are algebraically independent in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell-2}^{+}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
:

\displaystyle\{x_{i,j}\,|\,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant t\},

\displaystyle\{\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{a,a;i,i}^{(2r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<2r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\},

\displaystyle\{\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\},

\displaystyle\{\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b<n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)\},

\displaystyle\{\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{n,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant t,1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,n}(\mu)-1<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,n}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)-1\},

\displaystyle\{\dot{\mathsf{f}}_{n,n;i,i}^{(2r)}\,|\,1\leqslant i\leqslant t,0<2r\leqslant\ell-2\}.

With this fact in hand, together with (
11.27
), (
11.28
) and (
11.29
), one sees that the images of the generators of
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
given in Lemma
11.7
under the map
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}
defined in (
11.25
) are algebraically independent in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell-2}^{+}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
. Therefore,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}
is injective and those generators are themselves algebraically independent in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
, completing the induction step.
∎

Corollary 11.9
.

For any admissible shape
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
, the monomials in the elements

\displaystyle\{H_{a;i,i}^{(2r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<2r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\cup\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},0<r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt\cup\{B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)\}

taken in any fixed linear order form a PBW basis for
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
.

Corollary 11.10
.

We have
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)\cong\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{e})
. Here
e
is a nilpotent element with partition
\lambda
obtained from
(\sigma,\ell)
as described in §
11.1
and

\mathfrak{g}
is defined in (
11.18
).

Proof.

Define
\mathfrak{gl}_{N,\ell}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma):=\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma)/J_{\ell}
where
J_{\ell}
is the Lie algebra ideal generated by

(11.30)

\begin{cases}\{e_{1,1}\otimes z^{r}+\theta(e_{1,1}\otimes z^{r})\mid r\geqslant p_{1}\}\ &\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ odd,}\\
\{e_{1,1}\otimes z^{r}+\theta(e_{1,1}\otimes z^{r})\mid r\geqslant p_{1}\}\ \cup\{e_{1,2}\otimes z^{\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}+p_{1}}+\theta(e_{1,2}\otimes z^{\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}+p_{1}})\}&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ even.}\end{cases}

Furthermore, define
\mathfrak{gl}_{N,\ell}[z]^{\tau}(\sigma)
similarly by replacing
\theta
with
\tau
, where
\tau:\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]\to\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]
is given by
\tau(e_{i,j}\otimes t^{r})=(-1)^{r-1+\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}}e_{j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\otimes t^{r}
. Note that both
\theta
and
\tau
can be understood as the composition of an involution on
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
given by the Cartan involution followed by conjugation by a diagonal matrix with
\pm 1
on the diagonal and sending
z^{r}\to(-z)^{r}
. In particular there exists an isomorphism between
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\tau}(\sigma)\to\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma)

induced by conjugation by a diagonal matrix on

\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
and it follows that this descends to an isomorphism
\mathfrak{gl}_{N,\ell}[z]^{\tau}(\sigma)\to\mathfrak{gl}_{N,\ell}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma)
.

It is known from
[
TT24
, Lem. 5.5.(2)]
that there exists an isomorphism
\mathfrak{gl}_{N,\ell}[z]^{\tau}(\sigma)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathfrak{g}^{e}
. Now it is also clear that there exists a canonical surjection
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N,\ell}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma))\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
in particular there exists a surjection
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{e})\to\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
. Examining the PBW basis for
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{e})
(see for example
[
TT24
, Lems. 4.1 and 4.2(iii)]
), and using theorem
11.9
, we see that this map sends a basis to a basis which concludes the proof.
∎

Recall the Slodowy slice
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
. It comes equipped with a natural Poisson structure from Hamiltonian reduction and a natural grading called the Kazhdan grading
[
GG02
]
; see §
13.1
for more details.

Corollary 11.11
.

We have
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)\cong\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
as graded Poisson algebras. Here
e
is a nilpotent element with partition
\lambda
obtained from
(\sigma,\ell)
as described in §
11.1
, the grading on
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is given by doubling the canonical grading,

\mathfrak{g}
is defined in (
11.18
), and
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
is the Slodowy slice associated to
(\mathfrak{g},e)
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality fix the shape to be
\mu=(1^{N})
. As explained in the proof of Lemma
11.7
(see
[
TT24
, Thms. 3.8, 6.2]
) there is a surjective map of graded Poisson algebras
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
. It follows from the proof of
[
TT24
, Thm. 6.2]
that the map sends the basis in Theorem
11.8
to a basis. Therefore it is an isomorphism.
∎

11.4.
Truncated shifted twisted Yangians of type AII

In the following two subsections we establish parallel results to §
11.2
and §
11.3
for shifted twisted Yangians of type AII.
Let
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
denote the shifted twisted Yangian of type AII associated to the shift matrix
\sigma
defined in Definition
8.10
with respect to the following matrix
G
as given in (
2.9
):

G=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}J_{2}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&J_{2}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&J_{2}\end{array}\right),\quad\text{where}\quad J_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}.

Definition 11.12
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
be even and minimal admissible to
\sigma
, and fix a positive integer
\ell>2s_{1,N}
.
Define
I_{\ell}
to be the 2-sided ideal of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
generated by

(11.31)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\big\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},r>p_{1}\big\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is even},\\
\big\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)}-2^{-1}H_{1;i,j}^{(r-1)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1},r>p_{1}\big\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is odd},\end{cases}

and define the truncated shifted twisted Yangian to be the quotient

(11.32)

{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma):={\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)/I_{\ell}.

We will denote elements of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
and their images in the quotient
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
by the same symbols when there is no possible confusion from the context.
The following result is the analogue of Proposition
11.5
for type AII.

Proposition 11.13
.

Let
\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{m})
be an even admissible shape with respect to
\sigma
.
Then
I_{\ell}
equals the 2-sided ideal of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
generated by

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\big\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\nu_{1},r>p_{1}\big\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is even},\\
\big\{H_{1;i,j}^{(r)}-2^{-1}H_{1;i,j}^{(r-1)}\mid 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\nu_{1},r>p_{1}\big\},&\text{ if }\ell\text{ is odd}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
K
denote the ideal as in the statement of the proposition. Similar to the type AI case, it suffices to show
I_{\ell}\subseteq K
when
\nu_{1}=2<\mu_{1}
, and the general cases follow.

Suppose
\ell
is even. By the parity assumption (
11.1
), every
p_{i}
is even and hence they all have even multiplicity since the associated pyramid is obtained from an orthogonal partition. This implies that
\mu_{1}
is even and hence
\mu_{1}\geqslant 4
.
By definition,
{}^{\mu}H_{1;i,j}(u)=s_{i,j}(u)
for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1}
. It now suffices to show
s_{i,j}^{(r)}\in K
for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1}
and
r>p_{1}
.

By (
2.14
), we have

(11.33)

[s_{ij}^{(r)},s_{kl}^{(1)}]=\delta_{kj}s_{il}^{(r)}-\delta_{il}s_{kj}^{(r)}+\theta_{i}\theta_{l^{\prime}}\delta_{i^{\prime}k}s_{l^{\prime}j}^{(r)}-\theta_{k}\theta_{j^{\prime}}\delta_{j^{\prime}l}s_{ik^{\prime}}^{(r)}.

Taking
(i,j,l)=(2,2,1)
in (
11.33
) and letting
k
run over
3\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{1}
, we have
s_{2k}^{(r)}\in K
for all
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{1}
.
Similarly, taking
(i,j,l)=(1,1,1)
in (
11.33
), we have
s_{k1}^{(r)}\in K
for all
1\leqslant k\leqslant\mu_{1}
;
taking
(i,j,k)=(1,1,1)
and letting
l
vary, we have
s_{1l}^{(r)}\in K
for all
1\leqslant l\leqslant\mu_{1}
;
taking
(i,j,k)=(2,2,1)
, we have
s_{l1}^{(r)}\in K
for all
1\leqslant l\leqslant\mu_{1}
.
Finally, taking
j=k=1
and letting
3\leqslant i,l\leqslant\mu_{1}
, we have
[s_{i1}^{(r)},s_{1l}^{(1)}]=s_{il}^{(r)}-\delta_{i,l}s_{11}^{(r)}\in K
and hence
s_{il}^{(r)}\in K
for all
3\leqslant i,l\leqslant\mu_{1}
. This completes the proof when
\ell
is even.

Suppose now
\ell
is odd. By (
11.1
), every
p_{i}
is odd and hence they all have even multiplicity since the associated pyramid is obtained from a symplectic partition. This implies that
\mu_{1}
is even and hence
\mu_{1}\geqslant 4
.
By definition, it suffices to check that
{}^{\mu}H_{1;ij}^{(r)}-\frac{1}{2}^{\mu}H_{1;ij}^{(r-1)}=s_{ij}^{(r)}-\frac{1}{2}s_{ij}^{(r-1)}\in K
for all
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{1}
and
r>p_{1}
.
This can be easily deduced from (
11.33
) similar to the case when
\ell
is even.
∎

Corollary 11.14
.

The ideal
I_{\ell}
and the truncation
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
are independent of the choice of the admissible shape.

11.5.
PBW bases for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)

The natural quotient map
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)\twoheadrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
carries the canonical filtration to the truncated shifted twisted Yangian

\mathrm{F}_{0}({\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma))\subset\mathrm{F}_{1}({\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma))\subset\mathrm{F}_{2}({\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma))\subset\cdots,\qquad\qquad{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)=\bigcup_{s\geqslant 1}\mathrm{F}_{s}({\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)).

Similar to previous discussions, we declare the elements
H_{a;i,j}^{(t)}
and
B_{b,a;i,j}^{(t)}
in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
to be of degree
t
and then define
\mathrm{F}_{s}({\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma))
to be the span of monomials in these elements of total degree
\leqslant s
. Due to Corollary
8.12
and Corollary
11.14
, this is independent of the choice of the admissible shape
\mu
.

Being a quotient of
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{-}(\sigma)
, the commutative algebra
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
is generated by the elements
\{\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\}
(actually, their image in the truncation) described in Proposition
8.13
.

Observe that the baby coproduct
\Delta_{R}
defined in Theorem
9.2
factors through the quotient and induces a map on the truncation

(11.34)

\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)\rightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,{\ell-2}}^{-}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t}).

Similar to AI type, the map (
11.34
) respects the canonical filtration, inducing a graded algebra homomorphism

(11.35)

\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}:\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)\rightarrow\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}({\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell-2}^{-}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})).

Lemma 11.15
.

For any even admissible shape
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is generated only by the following elements

(11.36)

\begin{split}&\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i-1,2i-1}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},1\leqslant r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}\\
\cup&\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i-1,2i}^{(2r-1)},\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i,2i-1}^{(2r-1)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},1\leqslant 2r-1\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}\\
\cup&\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},\lfloor\tfrac{i+1}{2}\rfloor\neq\lfloor\tfrac{j+1}{2}\rfloor,1\leqslant r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\,\}\\
\cup&\{\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)\}.\end{split}

Proof.

This can be proved by the same argument as for AI in Lemma
11.7
, except that we use the results for AII established in Section
10
in place of
[
TT24
]
.
∎

Theorem 11.16
.

For any even admissible shape
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
,
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
is the free commutative algebra on generators

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i-1,2i-1}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},1\leqslant r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}

\displaystyle\cup

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i-1,2i}^{(2r-1)},\mathsf{f}_{a,a;2i,2i-1}^{(2r-1)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},1\leqslant 2r-1\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}

\displaystyle\cup

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{f}_{a,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},\lfloor\tfrac{i+1}{2}\rfloor\neq\lfloor\tfrac{j+1}{2}\rfloor,1\leqslant r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\,\}

\displaystyle\cup

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{f}_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)\}.

Moreover, the maps
\Delta_{R}
and
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}
defined in (
11.34
) and (
11.35
) are injective.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
11.8
.
∎

Corollary 11.17
.

For any even admissible shape
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
, the monomials in the elements

\displaystyle\{H_{a;2i-1,2i-1}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},1\leqslant r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}

\displaystyle\cup

\displaystyle\{H_{a;2i-1,2i}^{(2r-1)},\,\,H_{a;2i,2i-1}^{(2r-1)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\tfrac{\mu_{a}}{2},1\leqslant 2r-1\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\}

\displaystyle\cup

\displaystyle\{H_{a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a\leqslant n,1\leqslant j<i\leqslant\mu_{a},\lfloor\tfrac{i+1}{2}\rfloor\neq\lfloor\tfrac{j+1}{2}\rfloor,1\leqslant r\leqslant p_{a}(\mu)\,\}

\displaystyle\cup

\displaystyle\{B_{b,a;i,j}^{(r)}\,|\,1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n,1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{b},1\leqslant j\leqslant\mu_{a},\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)<r\leqslant\mathfrak{s}_{a,b}(\mu)+p_{a}(\mu)\}

taken in any fixed linear order form a basis for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
.

Corollary 11.18
.

We have
\operatorname{gr}{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)\cong\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{e})
. Here
e
is a nilpotent element with partition
\lambda
obtained from
(\sigma,\ell)
as described in §
11.1
;
M
is the number of boxes in the pyramid associated to
e,\ell
, and

\mathfrak{g}
is defined according to the following combinatorial recipe:

(11.37)

\mathfrak{g}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{so}_{M},&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ even},\\
\mathfrak{sp}_{M},&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ odd}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Fix the shape
\mu=(2^{\frac{N}{2}})
.
The proof is completely parallel to the proof of Corollary
11.10
with the following differences:
J_{\ell}
in this case is given by

(11.38)

\{e_{i,j}\otimes z^{r}+\theta(e_{i,j}\otimes z^{r})\mid r\geqslant p_{1}\ ,1\leqslant i,j\leqslant 2\},

and
\tau(e_{i,j}\otimes z^{r})=(-1)^{i+j+r-1+\mathfrak{s}_{i,j}}e_{j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\otimes z^{r}
in this case. The involutions on
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}
are no longer represented by conjugation by a diagonal matrix but by conjugation by a block diagonal matrix consisting of
2\times 2
antisymmetric matrices instead. We can still find an isomorphism
\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\tau}(\sigma)\to\mathfrak{gl}_{N}[z]^{\theta}(\sigma)
this time induced by conjugation by a block diagonal matrix with all blocks being
2\times 2
. Well definedness follows because we assumed
\sigma
was admissible for
(2^{\frac{N}{2}})
. Everything else in the proof remains the same.
∎

Corollary 11.19
.

We have
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)\cong\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
as graded Poisson algebras, where the grading on
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is given by doubling the canonical grading. Here
e
is a nilpotent element with partition
\lambda
obtained from
(\sigma,\ell)
as described in §
11.1
,
\mathfrak{g}
is as in (
11.37
)
and
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
is the Slodowy slice associated to
(\mathfrak{g},e)
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality fix the shape to be
\mu=(2^{\frac{N}{2}})
. The proof is then exactly the same as the one for Corollary
11.11
replacing the use of
[
TT24
, Thm. 3.6]
with Theorem
10.3
and the definition of the truncation ideal with the corresponding Definition
11.31
in type AII. Existence of the map follows just as in the type AI case.
∎

12.
Centers of the truncated shifted twisted Yangians

In this section, under some mild assumptions on
N
and
\ell
(
12.8
), we prove that the centers of truncated shifted twisted Yangians
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
are polyonomial algebras in distinguished generators, and conjecture that this statement on the centers holds without these assumptions. The conjecture can be rephrased as the conjectural existence of a Pfaffian generator in the centers of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
in the cases excluded from (
12.8
), and we construct a Pfaffian generator in a special case.

12.1.
Baby coproduct and sdet

We take a digression to prepare statements that will be used.
In general the shift homomorphism
\psi_{m}
from Lemma
3.6
is only an algebra homomorphism for extended twisted Yangians rather than for twisted Yangians. Thus for given
1\leqslant a\leqslant n
, the subalgebra generated by coefficients of
H_{a;i,j}(u)
for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a}
is a quotient of the extended twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{X}}_{\mu_{a}}^{\pm}
. Hence one can define the Sklyanin determinant for
H_{a}(u)
. We would like to calculate the image of
{\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{a}(u)
under the baby coproduct from Theorem
9.2
.

By slightly modifying
H_{a}(u)
in each diagonal block, we can obtain a smaller twisted Yangian. We consider this type by type.

Proposition 12.1
.

Consider
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{N}
of type AI.
Given
1\leqslant a<n
and
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mu_{a}
, the subalgebra of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
generated by
Z_{a;i,i,j,k}^{(r)}
for
1\leqslant j,k\leqslant\mu_{a+1}
and
r\geqslant 1
is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu_{a+1}}^{+}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be the subalgebra of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{+}
generated by
Z_{a;i,i,j,k}^{(r)}
for
1\leqslant j,k\leqslant\mu_{a+1}
and
r\geqslant 1
.

The relation (
4.3
) implies that
[H_{a;i,i}(v),H_{a+1;j,k}(u)]=0
and
H_{a+1;j,k}(u)
satisfy the quaternary relations (
2.12
). Thus by (
3.27
),
Z_{a;i,i,j,k}(u)
also satisfy the quaternary relations (
2.12
). It follows from Lemma
2.3
and (
4.10
) that
Z_{a;i,i,j,k}(u)
also satisfy the symmetry relations (
2.13
). Therefore, we have a surjective algebra homomorphism
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu_{a+1}}^{+}\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{A}
. Therefore, it suffices to show the homomorphism is injective. This follows from the proof of Theorem
4.1
as their associated graded algebras (with respect to the loop filtration) have the same size.
∎

Recall
\gamma^{-}_{c}(u)
from (
2.24
).

Proposition 12.2
.

Consider
{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{N}
of type AII. Given
0\leqslant a<n
, the map

s_{ij}(u)\mapsto\gamma_{-\mu_{(a)}}^{-}(u)\,\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)+j}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)+i}}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{(a)}}{2}),\qquad 1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a+1}

defines an algebra homomorphism
{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{\mu_{a+1}}\to{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{N}
. Moreover, the subalgebra of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{N}
generated by the coefficients of
\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)+j}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)+i}}(u)
for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a+1}
is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{\mu_{a+1}}
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from
[
Mol07
, Prop. 4.1.10]
. Thus the subalgebra of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{N}
generated by the coefficients of
\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)+j}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)+i}}(u)
for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant\mu_{a+1}
is a quotient of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{-}_{\mu_{a+1}}
. In order to show that this is an isomorphism, it suffices to show that their associated graded algebras under the loop filtration are isomorphic.

Note that it follows from Lemma
3.7
and Lemma
3.19
that

(12.1)

\begin{split}\gamma_{-\mu_{(a)}}^{-}(u)\,\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)+j}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)+i}}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{(a)}}{2})&=\gamma^{-}_{-\mu_{(a)}}(u)\,\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{(a)}}{2})\psi_{\mu_{(a)}}(s_{ij}(u))\\
&=\gamma^{-}_{-\mu_{(a)}}(u)\,\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{(a)}}{2})H_{a;i,j}(u).\end{split}

Similar to the proof of
[
Mol07
, Thm 2.8.2]
, the graded image of the coefficient of
u^{-r-1}
in the series
\gamma^{-}_{-\mu_{(a)}}(u)\,\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}(u+\tfrac{\mu_{(a)}}{2})H_{a;i,j}(u)
is given by

(e_{\mu_{(a)}+i,\mu_{(a)}+j}-(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}e_{\mu_{(a)}+j^{\prime},\mu_{(a)}+i^{\prime}})\otimes z^{r}+\delta_{ij}(e_{11}+\cdots+e_{\mu_{(a)},\mu_{(a)}})(1-(-1)^{r})\otimes z^{r}.

On the other hand, the graded image of the coefficient of
u^{-r-1}
in the series
s_{ij}(u)
is given by
(e_{i,j}-(-1)^{r}\theta_{i}\theta_{j}e_{j^{\prime},i^{\prime}})\otimes z^{r}
. Thus the claim follows.
∎

Recall
\Delta_{R}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\sigma)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mu}^{\pm}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
from Theorem
9.2
.

Proposition 12.3
.

We have

\Delta_{R}\big({\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{n}(u)\big)={\mathrm{sdet}}\,\dot{H}_{n}(u)\otimes\pi_{t}\big({\mathrm{qdet}}\,T(u)\,{\mathrm{qdet}}\,T(-u+t-1)\big).

Proof.

We first prove it for type AI with the help of Proposition
12.1
. Denote by
s^{*}_{ij}(u):=Z_{n-1;1,1,i,j}(u)
, then it follows from Proposition
12.1
that the series
s^{*}_{ij}(u)
for
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant t
satisfy the defining relations of twisted Yangian
{\mathscr{Y}}_{t}^{+}
.

Recall from (
3.27
) that
s^{*}_{ij}(u)=\widetilde{H}_{n-1;1,1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{n-1}}{2})H_{n;i,j}(u)
. By (
9.4
) we have

\Delta_{R}\big(\widetilde{H}_{n-1;1,1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{n-1}}{2})\big)=\widetilde{H}_{n-1;1,1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{n-1}}{2})\otimes 1.

By (
9.6
), we have

\Delta_{R}\big(H_{n;i,j}(u)\big)=\sum_{p,q=1}^{t}H_{n;q,p}(u)\otimes(\delta_{qi}-e_{qi}u^{-1})(\delta_{pj}+e_{p,j}u^{-1}).

Thus

\Delta_{R}\big(s^{*}_{i,j}(u)\big)=\sum_{p,q=1}^{t}s^{*}_{q,p}(u)\otimes(\delta_{qi}-e_{qi}u^{-1})(\delta_{pj}+e_{p,j}u^{-1}).

This implies that the action of
\Delta_{R}
on
s^{*}_{ij}(u)
is equivalent to the composition of the coproduct for the twisted Yangian (generated by
s^{*}_{ij}(u)
) and
\mathrm{id}\otimes\pi_{t}
, where
\pi_{t}
is the evaluation homomorphism for the Yangian
\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{t})
. Thus if we set
S^{*}(u)=(s^{*}_{ij}(u))
, then by (
2.26
) we find that

\Delta_{R}\big({\mathrm{sdet}}\,S^{*}(u)\big)={\mathrm{sdet}}\,S^{*}(u)\otimes\pi_{t}\big({\mathrm{qdet}}\,T(u)\,{\mathrm{qdet}}\,T(-u+t-1)\big).

By (
9.4
) and the fact that
[\widetilde{H}_{n-1;1,1}(v),H_{n;i,j}(u)]=0
, we can move all
\widetilde{H}_{n-1;1,1}(u)
(possibly with
u
shifted) in
{\mathrm{sdet}}S^{*}(u)
to the left and cancel. Since
\Delta_{R}\big(\widetilde{H}_{n-1;1,1}(v)\big)=\widetilde{H}_{n-1;1,1}(v)\otimes 1
, it only affects the first factor of the tensor product. Thus we obtain the desired formula.

For type AII, the argument is almost the same by using Proposition
12.2
and the fact that

[\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}(v),H_{a;i,j}(u)]=0,

which follows from the first equality of Proposition
3.20
and (
4.3
). Note that with (
12.1
), one needs to replace
\widetilde{H}_{n-1;1,1}(v)
in type AI by
\mathcal{S}_{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}^{12\cdots\mu_{(a)}}(v)
in type AII.
∎

12.2.
Miura transform and sdet

Throughout this subsection we work with the minimal admissible shape
\mu=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})
for
\sigma
. We will write
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)
and, as before, whenever
\pm
or
\mp
appears, the upper sign will correspond to type AI and the lower sign to type AII. For type AI we will use the presentation from Theorem
4.1
following (
2.8
), while for type AII following (
2.9
).
Recall the respective evaluation homomorphisms
\pi_{N}:\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})\to\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
and
\xi^{\pm}_{N}:{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}\to\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{\pm}_{N})
from (
2.2
) and (
2.19
), respectively. By
[
Mol07
,
Br09
]
we can identify
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,1}
with
\xi^{\pm}_{N}({\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm})
. Let
p=(p_{1},\dots,p_{N})
be the row lengths of the pyramid
\pi=\pi(\lambda)
associated with the pair
(\sigma,\ell)
starting from the top and define

(12.2)

\tilde{p}_{i}:=\begin{cases}\frac{p_{i}-1}{2},&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ odd}\\
\frac{p_{i}}{2},&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ even}.\end{cases}

In particular, let
s=\tilde{p}_{N}
.
Let
q=(q_{1},q_{2},\dots,q_{\ell})
denote the column heights of the pyramid
\pi
from left to right. As
\pi
is symmetric we only record the heights of the columns to the right about the symmetry, thus we define the following shorthand notation for
0\leqslant i\leqslant s
:

(12.3)

\tilde{q}_{i}:=\begin{cases}q_{\frac{\ell+1}{2}+i}\ ,&\textnormal{for }0\leqslant i\leqslant s\textnormal{ and }\ell\textnormal{ odd }\\
q_{\frac{\ell}{2}+i}\ ,&\textnormal{for }1\leqslant i\leqslant s\textnormal{ and }\ell\textnormal{ even }\\
0,\,&\textnormal{for }i=0\textnormal{ and }\ell\textnormal{ even}.\end{cases}

Analogously to
[
BK06
]
and
[
Br09
]
we define the
Miura transform

(12.4)

\chi^{\pm}:{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)\to\begin{cases}\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{N}^{\pm})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{\tilde{q}_{1}})\otimes\dots\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}),&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ odd }\\
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{\tilde{q}_{2}})\otimes\dots\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}),&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ even},\end{cases}

as the baby comultiplication applied
s
times. Note that in the first case we have used the fact that
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,1}
can be canonically identified with
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{\pm}_{N})
as the image of the evaluation map for the twisted Yangian by
[
Br09
]
. Furthermore, note that
\chi^{\pm}
is injective by Theorems
11.8
and
11.16
.

By Theorem
2.10
, the even coefficients
c_{2},c_{4},\dots
of
\mathcal{C}_{N}(u)={\mathrm{sdet}}\,S_{N}(u)
are algebraically independent generators for the center of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N}
. By Proposition
3.20
these coefficients also lie in

{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)
.
Furthermore we will write
\mathcal{B}_{N}(u):=\textnormal{qdet}\,T_{N}(u)\in\mathrm{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
.
From now on by slight abuse of notation we will also write
\mathcal{C}_{N}(u)
for the image of
\mathcal{C}_{N}(u)
in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
and
\mathcal{B}_{N}(u)
for the image of
\mathcal{B}_{N}(u)
under the evaluation map in
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
.

Lemma 12.4
.

We have

(12.5)

\begin{split}\chi^{\pm}(\mathcal{C}_{N}(u))=\mathcal{C}_{\tilde{q}_{0}}(u)&\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(u-\tfrac{N-\tilde{q}_{1}}{2})\mathcal{B}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(-u+\tfrac{N+\tilde{q}_{1}}{2}-1)\\
\otimes\cdots&\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(u-\tfrac{N-\tilde{q}_{s}}{2})\mathcal{B}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(-u+\tfrac{N+\tilde{q}_{s}}{2}-1).\end{split}

Proof.

Recall the decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)={\mathrm{sdet}}\,D_{1}(u-\mu_{(0)})\,{\mathrm{sdet}}\,D_{2}(u-\mu_{(1)})\cdots{\mathrm{sdet}}\,D_{n}(u-\mu_{(n-1)})

from Proposition
3.20
. Using (
3.23
) this can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\mathrm{sdet}}\,S(u)={\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(0)}}{2})\,{\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{2}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(1)}}{2})\cdots{\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{n}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(n-1)}}{2}).

It thus follows by Proposition
12.3
and the definition of
\Delta_{R}
that we have

(12.6)

\begin{split}\Delta_{R}\big(\mathcal{C}_{N}(u)\big)&=\Delta_{R}\Big({\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(0)}}{2})\,{\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{2}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(1)}}{2})\cdots{\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{n}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(n-1)}}{2})\Big)\\
&=\Big(\big({\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(0)}}{2})\,{\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{2}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(1)}}{2})\cdots{\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{n-1}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(n-2)}}{2})\big)\otimes 1\Big)\\
&\quad\times\Big({\mathrm{sdet}}\,H_{n}(u-\tfrac{\mu_{(n-1)}}{2})\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(u-\tfrac{N-\tilde{q}_{s}}{2})\mathcal{B}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(-u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{s}+N}{2}-1)\Big)\\
&=\mathcal{C}_{N}(u)\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(u-\tfrac{N-\tilde{q}_{s}}{2})\mathcal{B}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(-u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{s}+N}{2}-1),\end{split}

where we have used the facts that
\mu_{n}=\tilde{q}_{s}
and
\mu_{(n-1)}=N-\tilde{q}_{s}
. The statement of the lemma then follows by applying this result repeatedly, along with the definition of the Miura transform.
∎

12.3.
Description of central elements

In this subsection we provide an explicit description of algebraically independent elements of the center
Z({\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma))
of
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
.

Proposition 12.5
.

Let
M=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}
and let
m
be such that
M=2m
or
M=2m+1
.
The series

(12.7)

\begin{split}Z_{M}(u):=\begin{cases}\prod_{i=1}^{s}\prod_{j=1}^{\tilde{q}_{i}}\Big((\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{i}+1}{2}-j)^{2}-u^{2}\Big)&\\
\quad\quad\quad\times\prod_{i=-k}^{k}(u+\rho^{\pm}_{i})\Big(\gamma_{N}^{\pm}(u+\tfrac{N-1}{2})\Big)^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{N}(u+\tfrac{N-1}{2}),&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ odd}\\
\prod_{i=1}^{s}\prod_{j=1}^{\tilde{q}_{i}}\Big((\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{i}+1}{2}-j)^{2}-u^{2}\Big)\ \mathcal{C}_{N}(u+\tfrac{N-1}{2}),&\textnormal{ for }\ell\textnormal{ even}\end{cases}\end{split}

is a polynomial
Z_{M}(u)=u^{M}+z_{1}u^{M-1}+\ldots+z_{M}
of degree
M
in
u
with coefficients in
{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
. Moreover, the coefficients
z_{2},z_{4},\ldots,z_{2m}
are algebraically independent elements of
Z({\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma))
, of canonical degree
2,4,\ldots,2m
, respectively.

Proof.

It is known from
[
Mol07
, Thm. 7.1.1]
that the series

\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{N}(u):=u(u-1)\dots(u-N+1)\pi_{N}(\mathcal{B}_{N}(u))

is a polynomial
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{N}(u)=u^{N}+\tilde{b}_{1}u^{N-1}+\ldots+\tilde{b}_{N}
of degree
N
in
u
.
Moreover, the coefficients
\tilde{b}_{1},\dots,\tilde{b}_{N}
are algebraically independent generators of
Z(\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N}))
. The analogous result for
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}

[
Mol07
, Thm. 7.1.6]
states the following:

\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}(u):=\prod_{i=-k}^{k}(u+\rho^{\pm}_{i})\,\Big(\gamma_{N}^{\pm}\big(u+\tfrac{N-1}{2}\big)\Big)^{-1}\,\xi_{N}^{\pm}\Big(\mathcal{C}_{N}\big(u+\tfrac{N-1}{2}\big)\Big)

is a polynomial
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}(u)=u^{N}+\tilde{c}_{1}u^{N-1}+\ldots+\tilde{c}_{N}
of degree
N
in
u
.
Moreover, denoting
N=2n+1
or
N=2n
, the coefficients
\tilde{c}_{2},\ldots,\tilde{c}_{2n}
are algebraically independent elements of
Z(\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}^{\pm}_{N}))
, and each
c_{i}
lies in
\mathbb{C}[\tilde{c}_{2},\ldots,\tilde{c}_{2n}]
. We define, for
i=1,\ldots,n
,

\rho^{+}_{-i}=-\rho^{+}_{i}=\begin{cases}i-1,&\textnormal{ for }N=2n,\\
i-\frac{1}{2},&\textnormal{ for }N=2n+1,\end{cases}\qquad\rho^{-}_{-i}=-\rho^{-}_{i}=i.

Further set
\rho_{0}=\frac{1}{2}
if
N=2n+1
.
By Lemma
12.4
, we have

\displaystyle\chi^{\pm}(Z_{M}(u))=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{q}_{0}}(u)\otimes\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}{2})\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(-u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}{2})\otimes\dots\otimes\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{s}-1}{2})\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(-u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{s}-1}{2}),&\textnormal{for }\ell\textnormal{ odd}\\
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}{2})\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(-u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}{2})\otimes\dots\otimes\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{s}-1}{2})\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{q}_{s}}(-u+\tfrac{\tilde{q}_{s}-1}{2}),&\textnormal{for }\ell\textnormal{ even}.\end{cases}

As
\chi^{\pm}
is injective the algebraic independence then follows from the previously mentioned
[
Mol07
, Thms. 7.1.1 & 7.1.6]
.
The statement about the canonical degrees follows from definition of
\mathcal{C}_{N}(u)
.
∎

The following is the main result of this section.

Theorem 12.6
.

Assume we are in one of the following cases:

(12.8)

\begin{cases}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{+}(\sigma)\ \text{ and }\ N\text{ is odd, }\\
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{+}(\sigma)\ \text{ and \ both of }N,\ell\text{ are even, }\\
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)\ \text{ and }\ \ell\text{ is odd.}\end{cases}

Then
Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma))

is freely generated by the coefficients
z_{2},\dots,z_{2m}
. Furthermore, these elements lift a complete collection of homogeneous generators of the Poisson center of the Slodowy slice, via the isomorphisms of Corollaries
11.11
and
11.19
.

Before proving this theorem we need to recall some classical invariant theory from
[
Ja04
, §7]
.
Let
x\in\mathfrak{gl}_{M}
and consider the characteristic polynomial

\displaystyle q(x,t)=\det(t\ {\mathrm{id}}-x)=t^{M}+\sum_{i=1}^{M}q_{i}(x)t^{M-i}\in\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{gl}_{M}]^{\operatorname{GL}_{M}}.

In fact by
[
Ja04
, Prop. 7.9]
the coefficients
q_{i}(x)
are homogeneous polynomials of degree
i
in
\mathfrak{gl}_{M}
and are algebraically independent generators of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{gl}_{M}]^{\operatorname{GL}_{M}}
. Now let
\mathfrak{g}
be one of
\mathfrak{so}_{2m+1}
or
\mathfrak{sp}_{2m}
. Then it is known
[
Ja04
, Prop. 7.9]
that the restriction maps

\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{gl}_{2m+1}]^{\operatorname{GL}_{2m+1}}\to\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{so}_{2m+1}]^{\operatorname{SO}_{2m+1}}
and
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{gl}_{2m}]^{\operatorname{GL}_{2m}}\to\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{sp}_{2m}]^{\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}}
are surjective and that the kernel is generated by the
q_{2i+1}
. Consequently in these cases
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]^{G}
is generated by
{q_{2i}}_{|{\mathfrak{g}}}
.

If we fix a choice of
G
-equivariant isomorphism
\mathfrak{g}\cong\mathfrak{g}^{*}
then we can use this to transfer the natural Poisson structure on
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]
to a Poisson structure on
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]
. Also note that the Hamiltonian derivations of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]
are generated by
\operatorname{ad}^{*}(\mathfrak{g})
as a
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]
-module. Since
G
is connected, it follows that
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]^{G}
coincides with the Poisson center of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]
. We will denote the Poisson center of a Poisson algebra
A
by
Z(A)
.

Proof of Theorem
12.6
.

First note from Proposition
12.5
that
z_{2},\dots,z_{2m}
are algebraically independent.
So it remains to prove that
z_{2},\dots,z_{2m}
generate
Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma))
.
By the footnote to
[
PrJI
, Question 5.1]
(see also
[
AM21
, Theorem 11.1]
for a more detailed proof), the Kazhdan graded map
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]^{G}\to\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
restricts to an isomorphism

\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]^{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}])
.

By the definition of the Kazhdan grading (see §
13.1
, for instance) we see that for Poisson central elements, the PBW degree coincides with twice the Kazhdan degree. In particular in view of the previously discussed invariant theory
Z(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}])
for
\mathfrak{g}
one of
\mathfrak{so}_{2m+1},\mathfrak{sp}_{2m}
is freely generated by
m
elements of Kazhdan degree
4,8,12,\dots,4m
.

We know from Corollaries
11.11
and
11.19
that we have an isomorphism
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
where in the cases we are considering
\mathfrak{g}
is one of
\mathfrak{so}_{2m+1},\mathfrak{sp}_{2m}
. By
[
To23
, Prop. 4.7]
this isomorphism is graded if we equip
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
with the doubled canonical grading and
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
with the Kazhdan grading. In particular
Z(\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma))
is freely generated by
m
elements in canonical degrees
2,4,\dots,2m
.

Now by Proposition
12.5
the top graded components of the elements
z_{2},z_{4},\ldots,z_{2m}
are algebraically independent Poisson central elements in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
of the correct canonical degrees so that they must generate the whole Poisson center. In particular, these elements generate the center of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
.
∎

12.4.
Pfaffian generators

Throughout this subsection let
N
be even, and let
M=2m
be the number of boxes in the pyramid.
As we have seen in the above subsection, the elements
z_{2},\dots z_{2m}
generate the center of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
if we are in one of the cases (
12.8
). In fact, one can easily check that the cases (
12.8
) correspond precisely to the truncated shifted twisted Yangians such that
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is identified with a Slodowy slice in a classical Lie algebra of type
B
or
C
.

If
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}_{2m}
has type
D
then one still knows that the map
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}]\to\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
gives an isomorphism of Poisson centers. In this case, it is well known from classical invariant theory
[
Ja04
, §7.7]
that the restrictions of the coefficients of the characteristic polynomial from
\mathfrak{gl}_{2m}
to
\mathfrak{so}_{2m}
do not generate the whole center. Instead the restriction of the coefficient
q_{2m}
to
\mathfrak{so}_{2m}
admits a square root
\mathpzc{Pf}
called the
Pfaffian
. It is not hard to see that the Pfaffian
\mathpzc{Pf}|_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}
can never appear as the top graded term of an element of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N}^{\pm}(\sigma)
constructed from the Sklyanin determinant.

We introduce the following definition.

Definition 12.7
.

An element
\mathpzc{Pf}\in{\mathscr{Y}}^{\pm}_{N,\ell}(\sigma)
is called a
Pfaffian generator
if it admits the following three properties:

•

\mathpzc{Pf}
is central,

•

\mathpzc{Pf}
has canonical degree
m
,

•

The elements
z_{2},\dots z_{2m-2},\mathpzc{Pf}
are algebraically independent.

Conjecture 12.8
.

A Pfaffian generator exists if (and only if) one of the following cases holds:

(1)

{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{+}(\sigma)
with
N
even and
\ell
odd,

(2)

{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
with
\ell
even.

Note that by Theorem
12.6
a Pfaffian generator can only exist in the cases not mentioned in (
12.8
). Thus the “only if” part of the conjecture holds.

The work of Brown
[
Br09
]
supports the existence of a Pfaffian generator in all the unshifted cases in Conjecture
12.8
. As new supporting evidence for the conjecture, we construct a Pfaffian generator in the case
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}(\sigma)
for odd
\ell
.
The remainder of the section is devoted to proving the following.

Theorem 12.9
.

The element

\mathpzc{Pf}:=\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}
as defined in (
11.5
) is a Pfaffian generator in
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}(\sigma)
, for
\ell
odd.

Before proving this theorem we state a corollary, and then prove some preliminary results.

Corollary 12.10
.

For
\ell
odd, the center
Z({\mathscr{Y}}_{2,\ell}^{+}(\sigma))
is freely generated by
z_{2},\dots,z_{2m-2},\mathpzc{Pf}
. Furthermore, these elements lift a complete collection of homogeneous generators of the Poisson center of the Slodowy slice, via the isomorphism of Corollary
11.11
.

Proof.

The argument of Theorem
12.6
works almost verbatim, after taking into account the well-known fact that the (total) degrees of homogeneous generators of
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{so}_{2m}]^{\operatorname{SO}_{2m}}
are
2,4,6,...,2m-2
, and
m
; cf.
[
Ja04
, §7.7]
.
∎

Remark 12.11
.

If a Pfaffian generator
\mathpzc{Pf}
exists in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
, then the center is freely generated by the elements
z_{2},\dots z_{2m-2},\mathpzc{Pf}
, by the same argument given above.

In light of the above remark, the following simpler variant of Conjecture
12.8
is slightly weaker but suffices for our application to finite
W
-algebras. As the formulation is less technical, it is conceivable that different techniques can be applicable to attack Conjecture
12.12
.

Conjecture 12.12
.

Consider
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{+}(\sigma)
with
N
even and
\ell
odd or
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{-}(\sigma)
with
\ell
even. The center of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{N,\ell}^{\pm}(\sigma)
is isomorphic to a polynomial algebra with generators of canonical degree
2,4,6,...,2m-2
, and
m
.

Lemma 12.13
.

The element
s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}\in{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}(0)
is central.

Proof.

The proof is by direct computation and by a case-by-case analysis.
We will examine the commutators
[s_{i,j}^{(r)},s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}]
.
Consider first the case
i=j=1
and note that by the symmetry relation (
2.13
) this forces
r
to be even. By the quaternary relation (
2.12
) we have

\displaystyle[

\displaystyle s_{1,1}^{(r)},s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{=}}\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-1-t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+t)}s_{1,2}^{(r-1-t)}\big)-\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{t}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-1-t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+t)}s_{2,1}^{(r-1-t)}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t)}s_{1,2}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{ii})}}{{=}}\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-1-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t)}s_{1,2}^{(r-1-2t)}\big)+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t+1)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t+1)}s_{1,2}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-1-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t)}s_{2,1}^{(r-1-2t)}\big)+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t+1)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t+1)}s_{2,1}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t)}s_{1,2}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{iii})}}{{=}}\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t+1)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t+1)}s_{1,2}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t+1)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t+1)}s_{2,1}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t)}s_{1,2}^{(r-2-2t)}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{iv})}}{{=}}2\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t+1)}\big)+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t)}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}(2s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t+1)}+s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{=}}0.

Let us break down where these equalities come from. The equality (i) is just given by the quaternary relation whereas the equality (ii) is given from splitting up the sum. For the equality (iii) note that due to the symmetry relation (
2.13
) we have that
s_{i,i}^{(2s+1)}=0
and by our assumptions we have that
r-1-2t
and
\ell+2t
are odd. This explains the vanishing of the first and third sums on the left-hand side of (iii). For the equality (iv) note that by (
11.6
) and the symmetry relation again we have that
s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t+1)}\in I_{\ell}
which gives the next two equalities. Finally for the last equality (*) note that
2s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t+1)}+s_{1,2}^{(\ell+2t)}\in I_{\ell}
by definition. The case where
i=j=2
works exactly the same way so we will omit it here.

Consider now the case where
i\neq j
and note that due to the symmetry relation we may assume
i=1,j=2
.
Then

\displaystyle[s_{1,2}^{(r)},s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}\Big(s_{1,2}^{(r-1-t)}s_{1,2}^{(\ell+t)}-s_{1,2}^{(\ell+t)}s_{1,2}^{(r-1-t)}\Big)

(12.9)

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}(-1)^{t}\Big(s_{1,1}^{(r-1-t)}s_{2,2}^{(\ell+t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+t)}s_{2,2}^{(r-1-t)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=0}^{\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\rfloor-1}\Big(s_{1,1}^{(r-2-2t)}s_{2,2}^{(\ell+2t)}-s_{1,1}^{(\ell+2t)}s_{2,2}^{(r-2-2t)}\Big)=\sum_{t=0}^{r-1}[s_{1,2}^{(r-1-t)},s_{1,2}^{(\ell+t)}]=0.

Again the first equality follows from the quaternary relation and the second equality follows as we have
s_{i,i}^{(2s+1)}=0
by the symmetry relation as well as
s_{i,i}^{(\ell+2t+1)}\in I_{\ell}
.
For the final equality we use an induction.
First note that by the quaternary relation we have

[s_{1,2}^{(1)},s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}]=s_{1,1}^{(\ell)}-s_{2,2}^{(\ell)}=0
by the symmetry relation. Now assume the statement
[s_{1,2}^{(r-1-t)},s_{1,2}^{(\ell+t)}]=0
has been proven for all
t<s
. Note that by (
11.6
) we may always replace
s_{1,2}^{(\ell+t)}
by a scalar multiple of
s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}
and then we can just repeat the argument from (
12.9
).
∎

Lemma 12.14
.

The elements
z_{2},\ldots,z_{2\ell-2},s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}
are algebraically independent.

Proof.

It is known from Proposition
12.5
that the elements
z_{2},\ldots,z_{2\ell-2},z_{2\ell}
are algebraically independent. Using the expansion formula for the Sklyanin determinant from
[
Mol07
, Thm. 2.7.2]

{\mathrm{sdet}}(S(u))=\sum_{\omega\in\mathfrak{S}_{2}}\operatorname{sgn}(\omega)\operatorname{sgn}(\omega^{\prime})s^{t}_{\omega(1),\omega^{\prime}(1)}(u-1)s_{\omega(2),\omega^{\prime}(2)}(-u),

where
\omega^{\prime}
is defined differently in
op. cit.
from (
2.8
),
we see that the top graded part of the coefficient of
u^{-2\ell}
in the canonical filtration is given by the top graded part of

\sum_{\omega\in\mathfrak{S}_{2}}\operatorname{sgn}(\omega)\operatorname{sgn}(\omega^{\prime})s_{\omega(\omega^{\prime})^{-1}(1),1}^{(\ell)}s_{\omega(\omega^{\prime})^{-1}(2),2}^{(\ell)},

where we have used the fact that
s_{i,j}^{(\ell)}=-s_{j,i}^{(\ell)}
in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}
(by the symmetry relation (
2.13
) and oddness of
\ell
) and the fact that
s_{i,j}^{(r)}=0
for
r>\ell
in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}
. Now by
[
Mol07
, Lemma 2.7.5]
the map
\mathfrak{S}_{2}\to\mathfrak{S}_{2}
given by
\omega\mapsto\omega(\omega^{\prime})^{-1}
is bijective so that we get that the top graded part of the coefficient of
u^{-2\ell}
of
{\mathrm{sdet}}(S(u))
is given by the top graded part of
\sum_{\omega\in\mathfrak{S}_{2}}\operatorname{sgn}(\omega)\operatorname{sgn}(\omega^{\prime})s_{\omega(1),1}^{(\ell)}s_{\omega(2),2}^{(\ell)}
. However in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}
this is simply given by
(s_{1,1}^{(\ell)}s_{2,2}^{(\ell)}-s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}s_{2,1}^{(\ell)})=(s_{1,2}^{(\ell)})^{2}
, see also
[
Mol07
, Ex. 2.7.3]
. For the last step, again we used the fact that in the associated graded we have
s_{i,j}^{(\ell)}=-s_{j,i}^{(\ell)}
. Furthermore it follows immediately by definition that the top graded part of
z_{2\ell}
in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}
is the same as the top graded part of the coefficient of
u^{-2\ell}
of
{\mathrm{sdet}}(S(u))
. In particular this gives that the top graded part of
s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}
is the square root of the top graded part of
z_{2\ell}
and hence the top graded parts of
z_{2},\ldots,z_{2\ell-2},s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}
are algebraically independent in
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}
and hence these elements are also algebraically independent in
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}
.
∎

We are now in a position to prove Theorem

12.9
.

Proof of Theorem
12.9
.

First note that the statement about the canonical degree is immediate by definition of
\widetilde{B}_{1;i,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}
.
The proof is by induction on
\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}
. Note that when
\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}=0
we have
\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}=-s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}
and because we have
s_{1,2}^{(\ell)}=-s_{2,1}^{(\ell)}
by the symmetry relation so that in this case the result is proven by Lemmas
12.13
and
12.14
.

Now assume the result has been proven for
\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}<k
and let
\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}=k
.
Recall the baby comultiplication from Theorem
9.2
. We have seen in (
11.24
) and in Theorem
11.8
that this induces an injective map
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell}(\sigma)\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell-2}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1})
where we have used that the minimal admissible shape is
(1,1)
if
\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}>0
and note that
\dot{\mathfrak{s}}_{1,2}=\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}-1
. We will write
x
for the generator of
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1})\cong\mathbb{C}[x]
.
Consider the image of
\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}
under the baby comultiplication.
Using the definition of
\Delta_{R}
we see that the image is given by
\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}-1;p_{1})}\otimes x+\dot{\widetilde{B}}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}\otimes 1
. It is clear that
\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}-1;p_{1})}\otimes x
is central by the induction hypothesis.

Now consider the bracket
[H_{1;1,1}^{(2)},\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}-1;p_{1})}]
. Using the explicit description (
11.5
) and relation (
4.2
) we see that

[H_{1;1,1}^{(2)},\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}-1;p_{1})}]=-\dot{\widetilde{B}}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}
but by assumption
\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}-1;p_{1})}
is central in
{\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell-2}(\dot{\sigma})
so that we get
\dot{\widetilde{B}}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}=0
. In particular, as
\Delta_{R}
is injective, this gives that
\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}
is central.

Furthermore, by the induction hypothesis, we know that
Z({\mathscr{Y}}^{+}_{2,\ell-2}(\dot{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1}))
is a polynomial ring generated by
z_{2}\otimes 1,\ldots,z_{2m-4}\otimes 1,\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2}-1;p_{1})}\otimes 1,1\otimes x
.
For algebraic independence we once again pass to the associated graded with respect to the canonical filtration. Here
z_{2i}
are just the top graded pieces of the coefficients of
u^{-2i}
in
{\mathrm{sdet}}(S(u))
. Using Proposition
12.3
, the fact that the top graded pieces of
\tilde{B}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(u+\frac{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}{2})\tilde{B}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(-u+\frac{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}{2})
are the same as the top graded pieces of
\tilde{B}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(u)\tilde{B}_{\tilde{q}_{1}}(-u)
and thus have no even coefficients. By the induction hypothesis one explicitly verifies that the images under
\operatorname{gr}^{\prime}\Delta_{R}
of the top graded pieces of the elements
z_{2},\ldots,z_{2m-2},\widetilde{B}_{1;1,1}^{(\mathfrak{s}_{1,2};p_{1})}
are algebraically independent.
∎

13.
Equivariant quantizations and finite
W
-algebras

In this section, we recall finite
W
-algebras as quantization of Slodowy slices. We achieve our main goal by showing that a truncated shifted twisted Yangian is isomorphic to a certain finite
W
-algebra of type
\sf B\sf C\sf D
under some suitable assumptions; see Theorem
. The proof uses geometric techniques related to conic symplectic singularities. In particular we use the existence of universal equivariant quantizations of nilpotent Slodowy varieties, which we also recall.

13.1.
Slodowy slices and finite
W
-algebras

Throughout this section all graded vector spaces are
\mathbb{N}
-graded. We will often use the well-known correspondence between connected
\mathbb{N}
-graded commutative algebras and affine schemes with contracting
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action.

Let
G
be a connected complex reductive group, and
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}(G)
. A chosen non-degenerate
G
-invariant bilinear form on
\mathfrak{g}
induces a
G
-module isomorphism
\kappa\colon\mathfrak{g}\cong\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. The nilpotent cone
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{g}^{*})
is the image under
\kappa
of the set of nilpotent elements in
\mathfrak{g}
. It can also be defined as the central fibre of the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant coadjoint quotient map
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}/\!/G
, which is a reduced scheme (see
[
Pr02
, §5]
and the references therein).

Let
e\in\mathfrak{g}
be nilpotent, and write
\mathbb{O}
for the adjoint orbit of
e
. Let
(e,h,f)
be an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple for
e
. We denote
\chi:=\kappa(e)\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. The Slodowy slice is the affine space
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}:=\chi+\kappa(\mathfrak{g}^{f})\subseteq\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, which is transversal to the
G
-orbits (i.e. the symplectic leaves) of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. It is equipped with a Poisson structure
[
GG02
]
, which we now explain.

The semisimple operator
\operatorname{ad}(h)
induces the Dynkin grading
\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus\mathfrak{g}(i)
where
\mathfrak{g}(i)=\{x\in\mathfrak{g}\mid[h,x]=ix\}.
Then
S(\mathfrak{g})=\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]
is equipped with the Kazhdan grading, which places
\mathfrak{g}(i)\subseteq S(\mathfrak{g})
in degree
i+2
. We note that the Kazhdan graded pieces of
S(\mathfrak{g})
are all infinite dimensional. The form
\kappa
places
\mathfrak{g}(-1)
and
\mathfrak{g}(1)
in non-degenerate pairing, and so we may choose a Lagrangian subspace
\mathfrak{l}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}(-1)
with respect to the form
\langle x,y\rangle:=\chi([x,y])
. Define
\mathfrak{m}:=\mathfrak{l}\oplus\bigoplus_{i<-1}\mathfrak{g}(i)
and let
M\subseteq G
be the unipotent group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{m}
.

Considering the moment map
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\to\mathfrak{m}^{*}
for the coadjoint action of
M
. The fiber over
\chi
is
\chi+(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}
, with
M
acting freely, and
\big(\chi+(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}\big)/\!/M\cong\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
. Thus,
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
acquires a Poisson structure by Hamiltonian reduction.

The Kazhdan grading on
S(\mathfrak{g})
descends to a non-negative connected grading on
\mathbb{C}[\chi+(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}/\!/M]
, which equips
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
with a contracting
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action. We note that the Poisson bracket on
\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
has degree
-2
with respect to the grading

\displaystyle\{\cdot\,,\,\cdot\}:\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]_{i}\wedge\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]_{j}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]_{i+j-2}.

The coadjoint quotient map
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}/G
is
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-equivariant, faithfully flat and admits reduced, irreducible, normal fibres
[
Pr02
, §5]
. The central fibre of the morphism
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\to\mathfrak{g}^{*}/\!/G
is denoted
\mathcal{N}_{\chi}
and called the
nilpotent Slodowy variety
. It will play a central role in the rest of the paper. By our previous remarks,
\mathcal{N}_{\chi}
coincides with the reduced scheme
\mathcal{N}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
.

We note that the Poisson varieties
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\chi}
only depend on the orbit
\operatorname{Ad}(G)e
up to isomorphism. In particular it is independent of the choice of Lagrangian
\mathfrak{l}
.

The finite
W
-algebra
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)
is constructed from the enveloping algebra
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g})
by quantum Hamiltonian reduction. In more detail, the Kazhdan grading on
S(\mathfrak{g})
lifts to a Kazhdan filtration on
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g})
by placing
\mathfrak{g}(i)\subseteq\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g})
in degree
i+2
. The precise quantum analogue of
\mathbb{C}[\chi+(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}]
is

\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e):=(\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g})/\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g})\{x-\chi(x)\mid x\in\mathfrak{m}\})^{M}.

This algebra is equipped with a Kazhdan filtered algebra structure inherited from
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g})
, and it was proven in
[
GG02
, Thm. 4.1]
that

(13.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)=\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}].

Furthermore the footnote to
[
PrJI
, Question 5.1]
explains that

(13.2)

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}Z\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g},e)=Z\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}],

where
Z
indicates the center of an algebra, or Poisson algebra.

13.2.
Equivariant Poisson deformations of symplectic singularities

A symplectic singularity is a normal algebraic variety
X
with a closed, nondegenerate 2-form
\omega
on the smooth locus
X^{{\mathrm{reg}}}
, and a resolution of singularities
\rho:Y\to X
such that the pullback
\rho^{*}\omega
extends
to a closed (possibly degenerate) regular 2-form
[
Be00
]
. If the 2-form extends to a regular symplectic form on
Y
then
\rho
is known as a
symplectic resolution
.

If a symplectic singularity
X
is irreducible, affine and carries a contracting
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-action such that
\omega
is homogeneous of weight
w>0
then we say that
X
is a
conic symplectic singularity
.

The closed 2-form on
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
equips
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
with a Poisson bivector which, by normality, extends to a Poisson bracket on
\mathbb{C}[X]
. The conical structure equips
\mathbb{C}[X]
with a connected, non-negative
\mathbb{Z}
-grading
\mathbb{C}[X]=\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}\mathbb{C}[X]_{i}
satisfying
\mathbb{C}[X]_{0}=\mathbb{C}
.

The homogeneity of the 2-form on
X^{\mathrm{reg}}
ensures that the Poisson bracket is graded in degree
-w
:

(13.3)

\displaystyle\{\cdot,\cdot\}:\mathbb{C}[X]_{i}\wedge\mathbb{C}[X]_{j}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}[X]_{i+j-w}.

Example 13.1
.

It follows from
[
Gi09
]
that
\mathcal{N}_{\chi}
is a conic symplectic singularity. A symplectic resolution of
\mathcal{N}_{\chi}
may be constructed by applying Hamiltonian reduction to the Springer resolution of
\mathcal{N}
(Prop. 2.1.2 of
op. cit
).

Let
X
be a conic symplectic singularity and
\mathbb{C}[X]
together with its Poisson structure. A flat graded Poisson deformation is a triple
(A,B,i)
where:

•

B
is a non-negatively graded commutative algebra;

•

A
is a graded Poisson algebra admitting a flat homomorphism
B\to A
with image lying in the Poisson center;

•

i:A\otimes_{B}\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}[X]
is a graded Poisson isomorphism, where
\mathbb{C}
denotes the unique graded simple
B
-module.

The map
i
should be referred to as the
tethering isomorphism
and the algebra
B
will be referred to as the
base
of the deformation. One can show that, in fact, the image of
B
in
A
is equal to the Poisson center.

An isomorphisms of flat graded Poisson deformations over a base
B
is a homomorphism of Poisson algebras which respects the tethering isomorphisms, see
[
ACET23
, §2.3]
for more detail.

Write
GrAlg
for the category of graded commutative algebras. The functor of flat graded Poisson deformations of
X

(13.4)

\displaystyle\operatorname{PD}_{X}:\textbf{GrAlg}\longrightarrow\textbf{Set}

sends a graded commutative algebra
B
to the set of isomorphism classes of flat graded Poisson deformations with base
B
. To a homomorphism of graded commutative algebras
B_{1}\to B_{2}
we associate the morphism map of sets which sends the isomorphism class of
(A,B_{1},i)
to the isomorphism class of
(A\otimes_{B_{1}}B_{2},B_{2},i)
.

If
\Gamma
is a group then a
\Gamma
-algebra is an algebra
A
equipped with a homomorphism
\Gamma\to\mathrm{Aut}(A)
, and
\Gamma
-homomorphisms of such algebras are defined similarly.

Now let
\Gamma
be a reductive group acting by graded Poisson automorphisms on
\mathbb{C}[X]
. We say that
(A,B,i)
is a Poisson
\Gamma
-deformation if it is equipped with a graded
\Gamma
-action such that the induced map
A\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{C}[X]
is
\Gamma
-equivariant, and such that the action on the base is trivial. Isomorphisms of Poisson
\Gamma
-deformations are the isomorphisms respecting the
\Gamma
-action, and the functor of
\Gamma
-equivariant Poisson deformations

(13.5)

\displaystyle\operatorname{PD}_{X,\Gamma}:\textbf{GrAlg}\longrightarrow\textbf{Set}

associates to each graded algebra
B
the set of isomorphism classes of flat graded Poisson
\Gamma
-deformations with base
B
.

Example 13.2
.

The Poisson center
Z\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]
coincides with the ring of invariants
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]^{G}
, and a theorem of Kostant states that there exists a natural isomorphism
i_{0}:\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]\otimes_{Z\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]}\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{N}]
. In fact
(\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}],Z\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}],i_{0})
is a flat graded Poisson deformation of
\mathcal{N}
.

Similarly if
e\in\mathfrak{g}
is a nilpotent element then restriction
\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]^{G}\to\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
provides an isomorphism to the Poisson center of functions on the Slodowy slice (see
[
ACET23
, Lem. 3.3]
for references) and, by a result of Premet
[
Pr02
, Thm. 5.2]
, there is a natural Poisson isomorphism
i_{\chi}:\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]\otimes_{Z\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]}\mathbb{C}\to\mathcal{N}_{\chi}
. In this case
(\mathbb{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}],Z\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}],i_{\chi})
is a flat graded Poisson deformation of the central fiber
\mathcal{N}_{\chi}
.

13.3.
The functor of equivariant quantizations

Recall that the Poisson bracket on
\mathbb{C}[X]
lies in some fixed degree
w>0
, (
13.3
).

A filtered quantization (of a flat graded Poisson deformation) of
\mathbb{C}[X]
is a triple
(\mathcal{A},B,i)
with:

•

\mathcal{A}
a non-negatively filtered almost commutative algebra
\mathcal{A}=\bigcup_{j\geqslant 0}\mathcal{A}_{j}
, such that
[\mathcal{A}_{j_{1}},\mathcal{A}_{j_{2}}]\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{j_{1}+j_{2}-w}
. It follows that
\operatorname{gr}\mathcal{A}
admits a Poisson bracket is graded in degree
-w
;

•

B
is a split filtered commutative algebra, i.e.
\operatorname{gr}B\cong B
, admitting a map
B\to\mathcal{A}
to the center of
\mathcal{A}
. Thus
B\to\operatorname{gr}\mathcal{A}
maps to the Poisson center;

•

(\operatorname{gr}\mathcal{A},B,i)
is a flat graded Poisson deformation of
\mathbb{C}[X]
.

We call
B
the
base
of the quantization. It is not hard to see that the map
B\to\mathcal{A}
gives an isomorphism to the center.

Write
SFAlg
for the category of split filtered commutative algebras, i.e. filtered algebras satisfying
\operatorname{gr}B\cong B
. The functor of filtered quantizations of
X

(13.6)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Q}_{X}:\textbf{SFAlg}\longrightarrow\textbf{Set}

sends a split filtered graded commutative algebra
B
to the set of isomorphism classes of filtered quantizations of
\mathbb{C}[X]
. The effect on morphisms is identical to
\operatorname{PD}_{X}
, and isomorphisms of filtered quantizations are defined similarly.

Let
\Gamma
be a reductive group acting by graded Poisson automorphisms on
\mathbb{C}[X]
. We say that
(\mathcal{A},B,i)
is a
\Gamma
-quantization if it is equipped with a
\Gamma
-action such that
(\mathcal{A},B,i)
is a Poisson
\Gamma
-deformation of
\mathbb{C}[X]
, and the
\Gamma
-action on
B
is trivial.

Isomorphisms of
\Gamma
-quantizations are the isomorphisms respecting the
\Gamma
-action, and the functor of
\Gamma
-equivariant filtered quantizations

(13.7)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Q}_{X,\Gamma}:\textbf{SFAlg}\longrightarrow\textbf{Set}

associates to each split filtered graded algebra
B
the set of isomorphism classes of Poisson
\Gamma
-deformations with base
B
.

Example 13.3
.

We offer two extremal examples of elements of
\operatorname{Q}_{\mathcal{N},\Gamma}(Z(\mathfrak{g}))
when
\Gamma
is the trivial group. The enveloping algebra gives rise to an element
(\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}),Z(\mathfrak{g}),i)
. On the other hand, one can take
\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g})/(\ker\chi_{0})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}Z(\mathfrak{g})
where
\chi_{0}:Z(\mathfrak{g})\to\mathbb{C}
is the character of the trivial representation. The former is the largest quantization of
\mathcal{N}
, in a sense which we make precise in the following section. The latter is a quantization of the trivial deformation of
\mathcal{N}
.

13.4.
Universal elements of the deformation and quantization functors

We recap the basics of universal elements of functors, see
[
Ma71
, III.1]
for more detail. Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a category. A functor
\mathcal{F}:\mathbf{C}\to\mathbf{Set}
is representable over a base
B
if there exists a natural equivalence
\mathrm{Hom}_{\textbf{C}}(B,\cdot)\to\mathcal{F}
, and a representation of
\mathcal{F}
is a choice of natural equivalence, and
B
may be called the base of the representation.

A
universal element
of
\mathcal{F}
is a pair
(B,b)
with
b\in\mathcal{F}(B)
such that for every other such pair
(C,c)
there exists a unique
\phi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbf{C}}(B,C)
such that
\mathcal{F}(\phi)(b)=c
. By Yoneda’s lemma, every representable functor admits a universal element: if
\mathcal{F}
is represented by
B
then
b\in\mathcal{F}(\operatorname{Id}_{B})(B)
supplies a universal element. If the base
B
is clear, then we often just write
b
for the universal element. Note that the choice of universal element is not unique. Every automorphism
\phi\in\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathbf{C}}(B)
gives rise to a universal element, and so the set of universal elements of
\mathcal{F}
is an
\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathbf{C}}(B)
-torsor.

Namikawa has shown that for a conic symplectic singularity
X
the functor of (formal) Poisson deformations is prorepresentable and unobstructed
[
Na11
]
, and in the sequel
[
Na10
]
he used the conic structure to show that the functor
\operatorname{PD}_{X}
is representable (see
[
ACET23
]
for a algebraic survey of these results). If
B_{X}\in\textbf{GrAlg}
represents
\operatorname{PD}_{X}
then an element of
\operatorname{PD}_{X}(B_{X})
is called a
universal Poisson deformation of
\mathbb{C}[X]
(we drop the words flat graded for brevity), and the group of graded automorphisms of
B_{X}
acts simply transitively on
\operatorname{PD}_{X}(B_{X})
(this algebra was denoted
B_{u}
in
[
ACET23
]
).

Losev proved in
[
Lo22
, Prop. 3.5]
that Namikawa’s universal flat graded Poisson deformation of
\mathbb{C}[X]
admits a filtered quantization which satisfies a certain universal property. In
[
ACET23
, §2.7&2.8]
this universal property was reformulated in terms of the functor
\operatorname{Q}_{X}
, and upgraded to the
\Gamma
-equivariant setting.

Theorem 13.4
.

Let
X
be a conic symplectic singularity and
\Gamma
be a reductive group of Poisson
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-automorphisms of
X
.

(1)

If
\operatorname{PD}_{X}
is represented over
B_{X}\in\textnormal{\bf{GrAlg}}
then
\operatorname{PD}_{X,\Gamma}
is represented by the coinvariant algebra

B_{X,\Gamma}:=B_{X}/(b-\gamma\cdot b\mid\gamma\in\Gamma,\ b\in B_{X}).

The universal element of
\operatorname{PD}_{X,\Gamma}
is called the universal
\Gamma
-deformation.

(2)

Viewing
B_{X}
as a split filtered algebra,
\operatorname{Q}_{X}
is represented over the base
B_{X}
and
\operatorname{Q}_{X,\Gamma}
is represented over
B_{X,\Gamma}
. The universal element of
\operatorname{Q}_{X,\Gamma}
is called the universal
\Gamma
-quantization.

(3)

If we pick a representation

\zeta:\mathrm{Hom}_{\textnormal{{{GrAlg}}}}(B_{X,\Gamma},\cdot)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\operatorname{PD}_{X,\Gamma},

then we may choose a representation

\eta:\mathrm{Hom}_{\textnormal{{{SFAlg}}}}(B_{X,\Gamma},\cdot)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Q}_{X,\Gamma}
so that the following diagram commutes:
